## Imposter syndrome

**Date:** 2023-08-21  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

This week I was reached out to by a training and development manager of a CEO who liked my social media and wanted to vet me to be his coach. When I was being vetted by the development manager he said that he thought I was a good fit for his CEO and to send him over my contract so the company can look it over with legal.

He’s emailed me again this week to ask if he can chat with me and I feel nervous to respond.

This is a first for me.

I just hit my 2 yr in business, I am still not full with clients, I’ve only had 3 consults this whole year and now I’m being vetted! I have no idea what I’m doing and I’m feeling so intimidated. I’ve never not met a client before working with them and is this a thing that companies do?

I want to feel confident and like I know what I’m doing but the training manager was even shocked at my price and asked “do you know how much coaches charge?” Of course I’ve heard of the rates master coaches charge, but I am still learning how to get consults, stay out of the pool and sell a 6k year package!

This all feels unreal and I guess I’m feeling out of my league. Obviously my marketing was good enough to attract this CEO so that says something. But I guess I don’t feel like a professional coach. I feel like they are going to find me out. Major imposter syndrome going on, please help!

**ANSWER:**

You are wanting to feel confident so let’s build up your confidence by focusing on what you do know, what you are good at and how you can help.

What is this person looking to be coached on? What results do they want?

How can you help them with those results?

How do you feel when you ask these questions? Can you find how you DO know how to help?

## A client with Imposter Syndrome and Lack of Belief

**Date:** 2023-06-23  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

At the core of it all, my client is struggling with belief and “Imposter Syndrome”. We broke it down to her wanting to feel confident and that propels her into action (in her new business). She has tons of evidence to show herself that she’s already having success in her new business and lifestyle… but thoughts of imposter syndrome continue to come up for her. Should I be coaching her to dismantle a limiting belief that is keeping her in imposter syndrome? Or should I be coaching her to move forward in SPITE of not having confidence? And then she will build confidence after moving forward and seeing all that she’s capable? But I kind of feel like that’s what she’s done so far… and she might keep getting stuck because there is something deep rooted that still keeps her feeling like she doesn’t knows what she’s doing, etc… aka: belief in herself and all that she’s accomplished. I’m just not sure what the best next steps are here. Building self-worth, belief, and dismantling a limiting belief that is keeping her stuck? In that case… how do you get someone to simply believe in themself when they have all the evidence but still don’t? Or teaching her to move forward through those doubts?

**ANSWER:**

Listen to the podcast called #39: Limiting Money beliefs.

It talks about working with one thought at a time instead of thinking that you need to dismantle a whole belief system – which can feel overwhelming.

She sometimes has the thought “I don’t know what I’m doing.”  Notice that thought doesn’t happen all the time. It just happens sometimes.  What happens when she thinks that? Look at a specific situation.

C what’s the situation/circumstance?

T I don’t know what I’m doing.

F How does she feel when she thinks that?

A How does she show up? What does she do or not do?

R What is the result of that?

Then – maybe she doesn’t know what she is doing. Is that a problem? How does she respond to herself when she notices that she doesn’t know what she is doing?

Confidence also is a feeling in a moment that comes from a thought. I bet now she does sometimes feel confident. Just like she sometimes thinks she doesn’t know what she is doing.

What is she thinking when she does feel confident? Can she be confident even when she doesn’t know what she is doing? How might that be possible?

## Imposter after coaching

**Date:** 2023-05-25  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

I just had a free coaching sessions and learnt that the person was very well certified in coaching and therapy. I am not certified. However, I could see how I could help her and that she’s suffering heavily from the Expert Imposter syndrome.

But after I realised how well certified she is my own Imposter came up were I was telling myself that I don’t know everything and she’s going to judge me and not buy from me because I don’t know as much as she does etc.

Now I even feel like I don’t want her to reach out to me again because I’m not certified, and I know this is not helpful because I can help her and I’m naturally good at coaching and intuitive.

I don’t want this to stop me from showing up

**ANSWER:**

Knowledge, and certifications – on their own – are meaningless.

Wisdom is the ability to use the knowledge you have to improve your life.

It’s clear this client isn’t able to help herself with all the knowledge she has – which is why she’s seeking your help – because she sees within you the ability to help her.

AND – there’s never a scenario as a coach where you’re supposed to know everything. Ever.

In fact, knowing everything will get in the way of you profoundly connecting with and helping your clients – because you’ll think you know what is best rather than tapping into your deep curiosity to help them uncover what is best for them.

What do you think?

How might it feel for you to think, “
I’m naturally good at coaching and intuitive – 
and this serves my clients to see their blind spots in profoundly helpful ways.” ?

## Strong Imposter Syndrome (4) /Working Through Negative Emotions (4)

**Date:** 2023-04-27  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

I am worried that people will not take me seriously, and the emotion that comes to me is embarrassment. Where I am now is not where I set out to be, but I know that there is never a timeframe, and the things that brought me to the place I’m at today are things that I want to inspire people from, and not be ashamed of.

I am worried that people will make fun of me, but then I start talking to myself: “I don’t care what people think – this is for those people that need me because I’m a brilliant coach for exactly where I am. Anyone who finds their way to me needs exactly what I have; nothing more, nothing less. What people think of me is none of my business, because no matter what it doesn’t define me.”

I think I am worried about the people that knew me before the transformation, they are looking for the People-Pleasing Alex, the girl that loves attention, the girl that always cares what others think. And I’ve been ghosting my business in fear of projecting that, but I’ve been projecting that subconsciously to myself, and THAT is what makes me steer clear of meeting people, telling them I’m a Life Coach, and making offers to help. I want to build my belief triad back up with intangibles.

The things that are uncomfortable to me are Social Media, posting the content, and writing copy – because I’m still learning how to do I, and I worry about how people will see me. I think from what I heard in one of Stacey’s podcasts that Sara Fisk said something about talking out the words and THEN writing them – which I think will help me because I’m not very confident in my writing, but I’m VERY confident in my speaking.

I think I don’t look good because of how inconsistent my posting is… I’m judging myself for it and thinking others are judging me back…

**ANSWER:**

You’ve uncovered such great thoughts here to explore, coach.

It seems part of you doesn’t care about what others think, and part of you does. So let’s explore the part of you that does, together.

Why is it a problem for you, that people that knew you from before would expect you to act in people-pleasing ways?

What do you imagine they’d think of you, seeing you show up differently now?

You love attention – this is a universally human trait. You don’t 
always
 love attention, much like you don’t always love sleep, or food, or anything else. Humans are designed with the fundamental need to be seen and heard – it’s an important part of our lives. Why is it a problem for you if people see you and think you love attention?

You shared you think people are judging you because you’re showing up inconsistently: here’s the best news – they’re not actually thinking about how frequently you post and how much you should post in order to consider yourself consistent.

Instead, they’re thinking about themselves…

Their problems, their ways to escape, and the things in their lives. How often you show up, and comparing it to an ideal, isn’t something they’re doing – at all. It’s not interesting enough for them to notice, because their own issues are front and center in their attention.

BUT – for the sake of exploration, let’s imagine someone does think you’re not posting enough. Why would that be a problem for you?

## Strong Imposter Syndrome (3) /Working Through Negative Emotions (3)

**Date:** 2023-04-24  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

I will be meditating on that thought – “I’m a brilliant coach for exactly where I am. Anyone who finds their way to me needs exactly what I have; nothing more, nothing less.” – because it feels distant, but my rational thinking tells me “Yeah, this IS true.”

“I know this is good stuff, but I’m worried about how people will see me.”

“This isn’t comfortable.”

“I need to look good.”

These are thoughts that come to me by default – and I know it. They just feel real. And then when I realize I’m thinking it like that, I get frustrated with myself because I know what my brain is defaulting to and I’m not stopping it. I’m beating myself up for it, maybe?

**ANSWER:**

It sounds like it, coach.

We oftentimes don’t even realize we’re beating ourselves up in the moment, so it’s brilliant of you to catch it.

Let’s take a closer look at those thoughts you’ve shared.

What are your specific worries to how people will see you? And why?

What specifically isn’t comfortable? And why?

Why do you think you don’t look good (or might not to others?)

## Strong Imposter Syndrome (2) /Working Through Negative Emotions (2)

**Date:** 2023-04-20  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

What will you make it mean when you sign your 5 clients within 30 days? And why?

Why do you want it to work so bad?

______________________________________________

I think that I’ll make it mean that I am actually coaching, I’m running a business and I know what I’m doing in the business. It’ll mean that I’m not a baby coach and that I can do this – but it’s all, like, validation from the result. And I knoooowwww I need to be living and thinking it already to be true in order for it to be true…

I want it to work because I want my business to be my full source of income – it would be so so fulfilling to live my life by serving others and helping them better themselves and their lives. But I also think that my desire to make more money is coming out and I don’t want it to. Like the want to make money – I think I’m coming off as money hungry.

————————-

What happens when your schedule time to do the scary things comes up? Can you tell yourself “I’m scared and I’m going to do it anyway”?

How does it feel when you avoid the work?

How does it feel when you are scared but you do it anyway?

______________________________________________

What happens when the scheduled time comes up is this thought that goes “I’m not ready.” for these random reasons, that bug in my head tells me I’m not good enough, I’m so young, I don’t want to be seen in some sort of weird way – and I KNOW all of those thoughts are what hold me back, and NONE of them are true.

I can definitely tell myself that I’m scared and I’m going to do this anyway, but I am worried that my mind will not be in the best place for creating value for my clients and it won’t come out in that way that I want to, so that people can really see I’m here to serve.

How it feels… When I’m not doing it I feel guilty, and like I’m a failure, and then when I am working while I’m scared I feel like I’m pushing my work… As if I’m in my head about the whole thing. And feeling conflicted? Maybe not good enough because I’m pushing?

**ANSWER:**

Of course, coach.

It makes sense that you’re hungry to sign those 5 clients in 30 days when you’re making it mean something so important to yourself – and that if you don’t reach the goal you’ll make it mean something so heavy and defeating for yourself (that you’re a baby coach and not running a biz etc.)

Anytime you tell yourself you’re a baby coach, you’re diminishing the incredible impact you can make in someone’s life. You’re reducing the value you offer to something infantile – when it’s far from that; it’s life-changing.

How might it feel for you to think, “I’m a brilliant coach for exactly where I am. Anyone who finds their way to me needs exactly what I have; nothing more, nothing less.” ?

Wanting to make money is never a problem (and we’re here to help you do that.) Focusing on money 
over
 serving the needs of clients is when things get unhelpful. What is preventing you from feeling fulfilled 
now
 – recognizing that every moment you improve your offers and sales skills that you are serving people and making it easier for them to better themselves and their lives?

About working – when you work you feel scared and when you don’t work you feel guilty. What helps you more? Working when you are scared? Or not working and feeling guilty? What is the best of those two not so great options?

Then – it’s possible to shift how we are feeling based on how we are thinking about something. Being scared or feeling guilty doesn’t just happen to you – it’s because of the thoughts you are thinking. What are those thoughts? Do you really believe those thoughts or are they just thoughts your mind offers by default?

## self-concept and imposter – 3

**Date:** 2023-02-09  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

Let’s think about confidence as a feeling that comes from a thought.

What thoughts create you not feeling confident?

– I think it’s the fact that my coaching business isn’t brining in a ton of clients at the moment and in my mind I am not there yet. It almost feels like I have to get there to feel confident and I know that’s not true. I get confident by taking action and believing I am confident now.

-When it comes to posting I like the thought you suggested of “My ideal clients will find this useful”

What other thoughts have you feeling confident?

C posting on bloating on social media (put in something you want to post about)

T I don’t want to be wrong.

F worry

A I edit a lot. I don’t say what I want to say. I look at what other people are saying.

R I am not wrong because i posted something so basic that no one is interested

C posting on cycle on social media (same post as above)

T – this information will make her feel understood

F confident

A – share my experience, speak clearly, get to the bottom of the issue

R – this post lands with my client

**ANSWER:**

Great job doing these two models.

That worry you might be wrong keeps you from posting at all.

What if it’s okay if you are wrong? Is that okay? You are posting the best information you have right now (which is all anyone is every doing.) And I bet that information is so helpful for your ideal clients.

You might also ask this if you get stuck in that worry:

What do I know right now about this topic? What can I share right now?

You will absolutely always be learning and growing. And what you share today you might share in a different way a year from now, but HOW you’ll get to where you are a year from now is by sharing today. And today what you know is helpful for someone.

## self-concept and imposter – 2

**Date:** 2023-02-07  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

For each of those things, I want you to ask: Could I believe this about me too?

Can you find how those things are true about you? They might not be true 100% of the time, but can you see where they are true?

– The words that she wrote are:

Supportive – I believe this 10/10, I am really good at supporting people

Knowledgeable – I know a lot and I can easily identify clients problem, but I am more at 7/10 because I don’t know everything. I know its not realistic to know everything and I know I have the ability to always find the answer, one client said I completely changed her life when she came to me with extreme gut issues about to go under surgery, and we reversed all of it and her doctor couldn’t believe it. I didn’t know if I could fix it but I knew where to look and I learned about it. I need more belief here.

Unfazed by things – I can believe this 10/10

Cool, calm, collected – yes, with my clients I am that – 10/10, maybe not always in life and perhaps that’s where I need to fill the gap.

Change people’s lives – yes 10/10 on this.

I want to believe I am confident and I am generally confident person and I want to be 100% confident in my business. Especially when it comes to randomly share my knowledge on social. I find I edit myself a lot when I share, I don’t have 100% trust and I look at what other people are saying about particular subject to make sure I am not “wrong”.

I think the fact that I don’t feel 100% knowing everything perhaps it affects my confidence.

I am confident when I am with a client 1:1, but I lose that when I speak to many on social and don’t trust myself as much there.

Id love some coaching on that.

**ANSWER:**

Let’s think about confidence as a feeling that comes from a thought.

What thoughts create you not feeling confident?

Confidence doesn’t mean that we always 100% know we are right. We don’t have to know something 100% to feel confident. Your confidence with helping that client came from the thought “I know where to look and I can figure this out.” For posting it might be: “My ideal clients will find this useful.”

What other thoughts have you feeling confident?

C posting ______ on social media (put in something you want to post about)

T I don’t want to be wrong.

F How does that feel?

A I edit a lot. I don’t say what I want to say. I look at what other people are saying.

R What result does that create?

C posting ______ on social media (same post as above)

T

F confident

A

R

## Imposter Syndrome : Pt. 2 (response to coaching)

**Date:** 2022-11-16  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

AAC Replied: Anytime we feel primarily uncertain about our ability to serve a certain group of people, it prevents us from showing up fully and passionately, and making compelling offers – because our uncertainty can be felt by others.

Why have you chosen a group of people to serve when you don’t feel certain of your ability to help them create their desired result?

Me: Wow. Such a great question. I am feeling uncertain about serving any group of people right now b/c I don’t have much proof or success to lean on – and I know this is a limiting thought. I want to go on the belief that if I can figure it out, I can show others to do the same. I want to lead the way in solving a problem/offering a solution to something that I’m going through myself (and I know others also struggle with) and maybe it’s ok that I’m only a few steps ahead…

You don’t have to have lots of experience and proof of coaching abilities to believe in your ability to figure it out, and help people.

How do you feel when you think of those offerings?

What do you believe about your ability to grow from each session by evaluating and tweaking your actions for next time?

**Date:** 2022-08-08

Hello!! Thank you for the coaching last week on my AAC submission! I was told to report back with my answers for some more coaching:

What do your finances have to do with your ability to help other people with theirs?

– The thought that came up was “because I can’t help myself, I’m in no place to try and help someone else.” I know this thought isn’t true because I CAN help myself by doing exactly what I help my clients do. My ability to help other people isn’t affected by my own personal finances. This is where I get to be a product of my product and a breakthrough I can speak on once through it.

Let’s take you and your personal life out of it. What do you believe about your ability to help people with their finances?

– I believe I have the ability to help other people with their finances. I’ve helped women pay off thousands and thousands of dollars of debt and that wasn’t a result of how my own personal bank account looked.

What might be different about how you’re showing up if you shifted your focus to the people who need you?

– I would show up way more. I would show up leaving my own drama at the door. I would show up in full service to them instead of worrying about being “found out”. I would show up without the need for validation. I’d stop worrying about what everyone might be thinking. I’d post/show up more without worrying about it being perfect.

What would be the result of that?

– The result of that would be helping people, which is always the goal. I would also hope that the result of that would translate to money being made in my business as well?

Awesome job working through this and gaining that awareness, Coach!

When we’re suffering, it’s usually because we’re focused on ourselves instead of focusing on others.

The way we make money is through serving others so if we focus on doing that, then we can help others AND help ourselves.

So what do you want to believe when it comes to your business that will keep you in service to your clients?

How does that feel?

**Date:** 2022-08-05

**QUESTION:**

I’m a money coach and am finding myself in a VERY rough spot financially. I help my clients save, budget, pay off debt, and change their mindsets surrounding money. While I *know* how to do all of these things and feel very qualified to coach on them, my current circumstances have me thinking that I’m a huge fraud. I don’t know how to show up authentically to make offers when I am feeling so unqualified in the present to coach on money. Here are all my thoughts:

– I need to make money now.

– Making money now will solve my problem of bringing in less than my expenses

– IDK how to show up authentically to make offers

– I shouldn’t be coaching on this

– I would never hire a money coach if her finances looked like mine do now

– I’m a fraud

– Maybe I should stop coaching and get a job

Unintentional Models on a few of these thoughts:

C – Current money situation – Not making enough to meet expenses. X amount in credit card debt. Using credit cards to float my monthly expenses. No new clients since March.

T – I need to make money now

F – Nauseous

A – Spin in indecision. Dwell in confusion on how to do so. Spiral in other thoughts from above. Do busy work in my business but not making any offers.

R – I don’t make any money

C – Current money situation

T – IDK how to show up authentically to make offers

F – confused

A – I don’t make offers on social media. I consume more content than I create. I don’t create new content.

R – I don’t show up authentically and don’t make any offers

C – Current money situation

T – I shouldn’t be coaching on this

F – unqualified

A – Hide and not make any offers. Wish I had more clients but also wish I don’t get anymore because I shouldn’t be coaching them.

R – I’m not coaching any new clients (I have clients inside my group membership who I still coach weekly)

C – Current Money Situation

T – I would never hire a money coach if I knew her finances looked like mine

F – Deceitful

A – Hide. Don’t make any offers. Consume social media content. Don’t create content.

R – No one hires me as their money coach

I can see that the results I’m creating are obviously not what I want and my thoughts are preventing me from making more money. I don’t know how to move past these thoughts when they feel so real/ truthful. Any advice on how I can move forward and get past these limiting thoughts? Thank you!!

**ANSWER:**

First, it’s impossible to come up with solutions when you’re in such doubt and judgment of yourself. So let’s take a look.

You shared, “I would never hire a money coach if her finances looked like mine do now”

What do your finances have to do with your ability to help other people with theirs?

Let’s take you and your personal life out of it. What do you believe about your ability to help people with their finances?

If you truly believe in your ability to help others, then making offers to help them is as authentic as it gets.

You’re making this about you instead of about serving your people.

What might be different about how you’re showing up if you shifted your focus to the people who need you?

What would be the result of that?

Bring your answers back here for coaching.

## How do you overcome imposter syndrome – Part 3

**Date:** 2022-07-27  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

My answers below:

“Even though I have a certification, that I need an even better one / more training, b/c mine wasn’t good enough.”

1. How so? What would an “even better one” look like? What skills would you learn that you don’t feel you have right now?

>> This thought stemmed from the fact that my program is not as well known as other programs, and that I have learned extra tools outside my program since then that have augmented my skills. Now that I think about it, it’s probably more a ambiguous fear than anything specific I am missing. Helpful to think through! I’m always curious about other coaching models too but that’s easy enough to research & compare.

“That I haven’t put in as much time as other coaches into training, growing, etc. since I do this part-time.”

1. Is there a specific amount of time you think coaches should spend on this? If so, what is it?

>> Ideally, a couple hours/week continuing to learn/train in the craft (not just the business side, which is what I’m putting more time into lately)

2. What would putting more time into training/growing mean? What would be different?

>> Finding a balance between putting my time toward biz vs growing my craft. This is hard to do when I’m new to 2k!

“I’m a fraud/people are disappointed with what they came for.”

1. Is this true? Why or why not?

>> I think this may have been true sometimes during the first year. It’s helpful for me to acknowledge that I’ve grown a ton since then and my sessions are consistently fruitful. I do think I tend to compare to an imaginary ‘better’ coach. I also have an unrealistic expectation for crazy breakthru every session.

“I’m promising more than what I can offer (even though I consistently have my clients renew)”

1. What are you promising them?

>> In my current package: Clarifying vision, connecting to motivation, overcoming limiting mindsets, overcoming overwhelm

2. Of what you’re promising, what do you believe you cant offer?

>> Actually, I do consistently help my clients with all of the above. I guess I just can’t distill it into a step-by-step formula, so I keep thinking I’m lucking out haha. If I don’t do this, its b/c I’m honoring the client’s chosen agenda, which when I think about it I’m actually ok with 🙂

Thank you for the reframe on how coaching people through a variety of problems is a skillset in itself! This has been a super helpful exercise in challenging my automatic thoughts. Thank you so much! I welcome any further feedback!

**ANSWER:**

Absolutely, coach. Amazing work here.

How might it feel to think, “I am a brilliant coach for exactly where I am. Any client who finds their way to me needs exactly what I’ve got to offer; nothing more, nothing less.” ?

## How do you overcome imposter syndrome? – Part 2

**Date:** 2022-07-26  
**Topics:** imposter

**QUESTION:**

Hello! My response to your great questions about imposter syndrome:

*Let’s find out what’s preventing you from naturally believing you are qualified and an expert.

*What are all the doubts/thoughts you have about your current skills, experience and abilities?

> Even though I have a certification, that I need an even better one / more training, b/c mine wasn’t good enough

> That I haven’t put in as much time as other coaches into training, growing, etc. since I do this part-time

> That I’m a fraud/people are disappointed with what they came for, and/or I’m promising more than what I can offer (even though I consistently have my clients renew)

> That b/c I’m not an ‘expert’ in any one area, I’m a novice in all areas

> That I’m not as powerful/transformative as other coaches

> That I’m basically making it up as I go (which I know is untrue but sometimes feels that way when I don’t have a specific framework I’m taking them through other than a classic coaching model)

* How do you feel when you think those thoughts?

> Ashamed, guilty, anxious, unmotivated to make offers bc I doubt my ability in myself.

**ANSWER:**

Hi Coach,

Let’s explore some of these thoughts:

“Even though I have a certification, that I need an even better one / more training, b/c mine wasn’t good enough.”

1. How so? What would an “even better one” look like? What skills would you learn that you don’t feel you have right now?

“That I haven’t put in as much time as other coaches into training, growing, etc. since I do this part-time.”

1. Is there a specific amount of time you think coaches should spend on this? If so, what is it? 

2. What would putting more time into training/growing mean? What would be different?

“I’m a fraud/people are disappointed with what they came for.”

1. Is this true? Why or why not?

“I’m promising more than what I can offer (even though I consistently have my clients renew)”

1. What are you promising them?

2. Of what you’re promising, what do you believe you cant offer?

Notice how your brain is wanting to keep comparing you against “other” coaches and wanting to focus on being an “expert”. You don’t have to have a niche, a system, or one-size fits all method… You just need to be able to understand your client’s problems more than they can and help them. Are you helping your clients? Based on what you’ve shared it sounds like you are.

You previously said something very powerful also, “I meet my clients where they are.” That is a powerful 
skill
. Meeting someone where they are and offering to help means you are well-versed. You solve a variety of problems. You are trained to see many different perspectives, client blocks, and you are equipped to guide each client through it. With each variation you see in coaching, you learn what you love, what you don’t, and what patterns may keep presenting themselves in clients. This is how coaches eventually niche down or become “experts”. They start seeing the same patterns arise and helping with the same problems over and over. It’s okay if right now you’re helping clients with anything and everything. You’re adding to your skillset. Nothing has gone wrong.
 What do you think?

Bring your answers back here and we will continue to help you.

## Money & Unworthiness

**Date:** 2024-05-13  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

AAC: When you think of looking back at how much money you’ve made since you started your business, what comes up for you?

Money is merely the currency we use to buy things in our society –Imagine if sponges or paper clips were our currency. Or if our currency was a mix of selling rabbits, curtains, nail files and bananas. How do you feel when you think, “bananas scare me?” Or, “nail files are too good for me?”

What is it about money – something which is only good to exchange for goods and services – that makes you think you (who was born with incalculable human worth,) aren’t worth it?

When it comes to your business action, you said you sometimes feel exhausted. Why, specifically, do you feel exhausted in the first place?

Me

I feel overwhelmed. Like it’s too much to find it. I don’t have a business system to find any information I would need. This is showing me I need to set up a system for the backend of my business like a real business owner/CEO would have.

I’m laughing at the thought of “bananas scare me” or “Nail files are too good for me”. It’s comical & ridiculous. When I think about money I picture mean, shady & dirty(perverted) men. I know this makes no sense but that’s what comes to mind. I don’t want to be in that circle, it’s threatening & scary. I’m guessing I don’t feel worth it from my childhood trauma I was made to feel afraid and very small.

I think that’s why I like being in Stacey’s world. She’s a female that came from no money and created a world for women (and now men-but nice men from what I’ve seen) who are doing good in the world and I want to be a part of that.

I think I feel exhausted because it feels like there’s SO much to do and I never know where to start so I shut down. I’m seeing I would benefit from creating some systems for myself…

But it’s my first time trying to create a business and my brain tells me I don’t know what to create. I’ve never done it before..

C My Business

T I need a system to be a legit CEO

F Empowered

A CREATE: Money tracker, Client tracker, Content Calendar & Schedule, Potential Client Tracker with notes, Daily thought work, Running “Evaluation” document, Document with testimonials, Trello Board to create my program/offer, Notes to track how my clients are doing going through my program to be able to continually make it better & one google folder to hold all this in so I can find it on my laptop

R I have a system, I’m a legit CEO of my business

**ANSWER:**

The connections we make in our minds – the ones which hinder us – don’t have to make logical sense. That’s the cool thing about our brains, we can make all sorts of connections.

As a child, we made these connections unconsciously, based on what we believe will help us most in the moment. And now, the beauty of being an adult in a free society is we get to examine the connections we’ve made, and decide if we want to keep them based on the results they create.

You shared, “
When I think about money I picture mean, shady & dirty(perverted) men.”
 That may be one image you’ve got which feels terrible, and now you’ve also got another in Stacey and the community she’s created, “
a female that came from no money and created a world for women (and now men-but nice men from what I’ve seen) who are doing good in the world and I want to be a part of that.”

How easy is it for you to notice when you’re thinking poorly of money (based on your prior beliefs,) and gently refocus your mind to a world where people are making great money in healthy and noble ways?

Yes, systems can be a great support to you, if you’re not depending on them and instead depending on your confidence in yourself to figure it out. What do you think?

You shared, “
I think I feel exhausted because it feels like there’s SO much to do and I never know where to start so I shut down.”

* It seems you think there’s a right/wrong place to start, and doing it wrong would be unhelpful. Except, it doesn’t matter where you start – because once you start you’ll be in the mix and can evaluate and grow from that space. But if you never start, you’ll never know what to focus on and what to do to support your growth. What do you think?

## Working through worthiness

**Date:** 2022-10-24  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

Why do you think your untouchable, incomparable worthiness can possibly be impacted by something that you say, think, or do? I am assuming all my thoughts create my beliefs and my feeling of being unworthy must be something I create in my mind. It is, I believe, how I interpreted myself in my world. If I interpret something I say as “wrong” or i do something that is potentially harmful/hurtful…..then, I feel it deeply…. That is the connection, I think, between my thoughts, my actions and my feeling of my self worth.Ok, BUT, I do understand that we are born being worthy…. I inherently am worthy even if….Doesn’t matter what i think lol

When do you first remember this coming up for you? Answer: Worthiness issue comes from childhood, mostly. I was “hyperactive” as a kid and struggled in school to sit still and fit in at times. I could be a little impulsive and do stupid things and often not be able to sit still. Had a Grandmother who I spent a lot of time with that would exclude/judge me specifically for that reason. Felt rejected here. School was not easy, so had struggles here and felt stupid. I recall just feeling like a failure…like there was something wrong with me a lot of the time. Had great parents who loved me. I’m sure I would have been diagnosed with Adhd today. I do have a few very specific events in my childhood that I recall feeling shameful and looking back they were minor little things that are completely normal kid stuff. At the time, though, I took to heart. I also had a lot of anxiety as a kid that I suffered silently with this until I was older and realized other people in the world had these feelings – so worked through them. Rejection by key people in my life and worthiness seem to come to mind. Failure to do the right thing, being smart enough.

It seems that failure is a trigger for your thoughts about your worthiness…so let’s explore.

How do you define failure? Answer: failure for me is not meeting an expectation or being good enough as a friend or a parent, it is letting someone down, it can be that i was wrong, didn’t think something through, messed up. It may be as a coach that I couldn’t get someone the result they came for.

And what do you think it means about you? – So, my initial thought is that failure most likely brings me back to when I felt like this as a child. It felt as if something was wrong with me based on the reactions others had to me and my struggle to sit still and focus.

**ANSWER:**

Of course, coach.

It makes sense you think your behavior and worthiness are connected, when you made those connections at such a young age when you didn’t have other options available to you.

If your felt-experience back then was, “something is wrong with me,” rather than, “I’m an active kid and others aren’t as active and it’s safe to be different,” then of course you think your worthiness is in question.

This is the beauty of examining our beliefs and identifying them as optional thoughts – because it gives us back so much power as adults. Children don’t know their thoughts are optional, they think they’re the truth of the world. But as an adult, you get to look at the belief and decide if you want to continue believing it.

What do you think? How easy will it be for you to recognize when you think your worthiness is in question (i.e. when you’re feeling like a failure,) and remind yourself that failure isn’t absolute truth – it’s up to you to define?)

Since failure is up to you to define, you can make it whatever supports you the most. Even believing, it’s impossible to fail – only learn. E.g. “You’re either winning or learning.” Or, you could decide failure doesn’t exist, because it’s merely a judgment, not a fact. And you could decide that every attempt you make that feels like a failure is a fantastic data point for you to evaluate to find out 1) what worked, 2) what didn’t work, and 3) what you’ll be doing differently.

What do you think?

---

## money means something about worthiness

**Date:** 2022-05-16  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

Why do you think the amount of money you earn means something about your worthiness to receive it?

This question is very revealing. I started answering in a different AAC, but I want to continue my searching.

Initially I believe you HAVE to be worthy to have money. But that makes zero fucking sense, because some of the poorest people are “the most worthy.” i really don’t recognize how much my subconscious thinking objects to the idea that we are ALL 100% worthy, because when it comes to money…only the worthy people get it. I could make a case for why people with some of the MOST money in the world, aren’t as worthy as some other people are. But even that’s a lie. It comes back to all humans being worthy no matter what, it’s not based on anything…especially not money.

But this has a hold on ME when it comes to my beliefs on Money & Worthiness. I am not worthy of money. is what I have been believing. Or at least worthy of an amount of money greater than earning more than a few thousand a month. I can’t jump to I am worthy of more money than this because that feels like a straight up lie. But it is possible the two are completed unrelated. It is possible I don’t have to feel worthy of it before I can make lots of money. It’s possible I can receive more money and not tie it to explaining how worthy I am.

Perhaps I’m terrified if I make more money for myself OTHER people will tell me I’m unworthy of it, and I’m scared I’ll believe them, so I’m trying to NOT create the conditions where that could happen. So I’m feeling unworthy of it AHEAD of time. Keep my money low, keep myself out of the line of scrutiny. Keep it so they CAN’T say that. Maybe I can feel how complicated and dramatic other people make money so I’m following suit and sabotaging what I want to please their fears.

But my mind KNOWS it’s simpler. I create money with my mind cuz my mind creates value. I want it to be that easy. So why am I afraid they might call me unworthy? Why am I caring what they think?

Because in the past, my trauma response is to fawn. When people say “you’re wrong.” I say omg you’re right I’m so sorry. Omg I’ll stop, I’m so stupid. Let me go throw myself off a cliff.

Fuck. That.

I want to have more money. I want to receive more and NOT tie it to my worthiness. Other people DON’T decide what I make things mean. I DO.

This is huge. Of course I’ve been terrified to let these thoughts go.

Of course I’ve been postponing financial growth.

Trauma. I can heal.

**ANSWER:**

Absolutely, coach. And you will certainly heal it because you’re committed to yourself.

It makes so much sense that you’d be terrified of other people’s opinions, when your trauma response has been to fawn upon hearing, “you’re wrong.”

And you’re right – the only reason it would be uncomfortable for you to hear someone say, “you’re wrong – you’re not worthy to make that much money,” is if you believe them.

If you didn’t believe them, you’d think, “they’re obviously confused,” and perhaps feel a bit sorry for them.

You are worthy of every good thing that happens for you, and every good thing that you create.

What do you think?

## Pervasive unworthiness pt2

Thank you for coaching on this. I am committed to change this belief. It really does feel like a fact and every time I think of it there are 100 instances from my childhood that come up to strengthen this belief.

– Simone is not a non native English speaker, she just lives in a non western country. My brain puts all the people who are born in the west and have have English as their first language kind of in the same bucket.

I know of only one example Lisa Martinello from LCS who comes close in my brain. She’s from Italy and English is her second language. I love watching her. Again I can see my brain counteracts it with “ but has she had the success that you want?” “ is it really possible for you” . Its like it keeps looking for examples of possibility & gets disheartened when it doesn’t find it.

– how are we the same?

If I was honest, my brain resisted it so much. It felt angry too. My thoughts were ok I spend half my life noting the differences and being taught to be like them. And now the work is to find out how am I like them.

I felt Shame that I am stuck in this victimy story. I want to break this barrier not for my business but for my son too.

These are somethings I could find in response to that question:

[ we are all humans. We all have dreams. We all have challenges. We all have thoughts. ]

What comes up for me:

* this shouldn’t be this hard.

* Where are the mums who have proved it is possible with a neuro divergent child & the background I have?

* it is upto me to change this. ( but seriously why is it harder for me when others have their role models everywhere.)

* I am so pissed off at the world right now

* my child will live in this world where white, English and the west will remain the epitome of success. So unfair.

* i am looking for a needle in a haystack.

I am feeling like the more I look the more disheartened I get because I can’t find many examples. And it feels like I am trying to tell a lie to myself, & make it “ sound “ better to myself.

I feel like when I look at the combination of these three factors ( all of which were out of my control) –

I find zero examples to have a sense of possibility.

**ANSWER:**

What success have you had?

Your mind is telling you that you are unworthy of success because of your background and all of these other factors.

What success have you had already? You have signed clients.

What if we don’t even need to fully overcome all of these beliefs that you have right now? You can still create clients even with pervasive unworthiness. The beliefs and circumstances you are facing are generations old. You don’t need to get rid of them all this week. This is really important work that you are doing. The more you look for examples the more you will find them. It might not be this week or this month. This might be work that you spend your whole life unravelling – and that’s okay.

You can start unravelling all of these things and doing this work AND you can keep signing clients. How is that true?

And the more clients that you sign, the more evidence you will have that it IS possible for someone like you. Because you are doing it.

## Pervasive unworthiness

**Date:** 2021-04-15  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

I discovered an underlying pervasive constant thought that limits me: They ( people born in the west and native English speakers) are just better ( and it feels like a hierarchial thing). Like I can’t shake off the feeling that I am less than the white coaches. I noticed what it does for me is that I don’t think I am worthy of telling them anything or coaching them ( like why would they learn from ME?). Another thought is also “Of course they won’t coach with me because they don’t see me at their level, they see me less than them and I know that reality.”

It’s hard to explain, but there is a feeling of unworthiness at an inherent level because they are white ( or even black but because they are born in the west, my brain puts them in that better category too although part of my brain thinks they are one of us with similar struggles), native English speakers, born in the west. These three things make them better than me automatically.

I really find this debilitating and want to shake it off. It’s also frustrating because I feel a sense of unsafety, loneliness and always questioning whether I am authentic self or play the part of assimilating among the white and their cultural thought process. For ex – when I go around engaging in FB groups ( which have almost no one like me , and most definitely not as success figures, all my teachers are white and non ethnic. Even in 2K – there is no one who is ethnic and has english as a second language.) This frustrates me so much.

Also, in situations where a white/westerner client pays me – there is a sense of “wow – she signed up with ME?!” and not a feeling of “of course she signed up with me.”

I grew up in a culture, where going to the west was the goal in life. Where westerners were the angels on earth. Where the whole aim in life and everything we did was to be like them as much as possible. They were the people who had made it. They had everything we ever wanted. And now knowing that I am wanting to step into my full greatness and calling BS on that. But I want to do it at a deep emotional level too. Not just say it.

I’d love some coaching to question these thoughts and change how I see myself – will be much appreciated.

**ANSWER:**

How we change what we are believing is by questioning what we are believing. Is it possible that some of the thoughts our brain offers us aren’t true? There are lots of things you mentioned that you can totally prove and that are factual. But what other thoughts might help you feel more worthy?

How have you helped the clients who have signed up with you? Make a list of all the ways.

Right now your brain is focusing on how you are different. How are you the same? How are you the same as all of the other coaches in 2k? What DO you have in common with them? You are all coaches learning to make 2k. What else? How ARE you the same and just as capable? What evidence can you come up with to help your brain see how you are worthy.

You can also look for role models who are similar to you. Two of the things your brain loves to tell you make you less worthy are being not from the west and having English as a second language. Can you find examples of coaches who aren’t from the west that you can use as role models? I think of Simone Seol. She is in the 200k mastermind and lives in Korea. Who else can you find for role models?

And you are thinking that there are no teachers who are ethnic and who have English as a second language, but is that really true? I don’t know for sure, but I bet there are. You could ask in the 2k FB group. There might be fellow 2kers or they might know of coaches you can start to follow. That will help you build more and more evidence that you can be successful and that you don’t need to change who you are to do it.

What are your thoughts on that? What comes up for you?

## Felling good about money NOW / Human worthiness

**Date:** 2021-01-28  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

This is a follow up on previous Ask a Coach question I had.

You told me : “You are thinking you need to have more money before you can start feeling good about money. What if feeling good about money (or even neutral instead of shame and unworthiness) is available to you now?

And – do you really think that a human’s worthiness is based on how much money they make? I have had this thought come up a lot for me too. Is that a thought you want to keep? Or do you want to start looking at your money, and getting aware of how you are relating money and worthiness, and start letting go of that?”

This are my answers :

Yes feeling good about money is definetelly available to me NOW. What’s funny is that when I don’t, it’s always because I worry about the future, never because I miss money in the present moment. What I experienced almost my whole life and especially these past few years is that at a given time, I ALWAYS have the money I need to live a good life and make my projects and dreams come true. So maybe I can rely on this experience I got to be more confident about the future ? I WILL ALWAYS have the money I need to live a good life and make my dreams come true.

Also I would like your input on this thought : OK when I think what I wrote above, I feel much more better about my money. How can I then go further and use my thoughts to become rich? Not because I need to, but just because I want to. I think the answer lies within your remark about human worthiness not being related to the money they make. I spend my days pointing that out to my own clients.

So yes, my worthiness isn’t based on how much money or success I create. I’m worthy no matter what, such as the hobo in the street or the president of the country.

I think I can go further and use my thoughts to become rich if I want to, by not making it mean anything on my worthiness. By seeing this goal more like I game, an opportunity to grow and learn new things about myself and the world.

When I believe this thought, I feel joyful, playful, curious and grateful to get to experience human life.

When I feel these emotions, I want to meet people and create relationships and value for the sake of humanity. Money will then come naturally as a cherry on the cake.

My question : does it seem to you an intersting path to follow to go further?

Thanks

**ANSWER:**

First, what is rich? How much money would have you? Do you define it by a certain net worth? A certain income? That answer would be different for everyone. Some people would say you are rich now. What’s the answer for you?

Then you can start thinking about how to work toward it. Let’s say is $xxx in the bank. or $xxx a year. What if you knew that you are capable of doing that? What’s the first steps toward that this week? It might be signing a client or meet 5 new people.

## Thoughts about my worthiness as a business owner

**Date:** 2020-11-05  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

So, I’ve been going through the 32 day reboot – except I got a few days in and missed like a week. I’m jumping back in but I have this thought that because I can’t show up for 2kfor2k consistently, and follow through with what I said I was going to do, then I don’t deserve to have clients and make money.

This is a pattern for me. I buy new materials, courses, etc. I work my way through them partially, but don’t show up as the best student. I feel like a let down, and why would anyone want to coach with me?

**ANSWER:**

You are thinking that you don’t show up consistently. When you think that it takes you down a path of thinking that no one will want to coach with you.

What if we changed how you are thinking about yourself? How is it true that you DO show up consistently?

Showing up consistently doesn’t have to mean that we show up everyday or that we always follow our plan exactly. Showing up consistently means doing exactly what you are doing – showing up, then noticing when we stop showing up and trying again and again until we reach our goals. You keep showing up and trying. That’s real consistency.

What happens when you think of yourself as a person who DOES keep showing up? Who consistently keeps trying? How is that true already?

---

## Unworthiness spiral?

**Date:** 2019-11-14  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

Maybe it’s a shame spiral, but an unworthiness spiral feels more accurate.

Question: I can see the loop that I am creating with my unintentional model. All of the thoughts I have tried to put in my intentional model feel unreachable which is just leading to more of the first model. How do I create an intention model that feels possible?

C: Working

T: You are never going to be the coach that you want to be.

F: Worthless

A: Stress eat and watch TV.

Irritable and short tempered – snappy with my kids.

Compare/Despair to other coaches.

Stopped working before I ever really started.

Mentally beat myself up for all of the above

R: Not at all who I want to be.

Intentional Model

C: Working

T: I am me??? I like me??? I get to choose how I show up??? I am a kick ass coach???

F: Worthy

A: Meet people

Tell them I am a coach

Make offers to help

R: ????

My own answer is to bridge the gap between feeling unworthy to worthy. It’s too big a leap for me right now and that is why I am having trouble coming up with what I would need to be thinking to feel worthy.

**ANSWER:**

Start smaller. Instead of working on being worthy, lets look at how you can start feeling better and taking some action.

It’s interesting because in reading what you wrote it looks like your goal is to feel worthy, but I am guessing that your goal is actually to make money or sign a client because you signed up for 2k and that you think that feeling worthy is what will create signing a client and making money.

Let’s start with your C – working. I am taking a guess that by working you mean taking action in your business. If this isn’t what you mean, then you can clarify and ask again. This coaching will be useful either way. 🙂 Working on our business is an action that comes from what we are thinking and feeling. What happens when we put working in the A line? What would you need to think and feel to create the action of working? The feeling might not be worthy. It might be something else.

C

T

F

A working (list what specifically this looks like. Is it going to a networking event to meet people and tell them you are a coach? Is it creating a post on social media that connects to your audience? Is it making a plan for what you will work on?)

R

## follow up to 5 of 30 days of coaching-so day 7 of 30 days of coaching

**Date:** 2026-05-07  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

I just want to thank you for that beautiful coaching. It seriously brought tears to my eyes. So much of it is spot on. Smart, articulate, and quick-witted were so praised in my family and society in general. Good grades and accomplishments were the things that were going to get you far…not just touchy-feely soft skills. I have two master’s degrees and was admitted to a PsyD program that I opted out of (I was two weeks postpartum when I was accepted and just couldn’t do it!!). But the pursuit of knowledge has always been this thing that I thought would make me “good enough.” I still want that Dr. in front of my name (it won’t happen, I have two kids in college, and I am 52, now I just want to make money). I am also surrounded by these really smart people, like true intellectuals, and I do feel less than because of the aforementioned thoughts about myself. And, OMG. They are just fucking thoughts.

I am sitting with these sentences: “Those two pictures do not match which makes me curious if this may have less to do with your actual capability, and more to do with an “identity” tug-of-war. Almost like your brain is holding onto an identity that possibly believes people who come from “nothing” have to over prove their worth before they are allowed to fully own it.” There is something there-I definitely feel like I have to prove my worth; it is very likely a result of coming from nothing and deeper attachment wounds that caused me to always try to prove that I am worthy. My worthiness has come far. I feel myself settling into my body and who I am. And then here…I hit another layer of it, and in this interesting way, I am at odds with myself and this identity tug of war. My strengths and skills are not what I have been programmed to prove “worthiness.” And, ha, the spiritual yogi side of me knows that it is all BS. You breathe, and you are valuable…no matter what. A good reminder.

**ANSWER:**

Hey there Coach,

I am so glad that previous coaching landed so powerfully for you. Happy to help. And I think you’re uncovering something much deeper than “I don’t feel smart enough.” I think you’re starting to see how
 tightly
 your brain learned to attach worthiness, safety, and legitimacy to achievement, intellect, and external validation.

What stands out to me currently is this “tension” between the part of you that deeply knows human worth is inherent… and the part of you that still feels pulled toward earning it through credentials, titles, accomplishment, and proving. Even as you say, 
“I know it’s all BS,”
 there is still a very human part of you that wonders if the “Dr.” title would finally quiet something internally (even though you’ve decided it’s not something you will pursue).

So this of course makes me curious… what do you imagine that title would 
finally allow you to feel about yourself that you may struggle to fully allow now?
 Respect? Legitimacy? Safety? Belonging? Relief? Superiority? Enoughness? Something else?

I also am curious what your brain has made “intellectualism” mean over the years. There’s a difference between intelligence and identity. Somewhere along the way, it seems like your brain created a “hierarchy” where quick-witted, highly academic, exceptionally articulate people ended up at 
the top
 of the “worthy” pile. And because you do not experience yourself that way internally 
(right now)
, your brain keeps trying to place you lower on the scale – despite decades of evidence that you are deeply impactful!

What’s fascinating is that the very qualities your brain dismisses as “soft” are the same qualities creating trust, safety, transformation, referrals, and connection in your clients.

What if your life is asking you to stop proving your worth through the exact metrics that “wounded” you into questioning it in the first place?

As as observer, I do not see a woman lacking intelligence or capability. I see a woman whose nervous system still feels emotionally tied to the belief that she must achieve at extraordinary levels BEFORE she is fully allowed to relax into her own value. And that is soo very different.

What are your thoughts? Feel free to sit with this and when you’re ready come back for any specific coaching you may still need. Thanks coach – you’re doing great!

---

## £10K package identity

**Date:** 2026-03-23  
**Topics:** worthiness, identity

**QUESTION:**

Hi there,

I want to create the identity of someone who sells £10K year-long containers.

I took a couple years away to focus on mothering so it’s been awhile since I’ve sold anything. Before that I was selling 6 months for £3.5K.

So this feels like a leap.

C – selling £10k year long package

Thoughts – I don’t have the marketing skill to sell at this level.

F – panic

A – freeze, spin in overwhelm, scatter my attention, try to learn too many things, get distracted, check out what everyone else is doing

R – I don’t develop the skill to sell at this level

T – no one will want to invest with me at this level

F – unworthy

A – focus my attention here – don’t create clarity, or reasons for people to invest, don’t make offers, make offers from ‘unworthiness’, hide, show up apologetically, hedge my actions

R- No one invests with me

T – I don’t have the identity to sell this

F- defeated

A – perform identity, use adrenaline to take actions, drop price, create 1-off offers, hide, avoid big actions

R – I don’t create identity

T – I don’t trust myself to be able to build the skill and identity needed to sell at this level

F – fear

A – spend time solving for lack of trust, find evidence that I can’t trust myself, see failures as evidence that I won’t do it,

R – I don’t build identity

T – I wouldn’t hire me with the lack of clarity/ articulation of my offer

F – shit

A – give up

R – No one hires me

T – It’s too big of a leap I should practice with a lower number and build identity/ confidence

F – cautious

A – drop price, make 1-off offers, keep actions small

R – Don’t learn how to sell £10k offer

T – I’m not accomplished enough to charge this much

F – failure

A – don’t acknowledge accomplishments, don’t celebrates wins, discredit accomplishments, experience and skills

R – don’t become more accomplished?

**ANSWER:**

Hey there Coach,

Welcome back, both to coaching and to this version of you that’s ready to go bigger. That matters. And you’ve already done something really important here, you’ve laid out the exact pattern your brain will run if left unchecked. That awareness is powerful.

Before we dive into the actions of a 10K coach selling a year-long container, I want to stay where you started with the identity piece.

Because you’ve already identified it feels like a leap and I agree. Not because it’s too big but because your current identity doesn’t fully believe in it yet. Your current limiting thought make it a leap not the goal itself.  And that’s okay. That’s actually right on time.

Whenever we set a goal that stretches our current identity, all the limiting beliefs show up. They’re not a problem, they’re part of the process. The work isn’t to eliminate them, it’s to 
build the identity of the coach you want to be 
in her marketing, her selling, and her delivery.

So let’s build her out more intentionally.

C — Selling 10K year-long container

Now from here, instead of focusing on current limiting thoughts, I want you to explore the identity of a 10k coach, how she thinks and feels and shows up.

What does she think about marketing?
(I’m asking this because right now your brain is telling you that you don’t have the skill. That belief will either create avoidance or overconsumption. Her thoughts will create something very different.)

What does she think about people investing with her at this level?
(This matters because “no one will want this” creates hesitation and hiding. Her belief is what allows her to invite cleanly.)

What is her identity what does she believe about herself?
(Is she waiting to feel accomplished enough… or has she decided who she is?)

What does she believe about trust?
(I hear you trying to solve for trust before action. But trust is built with a decision ) What does she think when things not going the way expected?

Why would someone hire you, what are you clear on, and how does she articulate her offer?
(This isn’t to prove you’re not ready it’s to help you create clarity on purpose.)

What does she think about this number from having already done it? (So you’re not relating to 10K as a stretch, but as something normal.)

How is she already accomplished enough?
(This question matters because right now you’re discrediting your experience. She doesn’t do that she uses it.)

The part that feels missing for me isn’t more learning it’s selling from your reasons. Selling 10K isn’t something you wait to believe after clients get results. It’s something you decide and then practice believing every single day.

“I chose 10K.”
Now the work becomes: Can I commit to 10K? Can I believe in 10K? Can I carry myself as a 10K coach today?

And I want to gently challenge this thought:
“It’s too big of a leap”

Just notice if you drop the price to feel safer, you don’t actually learn how to sell 10K. You attach the selling to the price and the identity to the price.   You stay in the identity you’re trying to outgrow. What are the reasons you want to sell it at 10k and create the identity?  If you like those reasons then notice ” too big a leap ” is not the truth about 10K its optional thought too.

The process is the same either way:
Decide who you need to become and practice becoming her as quickly as possible.

That’s what will attract clients who want that version of you.

I’d also recommend running your offer through the IOP in the Offer Week bonus section especially to strengthen your clarity and articulation. That’s a skill you build by doing, not by waiting to feel ready.

You’re not off track here.
You’re just in the middle of the identity shift.

Tell me what are your thoughts when you think about fully choosing  10K ?

---

## #61 consult eval pt 4

**Date:** 2025-04-22  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

I asked what is she wanting to feel in our work together- another random question… my intake does not feel like a conversation like I’d want it to, it feels more like a checklist, which might be ok

but she said, I want to feel confident in speaking and going into rooms without feeling frightened because someone already has something that I want. I don’t want to clam up as much when I am public speaking. I am so forgetful and that scares me.

she then mentioned that everyone around her wants to make her an extrovert and she is fine being an introvert. I’m forcing myself to go to things, and I’m happy about it, and she started talking about what other people try to make her be. She brought that up but when I asked if she was ok with that, she was – so I Was confused on why she brought it up.

I then asked if she procrastinated/neagtive habits ,etc… she gave answers to some but not anything that sounded like it affected her…she’d procrastinating starting school but she’s in school now taking action and felt really confident that she’d finish. she said she doesn’t answer the phone and changes minds on events, but looking back that was likely because of her thoughts about herself when being around people.

I don’t use the life wheel… I like more conversational style.

and I don’t know in these conversations if I am to ask what things would be like if they weren’t experiencing that or the impact of etc, when I am going to present that in BTG and also paint the vision with them.

she talked about being at networking events and feeling oh here we go agin, what should I say, etc..and it’s exhausting -all of this was coming from her lack of believe and worthiness in her own self, right?

when I asked about perfectionism and she answered giving info on how that shows up at work ,and also forgetting things- I just didn’t know if ti was really a problem I think many of these questions may not have anything to do with what she needed help with.

simply put she needed to believe different things about herself, her accomplishments and start valuing herself so she doesn’t compare herself to others. and let go of what she didn’t do in the past.

**ANSWER:**

One of the skills that will help you on consults is learning how to summarize. You want to be able to take the information they are giving you, then summarize it. You organize it and put it together so things feel clear for you and for them. It helps you both.

Based on this consult:

Where is she now?

Where does she want to be?

What are the steps you are going to help her with to get her there?

Picture this like how Stacey has this laid out in the Bridging the Gap video in the consults module. It might be a few different points that you work on or it might be one big thing of confidence broken down into different places. For example, confidence at work with other collegiate, at work with her work confidence in her work, with friends, etc.

Your mind might think that you don’t know. And you don’t have to know it perfectly. You just have to write down what you DO know. If the client was with you on the call, you’d do this, share it back with them, and you can ask if they want to add anything or change anything. So you don’t have to get it “right” you just have to get it started.

## Goals Driving Purpose from Fear

**Date:** 2025-01-22  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

“If we dropped all of these thoughts about your goals and what you should be doing, what do you need right now today?”

This is the question that was asked from my last coaching submission. Here are the thoughts that come up as soon as I ask that question and really listen:

I just want to sleep

I am so tired

I want to stop

I want to slow down

I can’t hear anything

I am exhausted

When will this be easier

I don’t want to give up on these goals but I can’t keep going

I am so tired

If I slow down, I won’t get it

If I stop thinking about it, it will never happen

But I don’t even know what I want and I don’t have the space to hear it

Will life always be like this where I use tired as an excuse

People do things tired all the time just keep going

Do it tired

If I had no goals, I would have no purpose

what’s my point here if I’m just existing

what am I seriously good for if its not going for things

I am bored

I need something to go for

I want to change so much and nothing at all

there’s so much I want and I want to do nothing at the same time

Notices: It seems like if I don’t have a goal, I don’t feel valuable or like I matter. Like having a goal makes you more important, worthy, interesting, and if you dont have one whether you are acting on it or not, you are straight up useless. And it’s bad to be useless because if you’re useless, why are you here? Why are you alive? Why do you matter?

I have ALWAYS struggled with inherent worthiness. That me being here is enough. Me just being is enough. I reach that stop point when I am challenged by the thought – what if you didn’t get anything you wanted for the rest of your life?

If I didn’t get the house I want to buy for my family, if I didn’t make 100k in the next year, if I didn’t lose the 10lbs, if I didn’t get to do cool things again physically, if I didnt have another kid, it fills me with panic. I HAVE to grow. It’s almost this NEED. But I think I am starting realize it’s terror, it’s fear, it’s a fuel that’s really hurting me. Maybe it’s shame.

These thoughts started a thread that I truly would love coaching on. There is no amount of action out there that will help me feel better about actually feeling like I shouldn’t exist if I don’t go for goals and stand out.

**ANSWER:**

Right now this is how your mind is seeing things: “If I didn’t get the house I want to buy for my family, if I didn’t make 100k in the next year, if I didn’t lose the 10lbs, if I didn’t get to do cool things again physically, if I didnt have another kid, it fills me with panic.”

But what would really happen if this year you didn’t lose 10lbs, you didn’t make 100k, and you didn’t have another kid? What would it be like if you didn’t HAVE to get those things done this year?

What would it be like if you never HAD to get them done? I’m not saying you can’t do them. But what if you didn’t HAVE to do them?

What if you could say – actually I don’t want to lose the 10lbs. I would just rather spend my energy on other things right now. If I say that to you – what would you think about me?

## The reflection and the opportunity

**Date:** 2024-12-09  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

I’m going to do it. I feel so grounded. I’m going to sell 500 spots in my membership. It’s going to be quicker than I think. Rather than focus on time. I’ve set the goal for a year to accomplish this.

The value inside is so high. I’m so grateful to serve this way. The ideas and creativity keep flowing. I’m not doing this alone. Through prayer and my relationship with God, I see his blessing and what is possible. That is so refreshing.

I’ve added meditations to my day in addition to Bible reading, and it is so filling.

Now I get to serve my community of women as they set their self-care routines that fuel them. Stepping away from the rush and stepping into their lives full. Letting the unworthiness go and enjoying the moments. Full present.

I really feel like the women in the family lift each other up. When we’re well cared for, we have so much heart to give. It’s not about the time. These are the things I’m sharing with her as a tiny piece.

I can see the path to market, sell, and introduce these 500 and beyond women to this course/program. I’m honored. I’m offering The 1:1 Experience to anyone who wants to upgrade. That feels so good.

When it comes to health, I have a coaching question because I can see it reflect in business, as well.

Sometimes I get to a point where I have success: I’ve lost the weight, grown the muscle, tracked the food, etc. I did a good job and love my results. Then I stop. I think ‘I can do this on my own now.’ Which looks like no schedule anymore, no tracking anymore, winging it. I think there is a level of self-trust where I can weave in some reliance on myself to carry through but ALSO continue to schedule a track food, marketing. Etc.

Can you help me explore this action of ’stopping’ from the thought ‘ I can do this on my own now.’ I will model as well. Thank you!

**ANSWER:**

Yes, coach – you’ve got this!

As for your thought…”I can do this on my own now…”

## Busting the feeling of Unworthy

**Date:** 2024-12-05  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

How I feel right now is the feeling I want to operate from most of the time. It’s calm, clear, focused, open, belief. THIS IS MY AUTHENTIC feeling. My most grounded and explorative.

From this place, I can see that when I feel unworthy, less than, or a hollow-ness inside that those feelings are not the ‘signal’ that I need to do better or am not doing enough (which are the thoughts that come with it). Instead those feelings are the signal to re-anchor. To pray.

To remember who I am. That of course, I will prioritize improving, but that feeling isn’t an indicator that I’m not doing enough. It’s simple a ‘short’ in my wiring (that feels useful). Almost like when you plug the hairdryer in and need to press the ‘reset’ button on the hair dryer. It’s not broken. Nothing is wrong with is, but it needs a pulse of energy from the socket to reset.

That’s me. That’s my clients. That feeling is the signal for the reset button. Not an indicator that you’ve done something wrong or aren’t doing enough.

AND I look objectively at areas of improvement in productive blocks of time, doing evals, not in the middle of the night when the anxiety hits. That’s not where I make decisions. I soothe myself, remind myself I’m here, pray, and breathe. But that emotion is not where I’m making decisions.

OK, now for what feels like the hard part. I’m here for it, but I want to TRUST that I will remember this. That I won’t ‘succumb’ or be too distracted by the feelings of unworthiness. I want to get better and better and spotting it.

Do you have some guidance to unwind that with me? Thanks! This is a big win.

This is what I want to say to myself when I feel unworthy: This feeling makes sense. This feelings does not mean anything has gone wrong. Of course, you will improve where you want to improve, but this feeling is not an indicator that you’re not doing enough. Take the facts into an evaluation and look from THERE to see where improvement is objectively. That’s where you’ll see the next steps and if anything needs to change / be adjusted. Don’t rely on the feeling alone. Look at the intentional thoughts and discernment. Unworthiness is a feeling that will pop up. Maybe often. Maybe not. Don’t use it as WHO you are as a person. Use it as an opportunity to make an appointment with yourself to do an evaluation. No judgement needed.

**ANSWER:**

I really like everything you said here. You know a feeling will just be there sometimes.

Sometimes if we are tired, or hungry, or just not feeling 100% physically, or have some stress, it’s more likely our mind will offer us feelings we don’t like. We aren’t going to feel how we want to feel all of the time. That’s part of being a human. It would be strange to never have any other feeling other than a good feeling.

Something I like to ask myself is – does this feeling feel proportionate to what’s happening? Is it temporary?

Or does this feeling feel disproportionate to what’s happening? Does it keep coming up?

If it’s the first two – proportionate and temporary. It’s probably just part of the human experience.

But if it keeps coming up. And it feels a bit extreme compare to the situation – that’s when we want to start looking at what’s creating it. What are these thought patterns that keep creating this feeling so I can unravel them. Or what are the triggers. Just like you said with the evaluations. Instead of trying to fix it and figure out how to be more “worthy”, you want to look at what thoughts or patterns are creating the unworthy feeling.
