## Niching Around Part 4

**Date:** 2024-05-03  
**Topics:** worthiness

**QUESTION:**

You previously shared you enjoy, “Helping folks who are taking adulting wayyyy too seriously and want that hop, skip and jump back in their lives and helping all those grown ass adults have grown ass fun again.”

This sounds like fun, but is it something you easily believe people are willing to pay for?

My response: I think like any niche it could work as long as I can get into my clients’ heads and speak to them like I know them. I might not be saying “ let me help you have more fun in your life!” but saying to them about how I can help them be less busy and more filled up.

What specific problems are solved when you help clients stop adulting too seriously?

My response:

They are so much happier. They are so much more relaxed. They allow their lives to be led by what they want as opposed to what they think they should be doing. And because of this they have happier and more fulfilling relationships, they build businesses that are exciting and unique, they go on those vacations they never thought possible and while they travel meet new kinds of people, they have more adventurous and are open to the world in a way they never were- kind of like a college student on a study abroad program. That kind of openness to the world.

What are the benefits of those problems being solved?

My response: They are just gifting themselves the opportunity to live any kind of life they want to. They are not trapped in just one kind of life.

What else is possible for them now that those problems are solved?

They no longer spend their time in self-doubt and trying to prove their worthiness but spend that time exploring who they want to be and trying things out like a scientist and having the self-confidence of a rockstar.

Are those results people are eager to pay for? Why or why not?

My gut- yes. I do think so. I think just as much as having a killer body someone would pay big money to bring back their JOYFUL and happy demeanor they used to have when they were younger and more carefree.

What niche decision are you leaning toward currently? How do you feel when you think about making that decision?

My response: Before I responded to these questions, business coaching, and after answering these questions- bringing back FUN, coaching. 🙂

When I think about making the decision I feel a little annoyed that I don’t know yet. I’m ready to go all in and get more specific with with my copy and clients and I can see the benefits of both niches.

**ANSWER:**

Of course there are benefits with both niches. There are also drawbacks.

And the great thing about being an adult in a free society is we largely get to make decisions for which benefits and drawbacks we want to live with.

You know which group lights you up – what’s preventing you from easily going all in with it?

**Topics:** identity

What i feel/ believe as a result is that people don’t take me seriously.

I feel ashamed that I’m “still” not able to have the energy that people trust. I’ve been doing this for long enough – I should have figured this out by now.

I used it to invite people into my first free offer in years. Once it was done, I stopped doing them.

I went back to recording taking head videos.

The reason I’m mentioning this is because I’m wondering if the reason I stopped doing that type of content was actually just me trying to return to a what feels safer.

I feel inconsequential again. Feel like the talking heads don’t feel as powerful (ie. Not taking seriously) might reveal more of actual thoughts about myself.

 the writing/posting

“I felt powerful
 in creating it.”
 and then you asked,

“more powerful “
 identity rupturing type content.”

## answers to 10K identity

**Date:** 2026-03-30  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

1.

She believes marketing is love letters to her audience. She’s skilled at using stories. Marketing is selling people on the vision. She believes so much in the work that she’s here to do, in the vision that she sees for the world – that she loves inviting people into that world. Marketing feels like breathing to her. She also committed to creating sharper and more precise language to describe her offer.

2.

This is a deeply transformational container that shifts the way they relate to themselves which shifts everything in their life. She’s deeply honoured by the women who are choosing the value themselves enough to make their transformation inevitable. When women invest in themselves at this level they are shifting time-lines – not just for them but for their children. She is investing at this level because she values the work that I’m here to facilitate and trusts herself to be able to create what she wants in her life. That it is that – an investment – that women who value the work will be happy to do.

3.

She values her own experience. She knows herself to be a woman with deeply lived and earned wisdom across a number of life thesholds. She has depth, soul, integrity, she has navigated hardships, trauma, motherhood and has created fertile ground out of it all. She has the skill and capacity to hold women in their depth, to deeply witness and support their unfolding. Because she has lived it. She feels this rooted in her being.

4.

This is the one that trips me up the most I think – funnily. She always first asks – how could this be working in my favour. She clarifies what it is she actually wants. And what she’s willing to do to create what she wants.

She looks objectively when things do go as planned. (not personally!!!) And evaluates (I’m NOT good at this. It feels like it takes so much energy and time to process my emotions that to try to figure out what shifts needed to be made – literally figure out what’s working, what’s not – feels like it would be a barrier to me taking action. So I chose not to evaluate and just keep taking ‘messy’ action hoping/ believing this will lead me to learning by doing).

5.

Women who know they are capable and confident but who are also sensitive who feel trapped by fear and are deeply conditioned into ‘good-girl’ self-abandonment – through somatic shadow work and fierce re-mothering, I help them feel powerfully self-lead without dominating themselves so they can lead from their inner authority

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach,

First, I just want to reflect back what you did here because it matters you spent real time in the version of you who already has what you want. And you didn’t just visit her you spoke as her and spent time imagining her. The clarity, the depth, the way she articulates her work and the women she serves that’s not theory that is tapping into the future version and mining her for wisdom and how to go.  That’s available to you now. You can feel how grounded and in service she is available to you for guidance and wisdom.

What I want to gently but directly bring your attention to is the part you identified as the hardest: the evaluation process.

This is the exact place where your next level is.

You said it feels like it takes too much energy,  like it might slow you down, so instead you keep taking messy action and trust you’ll learn by doing. And I want to offer you this perspective, learning doesn’t come from doing alone. It comes from doing and then deciding what it meant. Turning fails into gold, extracting  all the gold in each attempt.

Otherwise, you’re running the same experiment over and over and hoping for a different outcome. Hope is not a strategy that works here.

So let me ask you a question, and I want you to really sit with it:

What do you think happens if you actually do evaluate? This is when trusting the process works.

I’m asking because your answer will show you what you’re making that process mean. Is it heavy? Time-consuming? Confronting? Does it require you to see something you’d rather not? That’s where the resistance is. not in the process itself, but in the meaning you’ve attached to it.

Evaluation isn’t here to slow you down. It’s here to speed you up.

Because when you look at what worked, you can do it again on purpose. When you see what didn’t, you can adjust specifically. This is how every “failure” becomes a roadmap instead of a weight. It’s like mining for gold you don’t skip the digging part and hope to just stumble upon it. The gold is in the digging.

And right now, you’re skipping the part where the gold gets revealed.

So I want to ask you:

If you were willing to evaluate  even imperfectly what impact would that have on your growth?

And another one:

What would the entrepreneur you’re becoming do when she feels this resistance?

Because this is part of her identity. She doesn’t make it mean something about her worth. She doesn’t avoid it. She uses it. This is emotional maturity in business being able to look at your work, unattached, and decide what it’s teaching you.

You also mentioned how much you value your depth, your lived experience, your ability to hold others through transformation. This is you doing that for yourself. Not abandoning yourself in the discomfort of reflection, but staying with it long enough to extract insight.

So instead of thinking you need to evaluate everything, let’s simplify it: What are the most useful things for you to look at right now that would actually move you forward?

Maybe it’s your messaging.

Maybe it’s how your offers are landing.

Maybe it’s your planning because often your first plan is just your best guess, not your best strategy.

What do you think will help the most right now?

And here’s something to notice, the place you most want to avoid looking is usually the place that will create the fastest growth.

You don’t learn just by doing.

You learn by doing, looking, and then choosing differently.

Stacey teaches evaluation as a core part of the 2k process because it  actually creates results. It’s not about overthinking or slowing down,  intentionally look at your actions, the result, and what you want to create differently so you can move forward with clarity. She encourages you to bring your evaluations to ACC and in all the rooms she encourages this because this is where the real learning happens you refine, you see what’s working, and you get coached on what to adjust.

This is the work that creates momentum. It’s the difference between hoping your actions work and knowing how to make them work.

So the invitation here isn’t to do this perfectly. It’s to let it feel slow. To let it feel a little uncomfortable. To stop resisting the very process that will build your confidence and precision.

This is how you become the woman you described at the beginning.

You’re not off track you’re right at the edge of refinement.

Don’t resist the process. You’ve got this, coach.

## £10K package identity2

**Date:** 2026-03-30  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

Now the work becomes: Can I commit to 10K? Can I believe in 10K? Can I carry myself as a 10K coach today?

So – I’ve been moving through a deep desire to quit over the last week. I realise this is just my system re-callibrating to this new desire. Holding myself through it felt pretty intense.

I’ve been watching all the things my mind and body want to do to get me to abandon this plan.

Part of me feels like I’d need a 1:1 coach to support me through this portal. I do have access to a lot of resources. I’m proving to myself that I can support myself through as well – which feels good.

I feel like I’m working from pure delusion. Like I’m starting from scratch – not as someone how left a 6-figure corporate job. As someone who’s been a stay at home mom and worked minimum wage jobs before that.

There are time where it feels really alivening – like this is a fun game to play. And it does feel like it would take a lot to protect this feeling/ mindset

I thought I would use this on my socials – like ‘come with me as I step into the identity of a woman who sells £10K packages’. And frame my work to support women to step into their next level of embodied leadership.

I’ve already started telling people that my next 1:1 package will be £10K. Haven’t made any offers yet.

The thing that is proving to be the most difficult – is my brain saying that I’m doing it wrong. I’m not saying the ‘right’ things in the reals. I’m not coming from the ‘right’ energy. I’m not taking the ‘right’ actions. This is what leads me to wanting a 1:1 coach so they can point out my blind spots.

**ANSWER:**

Hey coach,

I want to start where you started the desire to quit. And here’s why that matters.

When you make a real decision not a surface-level one, but the kind that asks more of you doubt and resistance are part of the process. It’s not a sign you’re off. It’s actually a sign you’ve moved into something new. So the work isn’t to eliminate the desire to quit… it’s to understand that after the decision, the next step is recommitting. Daily. This is power move and will help with the the next steps of making offers.

Otherwise, your energy goes into re-deciding instead of building.

Right now, I see your brain offering you a story that you’re “starting from scratch.” I want you to pause there. Because your brain will always go find evidence for whatever you tell it is true. So if the thought is “I’m starting over,” it will show you minimum wage jobs and staying home. It will delete the 6 figure identity and all the skills you have already learned. But if the thought is “I’m a woman who has already created results and is now expanding them,” it will pull very different evidence.

That’s not fluff, that’s direction that is the mindset work necessary to create the self concept of a successful 10k coach, before you have the clients paying you 10k, you need to believe it..

And this piece really matters: learning how to believe in something before there’s evidence is 
the work
. Not just for you, but for the clients you want to lead. If you can walk yourself through this  believing in a $10K offer before it’s sold you become someone who can hold that belief for others too.

So I want to ask you a couple of things, very intentionally.

When your brain says, “I’m doing it wrong,” how are you responding to it? What are you focused on? 
I’m asking because if that thought goes unquestioned, it becomes authority  and it shuts down creativity, testing, and momentum.

What does “the right way” actually mean to you?
Not generally specifically? How are you measuring that ” right way” What are you making “right” mean in your content, your energy, your actions?

And how do you know when you’re in the “right energy”?
Because if that’s vague, your brain will keep moving the target — and you’ll keep feeling like you’re missing it.

This is where I’d guide you to get really specific. “Not right” isn’t useful feedback yet. It’s just a flag. Your job is to turn it into something you can actually work with.

You’re in a phase that requires testing. Iteration. Trying things, evaluating them, refining. You don’t 
know
 how to sell a $10K offer yet and that’s expected. The question is: are you willing to not know, and keep going anyway?

What if you expected this to feel like trial and error?
What if getting it “wrong” was part of getting it right?

On the coaching question I’m going to be very honest with you.

Having a coach is powerful. It will show you blind spots faster, support your nervous system, and collapse time. And you do not need one to create this result. That is the truth.  Having a coach is such a benefit for reasons you and I are both aware and don’t use that against you here.

So the question becomes:
Do you want support from a place of expansion and desire?
Or from a belief that you can’t do this without it?

Both are available to you  but they create very different experiences.

You’re already doing something really important: you’re watching your mind, instead of blindly following it. That’s the work. Now we refine how you respond to it.

And everything you’re describing the intensity, the doubt, the aliveness that’s what it looks like to step into a new identity before it feels natural.

Stay with it coach!

## Building Identity underneath my actions…part 2…

**Date:** 2026-02-26  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

This is the 2nd part to my previos LLOOONNGG answer ….

I am going to work on thinking better thoughts as I’m taking action. I love the thoughts listed in my last response, and in addition to yours, I’m going to add some of these…

“The right people will hear what I’m saying.”

“I am right on time.”

“My clients are out there.”

“I will keep going.”

“I AM a coach.” – I think this one feels tough because I don’t have any $$ to prove that what I’m doing is working. I will work on not needing money to prove this to me, but instead some of the nice messages I get from people who interact with my content.

I’d love a clearer plan to go from “here’s some info” to “I can help you.”

I love your questions about where I’m meeting people, etc.

I need to do more of this! I know tons of people, but I’m not going to places to meet new people. Also, if I really think about it, I don’t really ever make offers to help people. I think I am waiting for them to magically ask if they can work with me. I’m thinking if I had more confidence and clarity in what I do to help people, I’d be offering left and right.

I know my thoughts are primary, but I wonder if having a more concrete program with specific outcomes would sell me on my offer and make me want to invite more people into it? Am I just making that up ?

Maybe my next steps should be incorporating the helpful thoughts and finding some new real-life events to attend and meet new people.

If my result is “I create clients,”

T- “Clients are waiting for me and want me to work with them.”

F- I would be feeling confident and grounded.

A-I would be more likely to show up as a coach and not someone who hopes to be a coach someday.

In your last response, you asked me to decide how to meet people. I will start by saying yes to more things. I am the person who talks to strangers everywhere. Now I just need to decide that I’m speaking to them as a coach. If I’m focusing on midlife women, I’d say that I help them to live their happiest, healthiest and most fulfilled life now, and I want them to setup the foundation for a future that will never feel empty.

How do you recommend that I narrow or not narrow my audience and message? Do you think 4 areas of focus is too many?

Thank you!!!!

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach,

I love seeing the thoughts you’re choosing on purpose. “I am a coach.” “The right people will hear me.” “My clients are out there.” These aren’t just nice thoughts they’re direction-setting thoughts. Your brain will start organizing your actions around what you believe is true.

You mentioned not having the $$ yet to prove it. This is where most people unknowingly stop themselves. There is always lag time between the moment you decide and the moment the result shows up. Results are the echo of your belief and actions, not the starting point. This is why it matters that you don’t use money as the only form of evidence. Money is one form. But there are many others: people responding to your content, people opening up to you, people feeling seen by you, people asking questions, people lingering in conversation. All of that is evidence that it’s working.

I want you to deliberately ask yourself: how is this already working? I’m asking because what your brain looks for, it finds. If you only look for money, you’ll miss the entire path that leads to money. But if you collect evidence along the way, you strengthen the belief that keeps you moving long enough for the result to materialize.

You also asked about having a clearer plan and whether creating a concrete program would help you feel more confident and make more offers. This is such an important awareness. A program doesn’t create confidence. Your thinking creates confidence. But a structure can help focus your thinking. It gives your brain something tangible to stand on.

At the same time, clarity doesn’t come before action. Clarity comes from action.

Let me explain why this matters.

If you wait to feel perfectly clear before you invite people in, you’ll delay the very experiences that create clarity. Your process will be refined through conversations. Through listening. Through helping. Through seeing what actually moves people forward. Your first version isn’t meant to be perfect. It’s meant to be real.

So a useful question for you is this: what are you helping people do right now and yourself?

Not someday. Not in theory. But based on the tools you already use in your own life.

I’m asking because your offer is simply an extension of what you already know how to do.

For example, if a midlife woman feels stuck, drained, or disconnected from herself, what specifically changes after working with you? Does she trust herself more? Make decisions faster? Feel more energized? Stop abandoning herself? The more clearly you can see the before and after, the more naturally you’ll speak about your work.

And this connects directly to something you said that was very insightful you’re waiting for people to magically ask to work with you.

Most people don’t ask. Not because they don’t want help, but because they don’t know help is available This is why your role is to extend the invitation. Not from pressure. But from service.

You’re not convincing. You’re opening a door.

When you meet people, you don’t need to perform or prove anything. You simply listen. You care. And when it’s relevant, you can say something simple like, “This is actually the kind of work I do with women. I help them reconnect to themselves and create a life that feels fulfilling now, not someday.”

That’s enough. The right people will lean in.

You also asked about narrowing your audience and whether four areas of focus is too many. The real question isn’t the number. The real question is whether your message is clear to the person hearing it.

When someone hears you speak, can they quickly recognize themselves?

But you don’t have to solve this perfectly in advance. You can test. You can speak to midlife women and notice what resonates most. You can pay attention to which conversations feel most alive. Your audience will reveal itself through interaction.

Think of it like tuning a radio. You adjust the dial as you listen for the clearest signal. You’re already doing the most important thing you’ve decided to show up as a coach.

What would change if you fully stopped waiting for proof and instead decided that this period is the process of becoming?

Because this is the phase where you build skill, identity, and self-trust. This is where you stop hoping and start embodying.

You don’t become a coach when the money shows up.

The money shows up when you become the coach.

And you’re already in motion.

Keep going.

---

## Building Identity underneath my actions…part 1…

**Date:** 2026-02-26  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

Hello Coach!

First off, I’d love to express my sincere gratitude for this aspect of 2K. It is so valuable! I appreciate the thoughtful responses and the time you take to provide such great feedback!!!! Thank you!!!!

OK, so I read over the last response, and I love that you pointed out things know, but haven’t quite embodied. I guess that’s my primary job right now – integrate belief and certainty into every cell of my body .

Thank you for pointing out that 5 years isn’t too late or overdue. When I step back and look at it objectively, a couple of years was training and I haven’t yet jumped in completely so yes, 5, only part-time years isn’t a death sentence for my business .

I appreciate you getting me to look back at what I have done. It’s always so much easier for me to recognize the accomplishments of others and downplay how far I have come myself.

If I look back, I have done a few of the things…posting on social, sending emails out to a small list, running a few workshops, telling people I’m a coach, but NONE of it consistently. I’ve had the tendency to put my business on the back burner when other life things pop up and require my time and focus. Yes, usually the other things actually do require time and focus, but it’s also that I KNOW I can do those things and do them well. It’s easy to see why I’d lean into the things I can do well with little or no failure.

You asked some great questions about where to find clients and what to say/do to get them to want to work with me. Again, I appreciate you highlighting the need to acknowledge that I am in the process and going in the right direction.

Here’s where I’m at as far as who I want to help:

After flip-flopping and not wanting to niche down, for right now, I am focusing on mid-life women. A few years ago, I had this idea for the concept of The Evolving Nest. My idea was focused on the need for women to have support through the years as their kids are growing up, and still in the house. The other aspect is that we are never really empty, in my opinion, always evolving no matter where your kids live, how old they are, etc.

My thinking on what I help these women potentially falls into 4 main buckets: 1- physical wellbeing

2- thoughts/emotional intelligence

3- External world – relationships, home environment, etc.

4- Time, energy and purpose

I don’t know how clear I’ve been with who I’m hoping to serve and why. I have been trying to focus more on these 4 buckets lately in my posts, etc.,

**ANSWER:**

Hello Coach!

I also agree with you on the value of 2K, and thank you for sharing your appreciation. We are all here dedicated to supporting each student and coach, and it means a lot that you shared that.

You’ve done some very honest reflection here, and what stands out most is that you can see clearly now that it wasn’t the five years that determined your results, it was consistency. That awareness is powerful, because it shows you that nothing has gone wrong. You weren’t failing. You were engaging with your business in a way that felt safer for your brain. It makes sense that you leaned toward the things you knew you could do well and away from the areas where there was uncertainty or possible failure. That’s what every human brain does. Its job is to keep you in what feels familiar.

Now the work is not to figure out if you’re capable. You’ve already shown that you are. The work now is to manage your mind when it wants to put your business on the back burner, and deliberately bring your focus back. This is the shift from being interested in your business to becoming committed to it.

Wo do you need to become to take it so very seriously?

I also want you to know that your idea, The Evolving Nest, is very clear. You’ve put thoughtful attention into who you want to help and what they need. Those four areas you described show that you understand their experience. So instead of questioning whether it’s clear enough, you get clear the faster you to begin using it consistently

Let’s look at this from the perspective of creating the energy of a 2K coach today.

Who do you need to be today to help just one person?

I’m asking this because businesses are built by helping people, not by waiting until you feel fully certain. When you focus on helping one person, your brain moves out of doubt and into service.

What do you already have to give, with what you know and who you are right now?

This matters because your business grows by using what you already know. You don’t gain confidence first and then act. Confidence is created through acting.

How are you different when you are thinking about helping and serving someone, versus when you are thinking about yourself and whether you’re clear enough?

This helps you see that doubt turns your focus inward, while service turns your focus outward. One keeps you stuck. The other creates movement.

What do you know that could help a midlife woman who is in her own evolving nest right now?

And what experience do you want to gain today from your actions?

I’m asking this because your actions are what create your belief. Every time you show up, speak, offer, or help someone, you are building evidence for your brain that this is who you are.

When you focus on her what she needs, what she is feeling, and how you can help you naturally step into service. And service is what builds a business. Not perfection. Not certainty. Service.

So when you think about the women you want to help, how do you see yourself meeting them, serving them, and making offers to help them today?

This is how your business grows. One person. One conversation. One act of service at a time! 2k energy today!

## acc continued – identity

**Date:** 2026-02-10  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

Hi Coach,

Thank you — this helped me see something more clearly. I want to clarify one piece, because I don’t feel that I soften my message. I’m clear in what I say, what I sell, and how I help. Where avoidance shows up for me is more in certain actions, not in the messaging itself.

Specifically, I notice avoidance around:

Intentional engagement (starting conversations rather than waiting)

Posting stories to actively sell

Reaching out or following up when there’s been no response

Repeating offers when there’s been little or no buying

What comes up for me emotionally here is less about fear of being “too much,” and more about frustration and discouragement that it hasn’t happened yet. When people don’t respond or buy, my body interprets that as “this still isn’t working,” and I can feel deflated rather than motivated.

When I think about rejection, it doesn’t look like someone saying something negative to me. It looks like:

Silence after an offer

No reply to a follow-up

No conversion after consistent visibility

That silence is what my brain reacts to. It feels frustrating and draining, and I can see how it leads me to pull back from the more direct actions that would actually create momentum.

In terms of nervous system overload, for me this shows up as:

A tight, frustrated feeling in my body

Mental looping around “why isn’t this happening yet?”

Loss of energy for initiating conversations

Wanting to step away from selling actions rather than lean into them

So if I were to model this:

C: Increase offer frequency

T: It’s frustrating to keep offering when nothing is converting

F: Frustrated / discouraged

A: Avoid intentional engagement, avoid selling in stories, and reduce proactive follow-up

R: Fewer direct selling actions, fewer opportunities for buyers, reinforcing the frustration

I can see how my nervous system anticipates more frustration rather than fear, and avoids actions that feel like they could amplify that feeling. I’d love coaching on how to keep taking these direct actions from leadership even when frustration is present, instead of waiting for it to resolve first.

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach,

Let’s look into your model and what you shared:

You said:
 “So if I were to model this:”

C: Increase offer frequency

T: It’s frustrating to keep offering when nothing is converting

F: Frustrated / discouraged

A: Avoid intentional engagement, avoid selling in stories, and reduce proactive follow-up

R: Fewer direct selling actions, fewer opportunities for buyers, reinforcing the frustration

I can see how my nervous system anticipates more frustration rather than fear, and avoids actions that feel like they could amplify that feeling. I’d love coaching on how to keep taking these direct actions from leadership even when frustration is present, instead of waiting for it to resolve first.

AAC: 
What you’re noticing is important: your nervous system isn’t avoiding fear, it’s avoiding the 
anticipation of more frustration
. That means the work here isn’t confidence or courage, it’s building your tolerance for delayed reward. This is a nervous-system skill. Leadership asks you to keep placing clear invitations into the world 
without
 
emotional confirmation. The payoff sometimes comes later; but your capacity is being built now. Think about how well this will serve you long-term… The higher your capacity, the more success you can tolerate and hold.

This is also where anchoring into your identity as a leader matters more than outcome. When you lead from 
who you are
 rather than what’s converting or when, you build self trust. 
“This is who I am when I lead…I engage, I follow up, I repeat offers.”
 
Frustration doesn’t get to decide whether you act; it simply gets to be present while you do. In fact, frustration becomes the cue to simplify, stay visible, and keep initiating – not to pull back. You’re not waiting for the feeling to resolve first, you’re letting your actions teach your nervous system that you can lead yourself even when results are quiet.

What do you think? Feel free to come back with any coaching you may still need here.

## identity cont…

**Date:** 2026-02-04  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

1. How might I build belief before I’ve obtained the results?

I can build belief by creating evidence intentionally rather than waiting for results to validate me. That looks like following through on my standards (posting, selling, following up) regardless of engagement, tracking non-monetary wins such as DMs, replies, warm leads, and reflecting on past client results and feedback. I also see belief as something I practice through identity-based action — showing up as the version of me who signs 5 clients before the 5 clients arrive.

I’ve also noticed that avoidance shows up when belief wobbles. Not avoidance of showing up, but avoidance of fully owning leadership — particularly in selling. When results feel quiet, I can default to safer actions (posting, refining, consuming) instead of bolder actions that build momentum. Naming this helps me build belief first so action feels safer, rather than forcing action from doubt.

2. How can I build my audience more?

Building my audience requires more intentional connection beyond my current bubble. Practically, this means committing to daily relationship-building (commenting, starting conversations), more proactive DM outreach that is human rather than transactional, and prioritising collaborations such as lives, podcasts, and community spaces. It also means increasing discoverability through clearer hooks, shareable messaging, and being more present in places where my ideal clients already gather, including Facebook groups and LinkedIn.

3. With the audience I already have, what’s keeping me from making more offers?

What comes up is fear of being repetitive, being rejected, or interpreting low engagement as disinterest. When belief dips, offers can subtly shift into a softer, permission-based energy. I want to practice making offers from certainty rather than response-based feedback, viewing consistent selling as a service that builds awareness. Avoidance here is a signal to recommit to conviction, not to change strategy.

Where I’d like additional coaching

I’d love support around strengthening belief-led selling, reducing avoidance around bold action, increasing offer frequency without nervous system overload, and maintaining leadership even when engagement is quiet.

**ANSWER:**

Hi Coach,

Thanks for bringing your answers back. Let’s dig in to a couple things you shared and coach more deeply. In reading a few of your AAC coaching responses and submissions, a common theme is that your mind wants to look at the metrics/data/engagement and use it as the foundation of belief for your leadership. What’s interesting is that you can clearly list out the “thoughts” that would help you ground more confidently in your leadership and how you show up, but maybe don’t 
(yet)
 feel full belief around those thoughts you’ve come up with. We can address those in the other submission.

For this submission, it looks like there are some emotions that arise when it comes to making more offers.

So for this AAC submission, let’s address that.

You asked for some coaching on
: strengthening belief-led selling, reducing avoidance around bold action, increasing offer frequency without nervous system overload, and maintaining leadership even when engagement is quiet.

And you mentioned what keeps you from making more offers is: 
“
Fear
 of being repetitive, being 
rejected,
 or interpreting low engagement as 
disinterest 
and it shifts you into more permission-based energy vs. leadership energy.”

Our brain is so protective of us that it can often logically know what we need to do but struggle to embody the practices to do it because of the emotions we will have to feel along the way. What if stepping into your leadership even more boldly means being 
fully okay
 with being repetitive and letting your body feel the fear of it while you repeat your message over and over? Can you give yourself permission to feel the fear but show up that way anyways to continuing speaking to your ideal client?

Also, what would “being rejected” look like? How does your brain think it will happen/play out? 
Identifying this more specifically may show you where/why you hold yourself back from the embodied leadership you know you’re capable of deep within. This is another area your mind is wanting to protect you from – rejection. Which means to strengthen your leadership, you may need to put yourself in the position more often where rejection is possible. It doesn’t mean it may actually happen, but it means you would need to develop the skills emotionally to trust that you can feel rejection 
and still know
 you’ll be okay to keep taking action powerfully despite/in spite of this emotion being present.

In addition if helpful – Remember with emotions, they are fluid.. Not permanent. The emotions that feel unpleasant to us often put us in certain states of inaction. A big piece of our work as leaders and entrepreneurs are to let the emotions be there knowing they will come and go if we don’t make them mean a bunch of things. Emotions are just responses to our conscious and subconscious thought-patterns that tend to be super protective so that we feel “good” consistently.

Notice also how your brain makes “low engagement mean disinterest”. In your other submission you listed several intentional thoughts to combat believing “low engagement = disinterest”. Those were powerful and we can address them in the other submission thread. But something worth asking yourself is, what feels most challenging about fully believing those “leadership-level” thoughts right now?

When you mention,
 “increasing offer frequency without nervous system overload.”

Share what nervous system overload looks/feels like to you. How would “nervous system overload” show up do you think?

This is important to get clear on because if your brain already anticipates that increasing the frequency may “shake up” your nervous system, it could be a sneaky way it avoids growing into the level of self-leadership you desire as well.

A model might look something like:

C: Increase Offer Frequency

T: I don’t want my nervous system to feel overloaded

F: ___?_____

A: ___?_____List out all actions you’d take/avoid based on the feeling you choose.

R: Don’t change offer pace/frequency.

## IDENTITY #30DAYSCHALLENGEMYOWN83.

**Date:** 2025-11-07  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

The hardest for me has been changing my identity.

I come from the academic world, with a very academic mother (2 majors + 1 big certification) and an academic father (with a Master degree and who worked in universities until he passed).

My mom always said people who did not study where “so uneducated” (in a non very empathetic way).

I happily pursued a PhD and even a Post-PhD. I love research and I loved teaching… but what I loved most was teaching about business, marketing and money. I loved people always said I inspired them to go for more; that I changed their perspectives totally.

Many years ago I decided to start my own business. BUT THE “I´m not an entrepreneur, nor is my family” thought has been always with me. Even though in some occasions I have had some good results and people see me as a role model, my identity is still in the academic side. (I know a lot!!)

So, I keep studying. There is always something missing. Something I have to learn in order to serve.

I am always in need of the new information. I need to know it. (otherwise I will be so uneducated).

I admire the most those women in 2kx2k who where doctors, or high level employees and were able to get rid of that old idea about themselves.

I need the correct method to finally leave that old identity behind. YES! BEHIND. It does not serve me anymore. (And I am not going back to work at any university ever).

**ANSWER:**

You have some great beliefs passed on by your parents – that belief that people can always learn. That belief that learning and growth is a worthwhile thing to do.

Is it possible to learn and grow in ways other than academics? What do you think?

And what are some reasons people might not pursue university but might want to learn in other ways? Can you think of some reasons people don’t do higher education?

Are those people less “good”? What do you think?

This is how we start to shape our own identity. By questioning those thoughts that we’ve just always thought. By getting curios about it and looking at it in new ways.

## 100k identity – T = ‘I am doing it wrong”

**Date:** 2025-11-03  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

Hi there,

I am working on my 100k identity and have realised I am having this habitual thought: ‘I am doing it wrong’ (model below and as you can see i think it applies to wider life for me too). I’ve condensed a few ‘feelings’ into one model I hope thats ok.

C – my biz, tasks, parenting, training and instagram

T – I am doing it wrong

F – insecurity / lack /wrongness/shame

A – focus on: what i dont know yet. What I have not got results in

A – use the word ‘need’ and ‘should’ and ‘have to’ around everything esp to-dos (and then cycle through a new crappy model for those T’s)

A – Imagine all the futures where it doesnt work out and run shame spirals

A – look at all I havent yet done

A – tell myself BOTH of these at the same time and then freeze: ‘I have to do ALL the ideas’ and ‘I cant possibly do all the ideas at once;

A- forget I WANT to be here

A – focus on ‘me me me’ -> want and need to feel significant then and forget about love, connection, serving and others

A – try to push through and act from insecurity and feel drained and exhausted by it all

A – avoidance, busywork

R – dont get it right (or see where I do) and so re-ammo this original thought with more power (repeat cycle)

As a response to this, I’ve written a few alternate words I can try to use instead of need/should/have to. That feels good.

I also thought to build this T to bridge the gap away from this when I ‘catch myself in the act’ -> T – I don’t get anywhere by thinking ‘I’m doing it wrong’ so I no longer indulge in this thought

Today I’m feeling good. Seeing this model (and I dont know what else). and so looking over this I am like ok – how do I work more on this thought, I feel good today. But I can also see objectively that this is a belief I have indulged in and will likely continue to do from habit. My 100k self doesns’t believe this, she cant have in order to gain 100k afterall.

Help – can you help me spot gaps in my exploration so I can continue to unpick this (or maybe I am on track and am not seeing that (bc of this thought?! oh the irony! haha)

Thank you!

**ANSWER:**

Writing this out and seeing what this model creates is already helping you shift it. You are seeing it’s not helping you. All it does is make you “do it wrong” by preventing you from showing up how you really want to show up.

It’s also not great feedback to give someone (even if that someone is yourself) to just tell them “You are doing it wrong.” It’s so much more useful it someone can get specific and show you what you could shift to create results. For example, maybe if on consults you avoid objections, then someone might give you feedback like “next time get curious about their objection. Ask them more about it.” That helps you actually act on what’s not working and grow from it.

When you see this thought “I’m going it wrong” one response might be to ask it:

What exactly am I doing wrong?

Who is saying I’m doing it wrong?

What do I really think about this?

That could help you let go of thinking this so often. It can help you question it in the moment.

## Money Beliefs? Identity?

**Date:** 2025-10-01  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

I need help building my belief that my coaching is worth the investment.

Currently I contract coach – coaching skills-wise, I am pretty dialed in on creating awareness, helping them design their guardrails etc – I wouldn’t say their transformations are earth shattering or total breakthroughs, but they mostly in general are making small sustainable changes and gaining massive clarity. They show up every week and I consistently coach about 10 people a week. I have been coaching in this capacity for 2 years and have PCC designation.

Out in the “real world” outside of contract coaching, I haven’t really tried to sell, I’ve been hesitant to talk about what I do (niche is music industry professionals and arts groups an it’s been difficult for me to figure out my lane). I have had one official niche client (who kind of flaked on me toward the end of the 6 mo contract)and a few others here and there.

Writing this I see maybe I lack belief in that the work “works”, therefore don’t talk about it.

Where do I start to pull this apart to move forward?

**ANSWER:**

Let’s start by looking at the results you help someone with.

And it’s okay if you aren’t 100% clear on this yet. Let’s use this as a space to get some ideas going. Getting specific can really help you start to see what you do and how you help. It also helps you come up with ways to talk about what you do. We want to get you to that place where you feel proud of the work you do and want to share it with people.

What is your niche struggling with?

And how do you help them – what is the work that you are doing with them and that they are learning to do?

What happens after they work with you – what results do they have or what do they know how to do?

For your contract job – is it the same coaching as your niche? Answer the above questions for your contract work too. We want to see if there is anything we can pull from that and apply to your own coaching practice.

And let’s look at money too. You aren’t sure if your coaching is worth the investment. How much is your coaching? Is there a price you think it for sure would be worth? Or are you hesitating at any price point?

Okay so this is my after, and I want help bridging the gap between the two. When I think of the kind of person I need to be to be a good match for my clients, like someone who REALLY gets them and can help them at a deep level… well in many ways it’s that relentless woman vibe I was telling you about that’s my podcast.

That future version of me that i’d like to be, well she’s really strong. She can do really hard things. She’s really happy and bright, and she’s no worried about what other people think about her as much as I currently am. She’s really good at communicating and helping people. She’s like… even more motivated. She is very focused on helping and serving. she doesn’t hesitate to speak her mind. She’s a lot more honest and upfront in a good way instead of trying to be palatable. I feel like I do that sometimes now. Like, I still want everyone to like me. and I want to prove that I’ve got this and I am not stupid for starting a business.

But this after version of me, she owns it. she doesn’t have shame around how long it took. she is like one million percent better because of it. she is better at asking for help when she needs and wants it instead of pretending everything is fine. She is better at following through on the hardest things, and doing what matters most. She is more committed, and it shows in her smallest daily actions. She’s always working on something, and she is kinder. She is less judgmental of others, and therefore less judgement with herself. She is more regulated emotionally, and can handle hard emotions and doesn’t buffer them away, even if it’s just with more positive thinking. she is aware of her strengths, she’s aware of her weaknesses, and she can be really chill about it. not trying to hide. She’s not trying too hard anymore. As in, not. trying to be perfect, or get it all right, or have people like her, she’s just out there helping people, and joyfully living her life, being herself, and feel more free and having more fun with it all.

I’ve been saying lately “when will I learn!” Because a lot of this, is stuff I struggled with as a dancer and a singer. And now I am facing the same struggles here. I want to drop it for good. This perfectionist, “good girl,” people pleaser part of me that’s honestly really draining. Okay that is all, I’d love your thought’s working on this!

Let’s bridge that gap, but first let’s expand on your after a bit more. Let’s make it more clear because that will help us start to see some of the steps from where you are now to where you want to be.

And when she’s having a hard time being that relentless woman – how does she handle that and get back on track?

I feel like I’ve just been pushing it away and resisting it, in the name of “being in a good energy” but like, I”m realizing this part of me needs to be explored so it can be let go eventually. When I try to go there, like i am right now, it’s super uncomfortable. There’s a lot of shame, like feeling embarrassed that it’s taken me so long to make even $4k, and how on earth am I gonna make the difference I want to make in people’s lives if I’m this bad at the business side. It feels like…. really allowing myself and seeing myself in my weaknesses and not like, covering it up with more execution and just the doing… I’ve done work on myself like this to a point, but never this deep probably because it sucks haha. It’s painful for me to see myself not getting something, because I am smart and usually get things really fast. And I have a lot of natural talent for things I’ve done in the past, but it’s been reallllyy different with this., it’s felt like the hardest I’ve ever purposely done. Like life hands you challenges, I’d had plenty of that, but I picked this one haha, and it’s felt like SO hard. It’s requiring so much patience and humility. Okay that’s my before, I will do my after next.

**ANSWER:**

You want to spend some time getting to know this side of yourself.

You have more than one part of yourself. There is this part of you that does know you can do it and figure it out – that’s why you keep showing up.

Then there is this other part of you too.

“It’s felt like the hardest I’ve ever purposely done.” This is the hardest thing you’ve ever done. It’s really pushing you and your skills and your beliefs about yourself. Usually things come more easily for you. You are used to figuring things out faster than other people – and having a business it’s seeming to you like it’s harder for you than others. Which might be true – it could also be true that usually we just hear success stories. We don’t hear about the years of people “failing” or the people who decide it’s not for them.

Let’s talk more with that part of you.

You are thinking you aren’t enough right now. Are there maybe some reasons that you think this might be harder for you than for some other people? Let’s have a conversation with that side of you.  You feel not smart. Is it okay if you aren’t smart in this particular area? Is it okay if it is harder for you?

What do you want to say to that part of you? Or what does that part of you that’s feeling this way – not smart, not enough, embarrassed, shame – need to hear from someone?

## Entrepreneur identity needs support

**Date:** 2025-06-10  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

Hi coach

I haven’t been asking help for a long time and I make it mean all sorts of (bad) things.

First I want to celebrate my progresses. I’ve been making progress in how I see myself as a coach and as a leader. I’ve got more engagement on my posts and I know people are waiting for me to speak.

I’ve got a new client, (who is dedicating 15% of his very low income to work with me, I am so proud of him and me) Another one is waiting for me to offer a consult (oops, I had forgotten, I’ll do it as soon as I am finished here) and another person told me that she is saving money for coaching. So yay! It feels incredible. I can see I increased my skills and my capacity.

Now, the dirty laundry :

I seem to enter a low cycle. Maybe not but I haven’t been able to do anything for three days (and then I have also been able to get out of my way and still meet people and inspire them).

The thought that I want to give up and that I don’t have what it takes is coming often. I have been using « how to sell life coaching » against me. I think I am not an entrepreneur and I will never be. That I don’t have risk tolerance and I am not prioritizing money. (This last one feel VERY true)

Here is one model :

C : person in a « coaching conversation » asking me about my offer

Me : being surprised, answering « hum I don’t know » and making something up because I don’t remember how long it is and how much it costs

T : You want to earn money and you don’t even know what you are selling

F : Shame

A : entering a low cycle, freezing, fearing contacts with humans, being unable to post, to offer zoom meetings, forgetting to schedule a consult with a client who is waiting for it, looking for flaws, having difficulties to celebrate my wins, judging me as « bad with money », labeling myself as « not made to be an entrepreneur », thinking about giving up, using a super helpful webinar against myself, not being lighthearted, not being in a growth mindset, comparing and criticizing myself, not asking for help and judging me for it.

R : creating more shame and annihilate my chances to master my offer as a real entrepreneur

Please help me get out of there !

**ANSWER:**

What we want to do is focus on what IS working.

That you are meeting people. You were having a conversation with someone about your offer. You are talking about what you do.

Why we want to do that is because when you don’t do that, you end up in this model that you shared where you are judging yourself and feeling worse. And that has you not taking action.

If we are focused on how you ARE doing great. Then we noticed you hesitated in sharing your offer because you weren’t sure of the time or the price. What we might do is say – you know what, that’s fine. You were talking to someone. You were letting people know that you do. Let’s see what we want to try next time.

In these cases you could offer a consult to get them started. Maybe you sometimes adjust the length of the coaching program depending on the clients. You might need a consult to get an idea of how long you want to make the program for that person.

## Self Concept & Changing Identity

**Date:** 2025-05-15  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

I’m working on both my self-concept and changing my identity. I know they are both very big topics and I am working with a Life Coach right now on Self-love. I want to have the identity as a coach who makes $100k in her business. I struggle with stepping into that identity. I know that our thoughts produce our feelings and our feeling produce action and action produces results, but where I get stuck is how do I get started with becoming the person who makes $100k. It doesn’t feel authentic to “think” my way to the identity. Is it taking all the action to work toward the identity, or is it a little, or a lot of both? I’ve made $30k so far in my business over the past 4 years and I’ve just been treating it as a hobby. I also have a full time job, so that’s part of it. But, it’s time for me to step up as CEO and replace my job with my full time business.

**ANSWER:**

Changing your identity is a mix of thoughts and action.

When you think the thoughts, then you take the action, which creates results and helps you believe even more.

It’s this mix of believing ahead of time, but also growing that belief by showing up in a way that creates results that help you believe more and more.

You want to grow into that $100k coach.

What are some of the steps along that path?

## Why am able to embody a “confident coach” identity in my head, and in person , but it disappears when I turn on the camera to make a reel?

**Date:** 2025-04-25  
**Topics:** identity

**QUESTION:**

I think it is because I am trying to gauge how people will accept it? Only I do not do that in person. When I get into a conversation with someone about something I feel strongly about, I speak well and have been told I speak with conviction. People listen. BUT, when I go to make a reel, and say something that has been in my head into the camera, I lose that sense of confidence and “being” and feel like I am trying to be someone else. All the excitement and conviction that was in my head, or in the conversation is gone and I lose my sense of alignment with what I want to say.

My current Model options might be:

T – What will People think when they see this? or I’m going to appear stupid, or even worse, I am going to sound arrogant

F – Nervous, Self-conscious, or maybe shameful /embarrassed

A- I do 25 takes on a reel, and either don’t post it , or I post it and obsess over how many people did or did not watch

My desired model could be:

T – This thought is on my heart for a reason, so give value and the impact is none of my business

F – unattached (not sure if I like that feeling)

A – Do a reel, make the post and let it go

I would like to feel excited, and assured about the content I post. I would like feel like what I say matters, but yet I know the impact cannot be the goal. I have no control over that.

As I write this, the thought came up that I want to be as convincing in my videos as I am in my inner monologues with myself, and I am realizing just now, in real time, that this may be a thought error on my part I didn’t realize was there. If I am trying to be convincing then I am absolutely attached to the impact it is having and that will self-sabotage me every time. Right?

I need help with a Thought that leads to a feeling of confidence, and a sense of serving. I always want to lead with my heart, but somehow when I press record, I lose all sense of that inner wisdom and heart-centered being. I could talk to a room of 1000 people and be completely authentic. But maybe I only think that because Ive never done that.

I need help with a thought that can get me recording reels, without feeling so self conscious that it takes me an hour to record 1 minute of content.

Thank you to the coach reading this ginormous journal entry I just brain-dumped here. 2500 words is unbelievably generous and I am amazingly grateful you are willing to do this. Thank You for any guidance you have.

**ANSWER:**

“I could talk to a room of 1000 people and be completely authentic. But maybe I only think that because Ive never done that.” It’s so much easier in our head. It’s why so many people have big dreams but don’t actually act on them. When it’s in our head there is no vulnerability. We can’t fail. No one can criticize us. It seems easy.

Then you turn on the camera and it actually becomes real.

When you are making a reel –

You are talking to that empty nester woman. That’s who you are talking to. You want to picture talking to her – just you and her having a conversation. It doesn’t need to be perfect. She can hear your message even if it’s not said in the perfect way. You aren’t convincing her of anything. She wants help. What do you want to say to her today? How do you feel when you connect with her and what she’s going through?

## Day 14 / 30 – resistance

**Date:** 2026-04-03  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

Thank you for all of your insights and helping me uncover all of this over the past month. I feel like we hit a peak and things are becoming much clearer for me now. & yes it is absolutely clear that who I was being in WA was what actually supported me in creating the results I had.

I love your suggestions around creating the same results in a different way by receiving coaching here, using the 2k community and live calls on Monday’s as a “mastermind”, etc. I am absolutely going to be following this guidance and I can feel already how it’s positively impacting my progress.

My action steps are clearly placed in front of me now, but I have to be honest – I am feeling resistance around becoming the “who.”

I felt so inspired after realizing who I need to be to get back to that success. I was going through journals from that time period, mostly business related entries talking about things that were inspiring me, and I realized just how many original techniques I created in my client work back then.

I started thinking things like “I need to incorporate these techniques back into my 1:1 offer.” I also spoke with a past client recently who was singing my praises and I mentioned that I’m going to be selling general life coaching and she said, “You’re really going to be selling yourself short if you keep your offer as basic as ‘general life coaching,’ what you do is so much more than that.” & I agreed with her which sort of sent me into a bit of a spiral about my offer and title.

I don’t want to change the duration or the price but it did make me wonder about marketing it. I said I want it to be a year of “general life coaching,” but I’ll be sprinkling in a combination of subconscious work, nervous system work, spiritual work, emotional regulation, etc. That feels like more than “general life coaching,” it feels original. I think I’ve always struggled with my title because of that too. I’ve called myself a life coach, a mentor, a guide, a facilitator, never landing on something that feels “right.”

I don’t know, am I focusing on things that don’t matter?

**ANSWER:**

Hey coach!

You’re welcome for the suggestions. I really do think using this community and the Monday calls will be a powerful way to support your goals and what you want to create.

Two things are coming up for me as I read this:

First, when you said, 
“I need to incorporate these techniques back into my 1:1 offer”
… you absolutely can. If there were techniques you loved using before, you get to bring those back. You can pick and choose what you want to use with clients now. Nothing about your current offer prevents that.

And also
…
 that doesn’t mean you need to box yourself into the “perfect” label right now.

That can actually be a really sneaky way your brain tries to slow things down… waiting until everything feels perfectly defined before taking action that actually moves your business forward. It is completely okay to call yourself a “life coach” and also communicate that you use 
a variety of tools from your coaching toolbox
 
based on 
each client’s unique needs.

At the same time, I would take your past client’s feedback as a compliment to the depth of your skillset,
 but not
 as something that needs to redirect you or create confusion. You’re right not to let this spiral you! A title doesn’t need to carry the full weight of what you do, it just needs to open the door.

Calling yourself a “life coach” still creates an opportunity for you to share how you help.

It might sound something like this:

“What do you do?”
“I’m a life coach.”
“Oh, what does that mean?”
“It means people come to me when they want support in their life. I work with mindset, emotional regulation, nervous system work, spiritual work, etc., and I tailor how I coach based on what each person needs!”

When you market this way, you open the door to a wide range of people who are looking for support. Then inside the coaching, you get to decide which tools you use.

For example, if you had 15 clients as a life coach right now, you might have:

5 who really love the spiritual work

5 who benefit most from mindset and emotional regulation

5 who use a mix of everything you offer

That’s the beauty of what you’re building as a “general life coach”. It doesn’t mean you can’t talk about your different methods or skills, it just means you get to help anyone who comes your way and use your best judgement to decide what each client may need. What some clients need most, other clients of yours may differ a bit… and that’s okay!

The way I think about “general life coaching” is that it allows you to
 invite in all walks of life
 and
 learn from a wide range of challenges they bring. 
Over time, you’ll naturally see what you love using most. Maybe it’s all of it, maybe it’s specific tools with specific people, and maybe eventually, you’ll notice patterns in who you’re drawn to and decide to niche more intentionally.

But right now, your job isn’t to perfect the title. It’s to move your 1-year offer forward confidently and knowing internally what you’re capable of providing each client. You can invite people in, use the techniques you love, and deliver powerful coaching without needing to turn it into a 
perfectly defined brand/title
 just yet.

You know? What are your thoughts?

Such great work you’re doing here for yourself coach. Kudos!

## It’s resistance

**Date:** 2026-02-04  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

This coaching was so insightful. Specifically these areas stood out to my awareness and hit right:

Commit to your decision for more

You’ll need a reason you’ve already decided on

Not to eliminate resistance but to understand why you’re willing to move with it.

Honoring your plan when your brain argues with it.

Plan on those feelings ahead of time (rather than planning in a way that would avoid them)

These statements had me asking questions, in a positive light, reflective, and processing an insight. Thank you for taking the time for such thoughtful responses.

Two things come to mind:

I’m such a flexy person. I can be happy with most options. This is a great quality I love about myself. However, when there is something I want and another person makes a case against it, I feel like to a degree I have to submit.

I’m doing that to myself here – because the feeling is resistance – and my repeated, trained response to it – in these cases anyway – is: that it’s not worth it, I’m a bother, what I want is not important compared to other things going on, and I don’t need it to be happy or satisfied.

I see where this likely started from the past. Someone else was arguing with my plan – and I didn’t honor it – gave in as a result – to make it easier for them. They won. I felt dejected. Then I felt like I had to be independent in order to get what I want. If no one knows, no one can argue it. But that’s not my MO deep inside. (My eyes literally just teared up.) My way of being is a shared experience with others – the opportunity – practice my resolve, practice my resolve, breath in resistance as it comes up – remember what I chose and why. No need to argue or give in. That doesn’t mean I’m not flexible. (Another tear)

Today, I see it more clearly. I will allow this to process as the awareness itself is powerful and moving. With this knowledge, I want to respond to resistance differently. I have the choice. Always.

I already do a great job on this in so many areas – specifically when I want to provide a high quality product or gift to someone. No one can stop me. I pray, slow down a bit, and put my whole heart and guts into it – to the best of my ability at a given time. I’m proud of that work – so I push through and take resistance with me. He’s along for the ride.

This is what I want to practice and bring awareness to today and this week while it sinks in deeper.

**ANSWER:**

One thing I want to reflect back first is how intentionally you’re already using questions  not to find what’s wrong, but to expand .  So often our default lens is “What’s the problem?” and the brain will faithfully answer 
that
 question. When we ask better questions, the brain looks for better answers. That’s not just mindset  it’s training your brain what to look for. Beginning with 
what worked
 or 
what’s also true
 is a powerful evaluation tool, and you’re already practicing it.

What really stood out to me is your awareness around resistance  not as something to eliminate, but something that has meaning. You named this beautifully: the moment resistance shows up, an old, trained response steps in  
it’s not worth it, I’m a bother, what I want isn’t important
. That’s not who you are; that’s a pattern that was learned when honoring your plan felt costly.

So here’s a question I want you to sit with, not to analyze but to orient yourself in the moment:
When resistance shows up, what do you actually need right then? Ask yourself what do I need? 
This question matters because it interrupts the automatic conclusion that resistance means “stop” or “submit.” Instead, it creates space to respond rather than repeat.

You also said something really important  that your natural way of being is shared experience. You don’t want to disappear to protect your desires. You want to 
practice resolve in connection
. That’s not rigidity. That’s self-trust. Flexibility doesn’t mean abandoning yourself; it means choosing consciously rather than reflexively.

I want to offer another reframe using your own words:
What if resistance is the exact moment you get to practice being the version of you you’re becoming?
Not by arguing with yourself. Not by giving in. But by breathing it in, remembering what you chose and why, and staying with yourself.

You already know how to do this. You described it clearly in how you show up when something truly matters to you  when you’re creating, giving, offering something from the heart. In those moments, resistance comes along for the ride, not as a decision-maker but as a passenger.

That’s the practice now. Repetition. Allowing the feeling, interrupting the old pattern, and responding in the way you’d want others to respond to you  with steadiness, respect, and care. Give that to yourself first.

Take your time to Let this awareness sink in deeper. There’s no rush to “do” anything with it. Awareness itself is movement. And when you’re ready, we can keep building from here.

You’re doing meaningful work. Keep going.

Thinking about the clients when you are at home is a sign your mind is trying to figure this out. You want to figure this out.

When we look at what are seeing in them first – then we can start to solve for it. For example, we can start to sort out someone who says “I just don’t have the time.” from a place of really wanting change but needing help vs. someone who really doesn’t want to change at all.

## Resistance

**Date:** 2025-07-16  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

Hello. I previously sent in a question about how to let go of being resistant to things.

The response was, “And tell me why you want to be a coach. Why do you want to get coaching clients?”

I want more than anything to be a coach! I am currently a trauma therapist and love the work that I do but I want to move into coaching for a few reasons. I love the energy of coaching vs. therapy. I have had some clients in therapy that I was able to move into coaching with (at my current company still, not as my own clients) and it was exhilarating. I love the energy of coaching clients. Secondly, I am tired of having my company take 50% of my income. I am the one in the room doing all of the work, day in and day out and they take half. Ugh. I want to be able to work fewer hours and enjoy my daily work more and have more time freedom with more income. I want more time with my husband and more relax time to recharge for clients. I want to do speaking events and have more community outreach. In a previous job, I was a national trainer and traveled the country and I loved it.

All of that is background. Here is where I am stuck –

While I am 1000% certain in my abilities to help people to make profound and lasting changes in their lives (I have been a therapist for more than 11 years), I am resistant to the idea that I can run my own coaching business. I hate business things and marketing and things like that. I don’t have a business brain. I just want to be in the room (or virtual) and be face-to-face with clients and creating transformations together. I absolutely love the face-to-face part. I am resistant to the idea that I can create enough clients to replace my current income. I am resistant to the idea that I can create even one client. I am resistant to the idea that clients will pay me for coaching.

When I am calm and logical, I know that I can be a huge success as a life coach. I just resist creating it. I’ve watched all of the 2K modules and I’m listening to the live coaching and it is helping me to bridge that belief, but man, am I struggling with believing that all of this is possible for ME. Yes, it works for others, but probably not for me. It is dumb to resist what I KNOW that I can be amazing at – helping others transform their lives. And yet, I’m stuck. How do I let go of this resistance? Thanks!!!

**ANSWER:**

When you work for someone else who is bringing in the clients – you can spend most of your day right now 1:1 with clients. There is probably some admin time too, but most of your time will be spent with clients.

When you are building a business, you are going to spend more time marketing and selling than your are right now. It can sometimes take us putting in more hours at first – especially if you need to keep working as many hours as you are as a therapist for financial reasons – in order to build up the coaching business so that you can then work less.

Let’s look at how you might enjoy and even get energy from doing that marketing and selling.

You talked about how exhilarating it feels when you have a coaching client. You love being in that energy and helping people through coaching. Let’s use that. That is going to have you WANTING to show up and feeling energized by showing up if we can learn how to have that same feeling from the marketing/selling part of coaching. That part is really the first part of the work you do with a client – it’s letting them know who you are, how you help, and what’s possible for them. What ideas do you have now to meet those clients and share how you can help? Or what have you been doing already to meet people and make offers?

## How can I overcome my resistance to meeting people in person?

**Date:** 2025-07-14  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

~That there aren’t good places to do it

~That I don’t want my business to be in person

~That I only want to grow it online

I don’t want to go out there. I do want to meet new people, but I believe that’s only possible online.

You don’t have to meet people in person if you don’t want to. You can have a successful business doing it all online.

## Resistance to answering about fast results

**Date:** 2025-07-01  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

I posted this in the ROI FB group and wanted to bring it here too.

“#celebration #coaching I was listening to the most recent 2K call, in which Stacey challenged the person being coached about how she could get results for her clients even more quickly. I could tell the coaching was completely for me also, so I turned off the call and started brainstorming. This moving quickly to taking action is something that I wouldn’t have done two weeks ago. ROI has helped me realize that’s what needed to happen. 🎉

As I’m feeling into the question “If I only had 30 days with my clients, how would I get them results?” I realize I have my own edge here. The one in me who wants to just drift in a container, letting it be flowy, trusting I’ll pick up what I need to, having resistance to structure and intention and targets. And I realize until I solve this in myself, I don’t think I’ll see the easy clear answers for how to accelerate results for my clients.

My mind is going a little fuzzy trying to answer the question.”

I just brainstormed some ideas and things I want them to know and core principles and stuff, but I feel a bit frozen and like I’m confusing myself.

I guess maybe I’m also trying to bite off too much too fast? I’m still in my first year as a coach so learning a lot, maybe not ready to have my own course or methodology.

What if I just choose one thing to try?

An example could be having them do an ROI plan (maybe live on our first call?) or getting clear on one specific intention or issue to work on for each month and continuing to work on it deliberately.

I still feel aversion and resistance to “forcing” so much structure though.

Thanks for you thoughts!

**ANSWER:**

Taking into account the last coaching you came for, where we saw that you’ve had this big growth and you are adjusting to that. You want to keep going, keep creating more results faster AND you don’t have to do it all TODAY. There is this balance between putting things off – for example letting results take a year and then rushing them.

In the coaching about how to get clients results faster – you want to use that to improve your work with clients, but you also don’t have to rush your clients. You are starting out and learning a TON. Let’s imagine that you take what you brainstormed and decided to improve the results your clients get by 10%. What if they got results 10% faster and you took action and implemented that first. What would that look like? It might be more structure, but not overly structured to still allow for you and your clients to learn and grow at the pace you need. Not a pace where you are taking too long and putting results off – but not from a rushed energy either.
