## Resistance towards learning a consult

**Date:** 2025-05-21  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

I have noticed that I’m experiencing resistance towards learning the consult anatomy. I’ve gained a tremendous value in Stacey’s program, and I was in agreement with almost everything she has taught us so far, until consults. Consults suggest that I need to learn a completely new approach of doing a consult than the way I’ve been doing it. I’ve been doing consults for 6 years with a fair level of success. I didn’t crunch the numbers, but my rough estimate is that 90% of my training clients buy and renew for years to come, while that number is a bit lower for my health and nutrition coaching clients, my guess is closing rate is in the 60%. This is why I bought 2k for 2k, to increase my health coaching closing rate.

Now I know that consult is a valuable asset and a learning tool, but I have a problem when someone tells me “you HAVE to do this this way” because of all the wasted time in the past on doing what other coaches told me it was a smart thing to do. Because the answer was not in the niche, social media, or messaging that I’ve spent years clarifying (this has never increased my sales btw). The answer was always believing in my offer, my client and myself, I see this clearly now.

I do a consult as a very brief get together, to decide if we’re a fit or not, what is their goal and if I can help them reach it, I tell them how we do it, and what is expected from them. I don’t spend much time on their wellness vision that lives on the other side of the gap because that’s something we do inside my coaching program. I also don’t talk about their objections or overcoming them because I never ask for a close right there on the call nor ask them to pay right away. Instead, if they are a yes, I send them an email with onboarding documents and my payment information, and they do it in their own time. If they’re not sure or are talking to other coaches, I schedule a follow up to give them time to decide, and in the meantime I send them an email that summarizes my offer and pricing options. This has also worked well in the past, so I never thought my consults were the problem.

**ANSWER:**

You always get to take what you want from this program and leave the rest.

You don’t have to use the consult process if you don’t want to.

In your case, instead of trying to re-vamp how you do consults entirely, we could look at a few parts of the consult process and decide if you want to try them out.

Most of your consult process right now is similar to 2k. You talk about where they are now, you help them come up with a plan.

One of the areas that can be really helpful for clients is the overcoming objections part of the consult. This isn’t about “overcoming an objection” and getting a yes – it’s about giving the client a space to think about their hesitations and what’s coming up for them.

You might ask an open question like:

How are you feeling about everything we’ve talked about?

Or Do you have any hesitations coming up or questions I can help you with before we get off this call?

This gives them a chance to say things like – you know I really want to lose weight, but I’ve tried diets in the past and they never worked. I could never stick to them.  That allows them to be open with you and have a chance to talk about this with you, vs getting off the call and having to do it on their own with no support.

Do you already do something like this in your consults?

## Resistance to consistency

**Date:** 2025-05-02  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

Hi there! Going through the first Module “Organic Marketing” I noticed a strong body reaction (tightness, fear) towards the statements of Stacey “I am a consisent well of value” and “It is consistency above all else – this is what creates safety”.

There is this fear of once I start giving value, people will have this expectation of me to keep bringing value and that I will feel pressured. I am also not trusting myself that I will actually do the work do keep up the consistency (fear of failure). Thus, I better not start at all..

Here is a Model that I just did on this:

C: Start sharing valuable content in Social Media Posts

T: I will feel pressured to do it all of the time.

F: resistance

A: entertaining the idea of publishing content on Social Media, but not doing it, not start collecting ideas for content, not creating or publishing any content

R: creating pressure inside of me and disappointment for myself already now because I am not doing what I am entertaining in my mind

I can see how I am already feeling the pressure now. But my brain thinks it is still better not to start being the “well of consistent value” because this would mean I would have to do hard work (thinking, creating, writing, composing etc.).

What is coming to my mind now: I guess I have the choice between – being either consistent in disappointing myself and not going for what I desire and be exhausted from that inner conflict of “wanting to do it ” and “not doing it”. Or being consistent and yes, sometimes maybe also be exhausted from doing the actual work taking me closer towards my dream.

I would be curious to hear your perspective on this share.

**ANSWER:**

You are thinking that being consistent means doing hard work. That in order to be consistent you’ll have to do lots of hard inner work. And that puts lots of pressure on it.

What if being consistent could be posting on social media once a week? Sometimes more when you want to, but consistency could look like always once a week.

If you think about posting once a week on social media, how do you feel about that? Could you post once today or tomorrow and then schedule in a time to post again next week?

We want to start where you are at. That’s how you build consistency. It starts with figuring out how to post once a week and have that be easy. Then you start to see how you could post more often.

## Resistance to look at coaching successes

**Date:** 2025-01-13  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

I just realised that I have a lot of resistance to write down my own successes with coaching and the successes my clients had, even though that’s what I came up with to strengthen my conviction that coaching works.

When I asked myself why I have this resistance, what came up is fear of realising, that coaching doesn’t work – or at least not significantly and especially realising that I’m not as good as a coach as I hope to be.

Any input o this?

**ANSWER:**

Let’s try looking at this a different way.

I find it useful to look at what coaching does help with. How it helps.

And also sometimes what it doesn’t help with. What are the limitations of the type of coaching you do.

For example,

Coaching on relationships can help someone understand their communication patterns with their partner. It can help them know what’s happening, break it down, and be able to start to choose how they want to show up. They can start to see that they are only part of the relationship – and that they can influence the dynamic by how they show up.

But coaching can’t make someone get back together with someone if they don’t want to. It can give the relationship the best possible chance – but coaching can’t change someone who doesn’t want to change.

In the coaching you’ve had yourself, or the coaching you’ve done with others – what has the coaching done? How has it been useful?

You can also bring back the doubts that come up too about coaching working. Exploring that can be useful sometimes.

You get to post whatever you want.

You can post about politics if you want to. But you also don’t have to. You can post about what you normally post about.

And – it’s also okay to take a break. You can take a week off. Your business will still be there. Your business is working for you even when you need some time off.

## Resistance to Meeting People

**Date:** 2024-07-11  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

The question that I’m working on today is “who do I need to be to meet people?”

I made it through the organic marketing modules and I’m trying to piece it all together for myself. If I can think some really high quality thoughts about myself as a coach that inspire feelings like maybe confidence, that will inspire me to get out there and meet people? Where I’m beginning right now in my unintentional model is “I don’t know where to meet her,” which is creating confusion for me, and causing inaction, result I don’t meet anyone. I’m also thinking some “I don’t want to go searching for her” which is I think the thought causing the resistance. Actions and results are similar, inaction and I don’t meet anyone. Thank you!

**ANSWER:**

Let’s ask this question:

What are some ways I could let people know what I do and who I help?

The goal right now is to start letting people know what you do and who you help. It’s okay if you aren’t sure how exactly to meet your ideal clients, right now we want to start taking action and letting people know what you do.

What are some ways you have or can let people know what you do?

It might be finding a networking event.

Or posting on your social media about what you do.

It could be reaching out to people you already know. For example, if you are a coach for parents you might ask your daycare if you can put up a poster or send out an email to the daycare parents.

What else?

Who would you need to be to take those actions?

It might be willing to be unsure and take action anyway.

It might be willing to try something out even if you aren’t 100% sure it will result in a client.

It might be KNOWING people want your offer – and that if you let enough people know what you do then you will sign clients.

What do you think you’d need to think in order to spend 15 minutes taking action right now on one of those ideas?

## Resistance to Using the Life Wheel

**Date:** 2024-06-13  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

I can’t make sense out of it

I’m not sure how to tie it together to their problem and offer them a solution

I want to just ask questions and not build models or do life wheels

I’ve done two life wheels but couldn’t really figure out what the point was. I know Stacey says it helps you identify patterns of thoughts but I didn’t see any.

Any suggestions

**ANSWER:**

In the Consultation Code, (along the top of the page click on the drop down for “2k Process,” then go to Workbooks), there are two options for the first part of the consult. You can use the Life Wheel or you can use the Before/After Process. The Before/After Process is more question based.

When you say you don’t know the point of the life wheel, what do you mean specifically? Bring one of those life wheels that you did and your thoughts about each part so we can have an example to work from. That’s going to make this more clear and easier for your mind to understand.

The first part of the consult, where we do the life wheel, is about gathering information. We want to know how the client is feeling about each area of their life and we want to know what thoughts/feelings they are having.

There is a theme in your questions today of thinking you should just know how to do things – you should just know how to coach, you should just know how to do models, you should just know how to tie together their problem and offer them a solution. ## Resistance around selling my high ticket offer

**Date:** 2024-06-10  
**Topics:** resistance

**QUESTION:**

C: i have 2 tiers of my 1:1 coaching services: 3 months for $3k and 6 months for $5k – main difference is guaranteed results with $5k package (I’m a career and business coach for women); I have 20 hours each week to spend on my business

T: it’s easier to sell my $3k package b/c it’s more affordable and accessible; so many clients have said yes to the $3k but no one has said yes to the $5k yet; it’s harder to sell my $5k package b/c not as many people can afford it; I’d rather make some money than no money; if I only enroll $3k clients, I’ll need 34 clients to make $100K; I’ll only need 20 clients at $5k to make $100k – the second path sounds better in theory but I’m frozen in action

F: FEAR: afraid of rejection; afraid to not make my financial goals; afraid I’ll get more ‘nos’ at $5k and won’t be able to attract enough consults to convert 20 people; afraid my network will say I’m too expensive and word will spread (most of my current clients are people from my former job)

A: FROZEN; not putting as much messaging out there / creating content; not booking consults; giving people 2 options on consults ($3k or $5k) – and feeling more confident selling the $3k

R: not getting many clients lately; those I have signed have been at $3k; questioning if I can even make this math work…

please help me snap out of my funk. ack!!

**ANSWER:**

There are two ways to coach on this. You can coach yourself to believe in the value of your $5k offer and timeline and overcome your fear of offering it, or you can coach yourself to fully embrace your $3k offer and go all in on selling and believing in that offer. Which do you want to do? There’s no right or wrong, because both offers can be successful.

It sounds like you are offering two options because you want $5k instead of $3k, and you think they need 6 months to get better results…but you’re offering $3k to get them in the door because you don’t believe they will pay $5k.

The reason they aren’t signing up for your 5k offer is because you don’t believe they will. You think they won’t want it, can’t afford it and will think it’s too expensive. This is what’s making it harder to sell.

It’s true some people will have those thoughts. But right now you’re focusing on 
those
 people while trying to sell your higher offer.

Tell me about the people who 
will
 want the higher offer? Who are they? Why will they 
want
 6 months with you? Why will they want the guaranteed results? Why will they think $5k is worth those results?

What is the amount of time you truly believe your clients need to get the best results? Can they get them in 3? Why or why not? Do they actually need 6? Why or why not?

What results do you feel confident you can offer in 3 months?

The reason you’re selling more $3k offers is because you believe more people want it and will pay it. Because of your thoughts, you have much more comfort in offering it and your clients feel more comfort in buying it. For now, would it be a better fit for you to only offer 3 month packages with options to renew and keep going to create more results? Why or why not?

For now, which offer would help you get out there and sell and help people?

## Working on a play when out of belief

**Date:** 2026-05-05  
**Topics:** belief

**QUESTION:**

I was in an inspired state of possibility to create an online course and corresponding membership. I really believed it was possible and was enjoying the creative process. I told a business peer about my idea and they told me the “reality” that it would likely be hard to sell and to not get my hopes up. This pulled me right out of belief and I’ve had a hard time reengaging the original belief I have. I am coaching myself through that, but here’s my question.

I crafted a plan while I was. inspired. I had already taken the first steps. Should I continue to work the plan knowing I created it while I was inspired – kind of like delegating those tasks to the unbelieving work bee version of myself. Or should I wait until I’ve coached myself back into consistent belief?

Thank you!

**ANSWER:**

Hello Coach!

I actually think you’re asking a really powerful question here, and the fact that you can see the difference between your “inspired creator” and your “unbelieving executor” tells me you have a lot more awareness than you’re giving yourself credit for.

Let’s look at what happened first. You were in clean belief, creating, moving, enjoying the process. Then someone offered you
 THEIR VERSION of reality,
 and your brain grabbed onto it. It doesn’t mean their version is true, but it felt safer than staying all the way in possibility. That also doesn’t mean your original belief was fragile or wrong. 
It just means your brain is well practiced at protecting you from disappointment
. So now the work isn’t just “get back into belief.”

It’s deciding which voice you actually want to lead from.

About your plan. Why would a plan created in belief suddenly become less valid just because your emotional state shifted? The plan didn’t change. Your relationship to it did. So the question becomes, how do you want to relate to that plan now? Do you want to treat it like something that only works when you feel fully behind it, or something you trust enough to continue even when your brain is a little louder?

You don’t need perfect belief to take action. But it is worth asking yourself 
what kind of energy you want to bring to that action.
 Can you continue executing the plan while also gently rebuilding belief alongside it? Can both exist? Or does it feel more honest for you to pause and recalibrate so you’re not building from doubt? 
There isn’t one right way here
. I’m more interested in what feels grounded and self-led for you!

One more thing to consider: When you think about that moment with your peer, what did you make their opinion mean about you and your ability to sell this? That’s likely the exact place your work is. Not the plan, not the timing, but the meaning you assigned.

Feel free to come back with any of your thoughts or what you may still want some additional coaching on. We are happy to help!

## Day 10 / 30 – catch-22 & building belief that my ideal clients also want me

**Date:** 2026-03-27  
**Topics:** belief

**QUESTION:**

In response to coaching around “who has $700/month just laying around,” I can absolutely see that I have been projecting my own scarcity onto my potential clients. But when I start contemplating calling in clients who are in a different financial situation I think to myself, “why would they want to hire me?” That’s the wall I keep bumping up against. I’m having a hard time selling myself on a different result without my situation changing. But it’s always the catch-22 of me thinking my situation needs to change in order to attract clients while also knowing that I need to sign clients in order to change my situation.

But a woman who easily invests $700/month believes she will get an ROI because she trusts her coach and also because of her own commitment to the work. I AM willing to sell to her but I know it will all be for nothing if I can’t change my thought about how “she won’t want to hire me.” Sometimes the embodiment piece really confuses me. Like I have to pretend to be somewhere that I’m not yet.

& from a separate message around feeling doubt about my offer: I noticed that when I was charging lower prices, I lost motivation to sell. Charging $8k/year makes me feel motivated again. So, I am willing to stand behind my decision and built demand there from that emotional stand point alone.

I also loved the question: “do I fully believe this is worth it for the woman I want to work with?” I challenged myself further to answer the question: “what about my offer makes it worth it for the woman I want to work with?” I am willing to let people walk away if they aren’t aligned with my price and to hold that price confidently even when it’s uncomfortable, and I DO genuinely believe that these women exist – I just need to work on believing that they want to work WITH ME, and that they will be served by working with me. I don’t think I can answer my own question, “what about my offer makes it worth it to them,”

without first working on that belief. Or do I have it backwards?

Do you have any suggestions on how I can work on this?

**ANSWER:**

Hi again coach!

So when you say,
 “why would they want to hire me?”

I think this is stemming a bit from your previous submission where you believe you “peaked” in 2020-2023 and you don’t truly see and believe in the value you bring to the table 
right now.
 Your brain is subconsciously believing that because you aren’t living the way you were in 2020-2023, that somehow it has removed your 
“credibility, credentials, value, etc.”

Remember that although it can be inspiring to see how our coaches live – it’s never
 really
 about the house, the cars, the travel, their money, etc. When we hop on a phone call or zoom call with our coach, they aren’t touring us around their home, their yard, screen-sharing their bank account to show us the numbers, or boasting to us on that call about everything they have….

They are helping coach OUR brain. They helping US feel seen. They are holding space for US to feel and process raw, uncomfortable emotions.

The truth is… your current circumstances have 
nothing to do
 with who you are capable of helping. You just have to start practicing belief around that. Your value isn’t lacking because of your current situation. Your value comes from your experience, the lessons you’ve learned, your humanness, your relatability, the fact you walk the walk of the work – not just “talk the talk”. Your value comes from the fact you have had 
(and continue to have)
 clients and you actually DO know what you are doing as a coach.

You just have to really start trusting that. Clients don’t stay or renew when they feel a lack of value. The fact you’ve kept clients 
(even through tough seasons)
 is very telling of 
who you are as a coach
 for your people.

Coaches are in a pretty awesome role because 
no matter what our personal circumstances are
 at any given moment,
 our #1 job is to help someone else with their own.
 
When we are coaching, it’s not about “us” or where we live, what we have or don’t have,etc. This helps take the pressure of our own circumstances away. Coaching is about being a clean, neutral space for 
someone else
 to see their own mind and feel safe with us. We help them change many areas of their life they are struggling in….

You can do that… You do DO that already.

Nothing has changed in your skill — other than you’re actually equipped with more experience now than in 2020-2023 because with time also comes WISDOM.

Your address is just 
temporarily
 different. Your financial status is 
temporarily
 different.

But none of your clients are going to interview you on that when they get on a zoom.

They just want help for themselves – can you trust yourself to help them?

To work on this embodiment piece further, try working on these questions:

1. Why are you the BEST coach for the women who find you?

2. What do you feel confident in as their coach? As in, what qualities do you trust within yourself to help others?

---

## Clarity and Belief Doubts

**Date:** 2026-03-26  
**Topics:** belief

**QUESTION:**

While at a networking event recently, I noticed my thoughts when it came time to share what I do:

T – These people do not understand my offer, nor would they pay for it.

I have concerns about both of those thoughts because if they are not clear, then how will they know to refer anyone to me, and if I do not believe they (or perhaps anyone) would pay for my offer, then it makes me doubt what I’m doing.

So, I’m doubting my clarity and my belief in others.

I also feel a bit self-conscious about how I look or how I must appear in the eyes of others. I feel I tend to look insecure, but I do not know if that’s how others perceive me.

Ironically, it didn’t stop me from connecting, nor did it stop a well-established business owner from excitedly saying she wanted to connect with me to talk about a potential collaboration or at least meet. (And has already sent a DM inviting me to lunch.)

I would love to explore how I could shift this thinking and my beliefs.

Thank you!!

**ANSWER:**

Hey coach,

First, there’s so much here that’s working, and I want you to really see that. You went to the event, you noticed your thoughts in real time, you still connected, and someone with an established business not only engaged but followed up and wants to meet. That matters, because it gives us real evidence that your thoughts are not the full story. Your brain is offering a narrative, but your results are already showing you something more expansive is also true. You also had some thoughts positive thoughts that created positive results at the event.

Let’s start with the thought:T-  “These people don’t understand my offer, nor would they pay for it.” When you think that, what do you feel? I’m asking because the feeling that comes from that thought is what actually drives how you show up. Is it doubt, hesitation, maybe a kind of holding back before you even begin? And from that feeling, how do you respond? Do you fully explain what you do and allow space for them to understand, or do you simplify or pull back?

Do you experiment with different ways of saying it, or do you stay in something that feels safer?

This matters because the result, people not understanding or not seeing the value, is often something we unintentionally reinforce when we’re believing that thought. Not because it’s true, but because it shapes how we show up.

Instead of trying to fix the thought right away, I want to offer a different way to look at networking.

What if it’s not about whether they understand or would pay right now? What if it’s a space where you get to practice becoming the coach who can create understanding?

I think of networking as a place to meet and have real conversations as the coach you want to be.  A place to try on the identity of the coach you want to be. So rather than asking, “Do they get it?” you might play with, “How do I want to show up explaining what I do today?” or “What would it look like to help just one person understand this a little more clearly?”  The more you practice being the coach who can explain coaching, you will be ready when a client ready asks what you do.

I also want to touch on what you shared about feeling self-conscious and thinking you might look insecure. That awareness is powerful, and I want to gently explore it with you.

When you think “I look insecure,” how do you actually behave? Do you make less eye contact, rush your words, or second-guess yourself mid-sentence? I ask because what we often label as how we “look” is really how we’re acting when we’re believing a thought. And that means it’s something you can shift not by changing how you look, but by practicing how you show up and deciding to think differently about yourself .

And then I want to bring you back to your actual result. You thought they wouldn’t understand or pay, and yet you created connection, interest, and a next step. So what does that show you about that belief? Is it completely true, or just familiar? You don’t have to jump to a perfect new belief, but you can start building one based on evidence. Something like, “I’m learning how to communicate my work more clearly,” or “People are more open and interested than my brain predicts.” Belief is something you build through repetition and proof.

As you go into your next event, you might consider this: what if your only goal was to get a little better at helping people understand each time? Not perfect, not polished just practiced. That’s how confidence is actually created, and you’re already in the process of doing exactly that.

What are your thoughts ? Take your time and reflect what comes up and bring back any further coaching here.  Great work Coach!

---

## Taking on the mantel of coach and self belief

**Date:** 2026-03-23  
**Topics:** belief

**QUESTION:**

Ask a coach has Been has really helping me. I’m uncovering all sorts of very unhelpful thoughts, I could watch a million videos or listen to podcasts but with these thoughts…I’m sabotaging myself. I’ve been using “learning the consult method” against me. I’ve been using “I don’t come at this as a traditional coach” against me. So that…when I do a consult, these thoughts are there and I don’t have that “hell ya I can help you…” it’s more like “sure I can help you if you are interested and maybe you aren’t , maybe you are. ” ICK! I hear thoughts that “they aren’t ready,” ” they aren’t into what I offer,” “maybe I shouldn’t do consults as the entry point, ” “I’ve tried other things and had the same results, ” “what is the magic consult, freebie, funnel, workshop” that will work…I’ve been doing this for so long and others have so much more success than me maybe I should just give it all up. I mean, writing this outloud is making me frustrated. I don’t want to give up. I want to make this work. I don’t know if it will work – maybe I think, expand so I’m not so niched to this one community that “doesn’t seem to want or (better) understand what I offer” So the common denominator is me. I do struggle with the thought I am the coach they need to solve their presenting problem. The facts are that I have helped them solve MANY problems. And it works. So why don’t they keep coming or renew? I noticed in the last few consults that didn’t turn into clients, I get sucked into their story, so I don’t think I can help – I start distancing myself instead of digging in (and this is, I tell myself, because I’m not a “coach” in the traditional sense. I can be that way once they are working with me, but if I don’t yet have them as a student/client I don’t dig in like that.) I am learning. This thought is ok. But I don’t have many consults to practice on. I have a belief that”my people” don’t do consults. But here’s a thing….they don’t know what it is. And they don’t know that it can be a sales call. I have been putting myself out more – I’m doing a weekly live in my private fb group and making posts for IG based on that theme. But I really don’t know if anyone is seeing these. I write weekly emails – I see they are open, but ….. I know I can help. But I’m still allowing these thoughts and lack of external proof to slow my energy down.

**ANSWER:**

Hi coach,

We are so glad AAC has been helping you, especially in creating this level of awareness around your thoughts. That really is the work. It’s so normal to want to go consume more, podcasts, videos, learning the “right way” and there’s nothing wrong with that, there is so much value in that but what you’re doing here, actually exposing the thoughts that are holding you back, is the most powerful part.

You’ve already done something important you’re seeing the limiting beliefs instead of just reacting to them. Now we want to understand how they’re shaping your actions.

One thing I want to point out is how easy it is for your brain to go to comparison and make it mean something about you. So I’m curious what are you making other coaches’ success mean about you? I’m asking because your brain is wired to see that as a threat, as “they’re winning, I’m losing,” but what if you used that differently? What if their success became evidence of what’s possible, and you got curious about what they’re thinking and believing that you could also access?

The other thing I notice is the frustration. And frustration is often a surface emotion. So I’d ask what might frustration be keeping you from feeling? Is it disappointment? Fear of failing? This matters because when we stay in frustration, we don’t actually process what’s underneath, and that can keep you stuck in the same loop. Frustration is repelling your success by keeping you in negative energy instead of belief fueled action. Compare and despair keeps you in negative thinking and out of service and connection to your client.

You brought up a few things that are really useful to separate out  and answer so you can get clarity instead of them all feeling tangled together.

You said, “I don’t have many consults to practice on” and also “my people don’t do consults.” So let’s look at that more directly. What do you know you need to do to create consults? Not in theory, but specifically for you. And then where are you not doing that? I’m asking this to bring it back into your control, because when it feels like “I don’t have consults,” it can feel passive, but when you look at what’s in your control, you can start to solve for it. What do they need to hear in a way that makes them want consults. What is the value of a consult? This is a great episode to help you access the thoughts to create consutls too.

Episode 17: How To Get Consults

And connected to that what are the specific reasons consults aren’t being created right now,? What do you need to be thinking and feeling? ” How about “Clients are everywhere and I create consults”? Do you believe thoughts like this?

You also shared that when you’re on consults, you get pulled into their story and start distancing yourself. So I’m really curious what are you making their story mean about your ability to help them? Because their story isn’t the issue, but the meaning you give it will either keep you engaged or have you pull back. Make sure you are doing a deep evaluation after your consults.

You said you can coach deeply once they’re a client, so the ability is there. So then what changes for you mentally between “they are my client” and “they are not yet”? Understanding that gap is important, because right now it sounds like you’re holding back until it feels safer, but that’s also what’s preventing them from becoming a client.

With your content, you’re showing up lives, IG, emails—but your energy drops when you don’t see engagement. So I’d ask what expectations are you holding about how this should be performing right now, and where did those come from? And then, when you think “this isn’t working,” what do you stop doing or do differently? This matters because even small shifts in your energy and consistency will impact your results over time.

And then the opposite what does your action and energy look like when you believe it is working? Getting clear on that helps you see how much your thoughts are driving your results, not just your strategy.

You also said something really important: “I know I can help.” That’s a fact based on your experience. So I’d ask why are you not fully letting yourself stand in that when you’re in a consult? Not perfectly, but enough to stay present and lead.

You don’t need the perfect consult, funnel, or entry point right now. Those thoughts about “what’s the magic thing that will work” are just your brain trying to find certainty. But the real work is deciding what you believe about your ability to help and how you want to show up, even before you have external proof.

You’re not doing this wrong. You’re in the middle of learning how to see your thoughts and not let them run the show. And that’s exactly where growth happens. Keep going Coach!

---

## AAC # 220 Consult Eval – struggled to be in belief of coaching

**Date:** 2026-03-19  
**Topics:** belief

**QUESTION:**

I just did a consult that felt like I really struggled in. I had thoughts like “this is going off the rails” and “I haven’t bridged the gap”.

I didn’t get all the way through the process. I failed to clearly bridge the gap, and do the vision. And I could see that I was “explaining” what was happening to him without really understanding it.

I’ve processed through the feeling of “messing it all up” and the disappointment. But I also have a feeling of ashamed for doing it so poorly.

WW

For his work challenge, I could see how his feelings (reluctance) was leading towards not taking the actions of doing sales calls. It came out at the end of the call that he also had a thought that “people don’t want to talk to depressed people” and so he had been avoiding these calls, feeling apathy about them.

WDW

I was unable to clearly see the solution to his relationship issues. He said that his wife is uncommunicative, and yells and screams at him. She is reacting in a heated way frequently, and treating him poorly. She said she wants a divorce, but in two years time, and he didn’t want to keep being treated poorly so he went ahead and served her with divorce papers. But he also wants it to all work out. He wants respect and love and calm conversations and compromises.

I didn’t communicate to him how coaching could help him with his relationship issues. Probably because I didn’t see it that way, (because I can’t necessarily promise that improving his communication style will result in her changing her behavior.)

I couldn’t get a clear model out of him that made sense, that matched his feeling to his actions, so I could create awareness of his thoughts and their impact.

WWDD

Ask more about his wife to understand his thoughts about her. They seemed to waffle between “I want to appreciate and respect her again” but also things like “She doesn’t want to talk” and “she never doubts herself” and “she keeps changing her mind and taking back what she agreed to”.

Now I am feeling uncertain if coaching was the right solution for him. Even though I know it will help him, I don’t know if it’s the right thing to promise that coaching will help him improve his relationship with his wife.

This is not something I usually coach on and I think I felt out of my depth.

Can you help me learn and progress from this call, using something here to improve my consultation skills?

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach,

First, you are in it the work of progress already! You showed up, you did the consult, and now you’re evaluating it this honestly that alone is how you get really good. Nothing about this is wasted. This is exactly how skill gets built. I also want you to consider this: the consult didn’t actually “go off the rails” it revealed where your thinking got unclear, and that’s useful.

When you say you couldn’t see the solution to his relationship issue, I want to slow that down. What would a “solution” even mean here?  Your brain may have been trying to solve for his wife changing, and if that’s the standard, of course you’ll feel stuck because you can’t guarantee that.

So instead, what is solvable? If you had to answer now, what could coaching help him create regardless of what she does? This might look like him feeling less disrespected, showing up calmer in conversations, setting boundaries, or getting clear on what he actually wants and following through. Do you see how that shifts it?

The solution isn’t fixing the relationship it is helping him change how he experiences and shows up in it. So a useful question to hold onto is: what was  I trying to solve for him or the other person? That answer will either create clarity or confusion.

You also said you didn’t communicate how coaching could help because you don’t believe you can promise it will change her behavior. That’s true you can’t. But what can you stand behind? If he improved his thinking, his communication, and his emotional regulation, what would change for him even if she stayed exactly the same? I’m asking this because this is where your confidence comes from. Would he feel more grounded? Make cleaner decisions? Stop avoiding hard conversations? Trust himself more? That’s the work.

So the question becomes: what do you need to believe about coaching in order to communicate its value clearly? Right now it sounds like you were trying to sell an outcome you don’t fully believe in instead of standing in the outcomes you do believe in, and people can feel that.

You said you couldn’t get a clear model out of him, but why do you need him to give you a clean model? Most people can’t. If you shift from needing him to articulate it perfectly to guiding him into awareness, even if it’s messy, everything opens up. That can sound like asking what’s going through his mind right before he avoids the call or what he’s making it mean when she yells. And if he says “I don’t know,” that’s not a problem you can slow it down and help him take a guess.

You also noticed his thoughts about his wife were inconsistent. That actually makes sense. He both wants it to work and feels mistreated. He both respects her and resents her. That’s not something you need to fix in the consult that’s something to help him see. You can get curious about both sides and let him hear himself.

And the piece about feeling out of your depth,  you don’t need to be an expert in relationships to coach this. You coach thinking, feelings, decision-making, and personal responsibility, and all of that is happening here. So what made you decide you were out of your depth? That thought alone will disconnect you from your skill. How are you able to support him? What do you help with that had you say yes to consult?

If you take anything from this, let it be that confusion isn’t failure, it’s a signal that you need more information. And you don’t get that by asking more  questions with clear intention behind them. You’re closer than you think. Now it’s just refining what you’re solving for, what you believe about coaching is where the progress keeps improving every consult.  That’s the work, and you’re doing it!

---

## day 3 / 10 – how do I take action when I don’t have belief yet?

**Date:** 2026-03-09  
**Topics:** belief

**QUESTION:**

Thank you so much for your previous response. I was doing some self-coaching on the same model that I sent to you and what was really interesting was the realization that this thought system about “no longer being an expert” really made me feel like I had lost my ability to coach, or that the way I was coaching myself and clients before was wrong, and that’s why I couldn’t maintain growth. So it sent me off on this quest to figure out “the secret transformation tool.” Like I was missing something that I didn’t have before. I spent a lot of the past 6 months researching nervous system regulation because of that and talking about that research on my social media accounts but really not making any offers. The new unintentional model became:

C – Living with my brother, no car, low income months, 2 clients

T – “I’m no longer an expert.”

F – Confused

A – ruminating on what went wrong, wondering how I lost momentum, educating myself on different techniques, trying to figure out how to guide transformation.

R – I don’t position myself as an expert.

It’s so interesting to see it now because I really had gotten into the mindset that I didn’t know how to guide transformation anymore. That I had lost the ability or that the way I originally did it hadn’t worked. So even though I have been “showing up,” I haven’t stayed consistent or confident enough in my messaging to position myself as an “expert.”

Desired model

C – living with my brother, no car, low income months, 2 clients

T – “I am an expert, I know how to guide transformation regardless of my current life position, and it is possible to sign new clients and make more money.”

F – courageous, hopeful

A – __________

R – I am seen as an expert and it becomes possible for me to sign new clients and make more money.

My question is, if I’m not entirely believing the new thought in my desired model, how do I start taking action from that place? Or rather, how do I know who I need to be in order to fulfill this model? I think I’ve been stuck in the opposite belief for so long that it’s hard for me to see this new perspective though I really want to.

**ANSWER:**

Hi there Coach,

Let’s look over your intentional model and the questions you have:

Your Desired model

C – living with my brother, no car, low income months, 2 clients

T – “I am an expert, I know how to guide transformation regardless of my current life position, and it is possible to sign new clients and make more money.”

F – courageous, hopeful

A – __________

R – I am seen as an expert and it becomes possible for me to sign new clients and make more money.

Your question was: 
“If I’m not entirely believing the new thought in my desired model, how do I start taking action from that place? Or rather, how do I know who I need to be in order to fulfill this model?”

Okay coach, so here is the great news… You already know “who” you have to be in order to sign new clients and make more money. You stated “who” would you be in the T line. Intuitively, you know you have to believe you are an expert regardless of your current life position. When you think of yourself as an expert, you feel courageous and hopeful. So the next step is thinking of what actions you would take as someone who sees herself as an expert and feels courageous and hopeful. Really lean into this.

Imagine this for a moment: You are an expert coach. Well equipped to help your potential clients. You feel courageous. You feel hopeful 
(about your business, about helping clients, about making money), 
what do you do next in order to build your business? From this place, what do you feel called to do?

Is it to post on social media? Is it to send an email to your list? Is it to make a youtube video sharing what you do? Host a webinar? Write a blog? Go into the community and meet people? Start building connections? Make offers?

We must take action from who we desire to be BEFORE we feel ready. You are able to recognize that in order to make money and grow your business, you have to see yourself as an expert… so what does an expert do? Go do that now. Not when you feel ready, not when you feel 100% like the expert, but right now. As you take the actions as “expert” would take, you start to contribute to your value bank and get things in motion to create the results you want. That’s the “secret”.

What do you think? Feel free to come back with any thoughts or coaching you may still need. Thanks Coach!

## Response to last response – how to build belief

**Date:** 2026-02-24  
**Topics:** belief

**QUESTION:**

I am bringing Amy answers to AAC coach here and I’m committing now to showing up here consistently over the next month. Hesitantly, I am committing to the next 30 days, but I will make it happen.

So the coach response to my last question suggested I look at building my belief and changing it from – this isn’t working for me and getting clients seems hard to maybe I just haven’t been consistent and clear enough to gain the momentum I need.

In my conscious/practical brain, I am ok with posting, serving and sharing without return. I know there must be a block underneath that is preventing the serving to the jump of creating clients and revenue. I am making sure to t ell myself that successes inevitable if I just keep going. I know it’s possible because I see coaches all around me making it work. I need to crack the thing that’s holding me back so that any strategy I use is more effective and not built on a foundation of quicksand.

I have desire to make tis work, and after being involved for about 5 years, I have to work hard on the belief and thought that it can’t work. How can I do this so that I can quickly turn it around?

I am now in EC membership as well so I’m looking forward to picking away at the identity piece. There are times when I feel like there is so much material to absorb, but like my last coach response mentioned, it’s not enough to digest and absorb the material as a student.

Also in my last coach response, it was offered that I look at what a mom and business owner looks like. Again, I know I wan to be an example to my boys. They are mostly grown and want this for me too. I’d love to be an example for them on what’s possible. I do wonder if my role as a stay at home mom for 20+ years has impacted my belief in myself as someone who can be successful outside the home?

What do you advise that I start TODAY? Thank you!!!!

**ANSWER:**

Hey coach,

I want to start by acknowledging your commitment. Even hesitant commitment counts. Your brain is being honest about its uncertainty, and you are choosing to show up anyway. That is not a small thing. That is how momentum begins.

You shared this thought: 
“This isn’t working for me and getting clients seems hard… maybe I just haven’t been consistent and clear enough to gain the momentum I need.”
 I want you to really look at that. Not to force yourself to believe something different, but to notice what happens when you consider that this may simply be the normal stage of building anything new.

When the brain doesn’t see immediate results, its job is to flag danger and conserve energy. It interprets “no clients yet” as “this might not work.” Not because it’s true, but because your brain is wired to protect you from uncertainty. So the question becomes: what feels more useful to you  “this isn’t working,” or “I am still in the phase of creating clarity and momentum”? How is that thought true? what you look for you will find evidence and that will create belief.

I’m asking because the thought you choose will determine whether you continue showing up long enough for the result to materialize.

You also mentioned wondering if being a stay-at-home mom for 20+ years impacted your belief in yourself. Let’s look at that from another angle. Those 20 years did not remove capability. They built capability. I imagine you learned how to understand people, anticipate needs, manage emotions, stay committed to something bigger than yourself, and continue showing up without immediate reward. Those are not weaknesses in business. Those are foundational strengths.

You could make a list of everything those 20 years required of you. Not to feel better temporarily, but to show your brain factual evidence that you already know how to commit, serve, and lead and you have acquired skills.

You also said, “I know there must be a block underneath that is preventing the serving from turning into clients.” This is an important place to get specific. Blocks often feel mysterious, but they usually show up in very practical ways.

So I want you to look at this directly: How are you currently meeting people? How are you telling them you are a life coach? How often are you making direct offers to help someone solve a problem they have?

You asked what you should start today. Start with decision, and emotion. Decide that your job is to create clients and to connect in service.  Then allow your thoughts, feelings, and actions to align with that decision.

If your result is “I create clients,” ask yourself:

What would I be thinking if I knew clients were inevitable?

What would I be feeling if I trusted that this was working, even before I saw proof?

What actions would I take today from that place?

You would likely introduce yourself as a coach. You would start conversations. You would listen for problems. You would explain how you help. You would make offers.

Not perfectly. But consistently.

Another useful question is this: If you didn’t tell yourself “I don’t know where to start,” what would your best guess be? Your brain often does know. It just offers uncertainty first.

You can also begin by identifying the people you want to help. What are they struggling with? Do you talk about that problem openly? Do you explain why it happens? Do you teach them something useful now so they can experience immediate relief or progress? And then do you tell them that you can help them go further?

This is how trust and clients are built. Not by just absorbing more material, but by engaging in the process.

Most importantly, notice how you want to think about yourself as you take these actions. You don’t need perfect belief to begin. You only need enough willingness to move.

You might choose thoughts like:

“I am proud of myself for showing up.”

“I am building momentum.”

“My work will reach the people who need it.”

“I am becoming the coach I decided to be.”

Identity is not something you wait to feel. It is something you create through action.

So today, decide how you will meet people. Decide how you will introduce yourself. Decide what problem you help solve. And decide that your role now is not to wait for belief, but to build evidence.

Bring your answers back. We’ll refine them together. 5 years does not mean you are not behind. You are in the process!

## Building Belief 4

**Date:** 2026-02-18  
**Topics:** belief

**QUESTION:**

It has been a few weeks and I have done a webinar, posted more on social with my face in the picture and now I am about to host an in person event. I have made a lot of movement. The thought is “I am creating the life I want”. And I have done 2 videos. I still have some reluctance around the video.

There is a thought that is leading to the inaction. For instance, yesterday I had a moment to do a video, instead I did a written post, took a selfie and posted it. The thought that held me back in that moment was I was not ready, did not look camera ready and I did not want to appear like I did not know what I was doing, aka judgement.

And the question of why I want to post the video, the reach for videos is better, the way I would like to explain something or say something, will resonate more easily with a video are a couple of reasons.

I like the perspective that my client is stuck the same way I am stuck with video. And what I want to achieve is exposure for my client to see me and decide that I am the one.

I am still stuck with bridging the gap from the the thought of doing a video and not executing, though. Appreciate the coaching. Thanks

**ANSWER:**

Hi coach,

Let’s actually pause and celebrate the movement you are making! A webinar. More social posts with your face visible. Two videos. An in-person event. That’s not small. That’s momentum. And momentum is created exactly how you’re creating it — one courageous action at a time, repeated.

Now let’s talk about the reluctance. When you activate a new habit,  especially one that increases visibility , your brain will offer resistance. Not because you’re incapable. Because it’s efficient. It prefers familiar. It prefers conserving energy. So when you notice reluctance, one powerful thought to offer back is simply: 
“Of course.”

Of course this feels uncomfortable.
Of course my brain is offering me a way out.

You don’t need the reluctance to go away in order to move. You become stronger by overriding it not by eliminating it. You caught the thought: 
I’m not camera ready. I don’t look ready. I might look like I don’t know what I’m doing. 
Good. That’s awareness.

Now the real question becomes what do you want to do with that thought? How do you get yourself unstuck? Don’t believe everting your brain tells you.  Catching the thought is step one to the bridge the gap to activation,  But the gap between deciding and executing is bridged by behavior, not not always by better thinking. Even when you change your thought there can be sticky thoughts that try to keep you stuck.

You said, “I’m creating the life I want.”
Beautiful thought. But notice in the moment of action, a louder thought took over. What would it look like to execute even while the thought “I’m not ready” is present?  While it’s there.

The obstacle that keeps you stuck is the exact muscle you’re here to build. Mental toughness isn’t the absence of fear. It’s allowing the urge to avoid  and pressing record anyway.

You asked how to bridge the gap between deciding and not executing.  Execution bridges the gap.

It’s not comfortable. But every time you follow through while feeling reluctance, you expand. You become bigger than the obstacle. And then you can coach this from lived experience.

You mentioned something powerful: your client is stuck the same way you’re stuck with video. Exactly.

What would it be like if you no longer gave in to the urge not to execute? What would your client see you doing?

Who would you become if “not ready” wasn’t a stopping point?

Right now, “not ready” is being treated like a red light. What if it was just background noise?

The difference between coaches who grow and coaches who stall isn’t talent. It isn’t skill. It’s willingness to feel exposed. Willingness to look imperfect. Willingness to tolerate the emotion of judgment  real or imagined.

If you are willing to feel reluctance…
If you are willing to feel the fear of looking like you don’t know what you’re doing…
You will become unstoppable.

You don’t need to feel camera ready.
Are you committed to growth  or are you committed to comfort in the moment?

Every time you override the urge to stay small, you reinforce a new identity: the woman who keeps her word. The woman who moves before she feels ready. The woman her clients are looking for. Now it’s just reps, you got this!

## Lack of belief in potential clients

**Date:** 2026-02-12  
**Topics:** belief

**QUESTION:**

Hi there, I’ve been thinking about the belief triad and how my belief may be impacting why I don’t currently have any consults. For context, I am a book coach and I help working moms write novels. I believe in my ability as a coach and I believe that coaching is effective, but I realized that I don’t believe that people want to pay me to help them write their novel today (even though I have 3 clients who have paid me!). I get distracted by the 90% of people who are no’s—my primary platform is TikTok and I get distracted by the people who are recording their journey of writing a novel, and I take that to mean people don’t need a coach to help; I tend to forget about the lurkers since they aren’t vocal – but my three clients weren’t loud before we started working together. I managed to write a book without a coach, and so I think others can too—even though it took me a long time and I know I can get them to their goal faster and with more joy. When I tell people I’m a book coach and have written novels, they say they’ve always wanted to write a book, but it doesn’t go further and so I assume that writing a book is a lofty dream but not something people feel compelled to pay for right now. I’ve also got the sneaky thought that it my current audience wanted to hire me, they would have (though I also believe that I haven’t said the thing they need to hear to compel them to book a consult). I think all of this is impacting my marketing and how I’m engaging with the world as a coach. I’d love to get some coaching on new thoughts to help with focusing more on the moms who are watching but don’t believe in themselves enough to take that step of booking a call. When I think about having helped my clients make this investment in themselves and I think about how life-changing this work is, I feel inspired and certainty and compelled to help, and I’d like to carry those feelings into my thoughts. I also want to get out of my own head when it comes to the 90% who are a no. Thank you!

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach,

Thank you for sharing all of that. There’s so much awareness in what you wrote.

I want to start where you started:
“I get distracted by the 90% of people who are no’s.”

What happens to you when you focus on the 90% no’s? Specifically how do you show up differently?

I’m asking because this isn’t just about a belief floating in your head. When you focus on the no’s, something shifts in your energy. Do you soften your invitation? Do you not follow up? Do you not take the conversation further when someone says they’ve always wanted to write a book?

You said when people tell you they’ve always wanted to write a book, it doesn’t go further. Why not?

Notice what thought is happening in that moment. Are you deciding for them that it’s just a lofty dream? Are you assuming they’re not serious? Because if you assume they’re not compelled, you won’t feel compelled either. And when you don’t feel compelled, you don’t sell the vision.

You shared that you believe in yourself as a coach and you believe coaching works but you don’t fully believe people want to pay you right now.  What if you directed your brain to focus on the 10 % that are potential yes’s? What would you be saying ? Ho would you show up if you were only focused on the potential yes and what they need to hear?

Your brain is highlighting the 90% because that feels safer. Brains scan for rejection. That’s normal. But what happens in your action line when you keep your focus there? You don’t give energy to the potential yes.

You also wrote your own book without a coach and you’re using that as evidence others can too. Yes, they can. But at what cost? How long did it take? How much doubt did you manage alone? You know you can get them there faster and with more joy and yet you’re minimizing that stretch because you did it the hard way. Why would they want that support?

Just because you climbed the mountain alone doesn’t mean others don’t want a guide.

When you think about the clients you’ve helped, you feel inspired and certain. That’s not random. That’s you thinking on purpose.

So instead of trying to eliminate the 90% no, what if your work is to redirect your brain  daily  to the woman who is watching quietly and doesn’t believe in herself yet?

When your brain says, “If they wanted to hire me, they would have,” notice that’s scarcity. People don’t hire because they vaguely want to. They hire when someone clearly shows them what’s possible and why it matters now. It’s your job to paint that picture. It’s your job to create possibility.

Why is having a coach worth it?
Why does finishing a novel matter for a working mom?
What shifts in her identity when she becomes someone who finishes?

Because here’s the honest part: in order to create desire in others, you have to feel it first.

How do you create desire when you’re focused on the 90% no’s? You can’t. Your brain checks out. Your marketing gets tentative. This is an internal process you visit every day.

Why do I want to do this?
What excites me about helping her?
What about this is compelling?
Why do I care?

When you notice your brain drifting to the no’s, gently direct it back to the yes. Over and over. That’s leadership.

You don’t need 90% of your audience. You need to believe deeply enough in the woman who is ready that your energy reaches her. Your lack of belief is leading your marketing right now. Shift the focus and your action will follow.

You’re already onto yourself. That’s a very good place to be.

Thank you for this question. I hope this doesn’t sound terrible. I have just seen so many people (not my clients) that say they want something and start out with fire and then fizzle out. The goal was to go up an over the Sundae Pass, which is an epic hike in the Beartooth Mountains. Everyone was very excited to sign up and buy the gear. And 2 of those 4 were my hiking partners that had done it before. That being said, here are some of my thoughts: 1. They are unlikely to do the hard work. I see a lot (in nursing especially) that unfortunately, people take the easy way out and don’t do the hard work to get better and take care of their health. 2. They might not be “tough enough”, again, they consider the work is too hard and choose to stay comfortable. I think this is because I think I am unusually tenacious, but I get it, that is not for everyone. 3. I think I am going to want it more than they do. Again, I don’t quit easily and can overdo it. I think these are the main reasons. Again, I hope this doesn’t sound awful. Thank you!!

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach!

First off, no judgement here! Thanks for sharing your true thoughts. Let’s coach on them!

What’s interesting is noticing what your brain is offering. Your brain is telling you, 
“I have just seen so many people 
(not my clients)
 that say they want something and start out with fire and then fizzle out.”

Here’s what I’d like to point out:

1.
 Think about how your brain is already assuming (from other scenarios) 
NOT
 
your actual client
 scenarios…

“I’ve just seen so many people 
(not my clients)
.”

Think about how this may impact how you show up to 
your clients
 
by your brain assuming most people will be this way. The truth is, yes, there will be plenty of people who start strong and then fizzle out. And you know exactly why — comfort. The brain loves comfort. Funny enough, your brain is also using comfort (in holding onto these thoughts) because it feels easier than figuring out what you’ll do if/when you have clients “fizzle out” or struggle with the hard work.

IF that happens, you’ll have to sharpen your skills as a coach, right?

How you coach them week-to-week may shift a bit when they are in resistance… or when their brain “just doesn’t like the hard” and wants to give up…. What if that’s okay? Do you trust that you’re fully capable of navigating those moments as a coach?

2.
 When your brain offers these outside examples, consider this. Coaching tends to invite a different caliber of a person. Why? Because when someone says “yes” to coaching, they are signing up to 
intentionally
 
break patterns, to 
intentionally
 
face what isn’t working in their life, and to
 intentionally
 
make changes that help them create what they want.

In saying this, yes, their brain will 
still be human
 and do human things…like seek comfort or tell them to quit.

You’re not wrong about human behavior… people 
do
 fizzle out.
But
 your brain is also pre-deciding WHO THEY ARE before they’ve had a chance to be met by your coaching.

And this is only because your brain is trying to protect 
you.

Coaching isn’t about working only with the people who are already strong, disciplined, or tenacious.

It’s about holding belief 
until 
they
 can access it themselves
. Belief is part of the intervention…. especially in coaching.

The moment you believe you’ll want it more than your client, you stop being a coach and start becoming a manager of outcomes.

What are your thoughts?

## Intentional model T: I am making it work

**Date:** 2026-06-09  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

Hi Coach,

Thanks for this very helpful coaching.

The thought « “My business is producing results that reflect the actions I’m taking.” Is working vey well for me, I feel more grit and I feel my brain switching to problem solving mode.

What a relief !

When you say :  « What evidence do you have that your business is working? You have clients renewing. People refer others to you. Some people ask for coaching even when you are not actively making offers.

Those are not signs of a business that isn’t working. »

I feel resistance and I feel my brain not buying it 🙁

Good to know, and also evidence that I am not ready YET for frontal negation of my so well practiced thought « it’s not working ». So once again the thought « I see my brain very attached to the idea that it is not working » is creating a feeling of gentle irony that makes it less dramatic.  I so much need less drama !

Funny enough, It feels good and true when you say « something is working », it seems acceptable for my brain that something is working, if it is not my business, lol.

I think it is acceptable because of the money aspect. I am not making a lot of money with my business, but I am actively taking care of it, thinking about it, making it evolve. That is working. The money part is yet to figure out.

When I build the intentionnel model, here’s what’s appearing :

C: Business

T: I am making it work

F: fun (I could have tried also openness and curiosity)

A: acting on every idea that my creative brain offers me, being curious, trying things, evaluating without making it means things about me, talking to people with a light heart, being unattached, watching every single evidence that it is working, every question, every connection, imagining all the potential and possibilities without comparing myself to others, doing it my unique way, having fun with marketing, having fun with selling, having fun with coaching.

R: I am making the business work with my life and my identity

It feels good and empowering to spend some time in this model but it also immediately feels delusional. I am telling myself « What about the offers ? Is it going to work magically without me making any offers ? Fun won’t prevent me from avoiding making offers, fun is not sustainable » It sounds like I am diving in black and white thinking again.

I’ll put some air in it again.

I’d you can help me do it, it is of course very welcome.

**ANSWER:**

HI Coach,

Yes, this is exactly what you want to keep doing over and over again.

Notice when you’re in the unintentional model the thought that it’s not working and then direct yourself back to, “I make it work.”

This is really the practice. Noticing when you’re in and out of belief and accepting that you don’t need to stay in the intentional model all day long to make progress.

You are your best and hardest client because you’re inside your own brain all day. It’s harder to see when you’ve drifted into old thinking. But every time you notice it and redirect yourself, you’re strengthening a new pathway.

One question I would play with is: How can you keep bringing yourself back to the thought, “I make it work”?

Because notice what happens when you do. You become creative. You inspire yourself to take action. You start looking for possibilities instead of evidence that it’s failing. That’s the first step.

Imagine if you knew you would follow through on your ideas. Imagine the ripple effect of having an identity that says, “I follow through.”

I also think your brain is wanting to go deep into thought management and find the perfect thought before taking action. Sometimes it’s not that deep. Sometimes it’s simply noticing, “This thought isn’t helping me follow through,” choosing one that serves you better, and then taking the next action.

I hear your brain wanting to jump to, “But what about the offers? Is fun enough? What if I avoid making offers?”

That’s the how.

What if instead you asked: Who do I need to be to make this work?

Because when you are being the person who makes it work, you find ways to make offers. You become willing to learn. You become willing to try things, evaluate them, and adjust without making the results mean something about you.

The person comes before the process.

The identity comes before the result.

You don’t need to know exactly how you’re going to do it today. That’s what your brain is looking for when it asks about offers. It wants certainty before it moves.

But if you stay in “I make it work,” and keep showing up from that place, you’ll figure out the offers. You’ll figure out the marketing. You’ll figure out the next step.

When hopelessness or doubt starts creeping in, borrow some belief. Listen to a 2K call. Put on a podcast. Direct your thinking toward higher-value thoughts.

Maybe it’s:

“I’m learning what works and what doesn’t, and I can adjust.” 
Or:

“I’m capable of taking small steps that add up to results.” 
Or even: 
“I’m ready to make money.”

Then ask yourself:

How can I meet someone today?

How can I add value today?

If I were in 2K energy today, what would I be doing?

Those questions help move you out of evaluation mode and back into participation mode.

Keep practicing putting air into it.

Keep noticing when your brain is attached to “it’s not working.”

And keep coming back to, “I make it work.”

What are your thoughts?  We can work through this together.

## Practical ways to make new intentional thoughts sink in andf stick…

**Date:** 2026-03-02  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

I know I have work to do as far as choosing new thoughts around my offer and how I can help people. How do you all get the new beliefs to stick? I know the subconscious is so tricky so I’m wondering what impact new thoughts will have if I consciously just think them? Do you write them? Say them? hang them up? i guess – how do we get them to go from thought/idea phase to new identities and rewiring?

Thank you!!

**ANSWER:**

Hi Coach!

Great question! While our subconscious is powerful, so it our conscious mind. The conscious mind is where we create those new thoughts we want to believe and the practice is really rehearsing those thoughts so often (by thought, journaling, saying it out loud, finding evidence to support them) that over time it moves into our subconscious mind – kind of on autopilot.

There are some teachings about something called the 95% vs. 5% in regards to our brain.

95% of our brain operates on autopilot…It consists of the thoughts, beliefs, patterns and habits we’ve practiced the most.

5% of our brain operates consciously and is the part we must use to create new thoughts, beliefs, patterns and habits.

The reason it feels hard for the new thoughts/patterns/habits to “stick” is because we have 95% of our brain power holding onto the older (more rehearsed) patterns/thoughts. It doesn’t however mean it’s impossible to change, it just means we have to invite our conscious brain (the 5%) to practice what we want MORE. Eventually those new thoughts, habits, beliefs and patterns become apart of our 95% programming and works in our favor.

Writing them, saying them, posting notes to keep seeing them, and finding evidence that supports those new thoughts are all great ways to make them more of a reality. Also connecting those new thoughts 
with emotions that feel good
 
in our body. When we do this, it’s kind of like creating a magnetic charge.

For example, “I can make 2k today”. That’s a thought that maybe doesn’t feel totally believable right now. But that’s only because 95% of the brain is finding evidence for the opposite. But, if we want to make 2k today, we need to keep that thought at the forefront and practice it often. Then we need to anchor into
 how we would actually feel
 if 2k came into our bank account every day. The feeling may be 
“elated, excited, thrilled”.

We want to 
hold those emotions with the thought,
 truly practicing
 the feeling of it in our body.

Over time, our brain can’t tell the difference between our thought/feeling being real or a dream… And when it gets to that place, we tend to create the result more often without having to consciously think it. Our subconscious begins to do more of the work for us.

1. What are some thoughts you want to practice thinking?

2. And what emotions are generated when you think them?

Feel free to come back with any thoughts or coaching you may still need! Thanks coach!
