## Not Giving Up & Going Intentionally

**Date:** 2026-02-16  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

Thank you for seeing me – And I honestly forget that I’m allowed to be human. I am still learning how to give myself compassion. I guess I would have to speak to myself as if my best friend was going through this situation: “this is a shitty situation that you didn’t choose” “this situation is a hard one” “be kind to yourself”.

When I think those other thoughts, “I just got fired” “am I capable and worthy”, I don’t even create the content because I feel inadequate to be sharing what I have to offer. I don’t network because I feel like a fraud. I do hesitate, shrink, overthink and work on things that are not meeting people, telling them I’m a Life Coach, or making offers to help. I hide myself. And then my brain goes into a cycle of fearing the outcome from the space I’m in knowing that these thoughts won’t yield me the results I want, then frustrated with myself in knowing that…

But that’s where the need for self-compassion I guess needs to come in to calm everything down. So my awareness is not what beats me down, it’s my inability to be compassionate with myself for where I am human.

If I started the clock now… There’s fear in thinking the previous outcomes will be the ones that come forward now, but that fear is subsided by Isaiah 43:18 and 2 Corinthians 5:17 – I will be saying those out loud VERY often to keep that thought out of my head.

The results I want to create in 90 days are to have 10 paying clients on 3 or 6 month programs.

The person that fully believed they are capable to do that would plan content around their client’s struggles and how they can help, go to networking events, show up for themselves through life’s chaos, and show up for clients during the consultations, without being afraid of saying something that would cause a potential client to getting angry or hurt.

They are struggling with putting too many things on their plate that aren’t for their benefit or their desire. They tolerate disrespect on high and low levels, and tolerate bending boundaries for other people. They are afraid to make any decision that is in direct conflict with someone they value in their life.

People that want to create my of their own peace in their life hire me.

Woman that want to be empowered to their highest selves hire me.

When my brain says “I got fired” I want it to mean “God wanted to give me more time for coaching, because that’s what He called me to do.”

I’m going to journal about who I want to be while I make it work.

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach,

I want to point out something you can keep bringing yourself back to when your mind wants to go to “i just got fired.”

You don’t have to overcome every thought. You don’t have to solve it immediately. Sometimes you actually want to allow it to be there, be curious about it, not believe it, not become anxious about anxious thoughts. That second layer is usually what creates the spiral.

You already see that compassion is the way through this. It may not feel natural yet. Some days you have to catch it and redirect it more than others. That is worth the effort. That is work worth doing.

Here’s the piece I really want you to notice: in order to have the business you want, you have to take a ton of action. And low-level, low-energy thinking  “am i a fraud?” doesn’t fuel action, it fuels hiding.

And you already see that pattern: 
I don’t create the content
I  don’t share what i have to offer
I don’t network
I hesitate
I shrink
I overthink
I don’t make offers
I hide

That’s not because you’re incapable, it’s because of what you are believing in the moment.

The shift I  want to point out is this: when you started focusing on your client, on the struggle they have and the suffering they are in, you moved into service energy. You get out of “get energy.” That is how you want to keep directing your mind.

Get energy = Thought about you and inadequate thinking. 
Service energy = how can i help? what are they tolerating? what are they afraid to say? what peace are they craving?

Serving keeps you out of suffering. It keeps you thinking about your client’s struggle instead of your own doubt. When you work through this for yourself and show up even with some negative thoughts present, you show the power of the work you do. That is compelling. That is magnetic.

You said you want 10 paying clients in 90 days. So here’s a question that matters: what does a well-planned action plan look like when you are in belief, not in doubt? Take each of those actions you don’t take when in low belief and plan them out.

Map it out while you’re grounded how much content, how many conversations, how many networking rooms, how many offers.

Then another question: what will you do with that action plan on the days your brain says “i got fired” or “who do you think you are?” How do you respond, plan on it , anticipate it coming up.

Because in the beginning results won’t come from feeling confident, they come from following the plan showing up when you don’t feel like it.

And about “I got fired”  absolutely make that mean, “God gave me more time to build what He called me to build.” Hold onto that. Let it fuel you. Let it fire you up. How will you use the time?

When you are called to do this work how do you show up?

What does a well-planned action plan look like when you are in belief? map it out when you are grounded in faith and you create the time to coach. 
how much content?
how many conversations?
how many networking rooms?
how many offers?

Keep journaling the who before the how. who is she  and what does she do consistently, even when she doesn’t feel like it?

The version of you who believes she’s capable doesn’t avoid fear,  She feels committed. She shows up with fear sometimes. She plans content around real struggles. She networks even when it’s uncomfortable. She makes offers knowing she cannot control how they’re received.

Compassion calms you.
Service fuels you.
Action builds belief.

---

## Belief Work and Intentional Thoughts

**Date:** 2025-08-20  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

I’m really getting down on myself lately. I currently have zero paying clients on my calendar. I have averaged about 1 or 2 consistently for the last 4 years or so. I officially launched my business in April of 2021 and I when I think about setting new goals I find myself looking at my old goals and thinking – I have accomplished none of this. I feel like I am kidding myself trying to make this a successful business sometimes. I notice I have a lot of fear of niching down and choosing one platform to really focus in on but I do believe that having that type of restraint will ultimately give me the success I desire but I have a hard time sticking with it. I keep wanting to reinvent the wheel thinking that what I am doing is not working. I am open to any suggestions. Thank you.

**ANSWER:**

Right now you think that what you are doing isn’t working – and then your action is to go back and re-invent the wheel.

It sounds like instead of evaluating and getting specific about what’s not working, you just think everything isn’t working.

Let’s evaluate this and get more specific. We want to see what IS working and what isn’t working. Then the what we will do differently, is specific and aimed to solve what’s not working.

If you look at your actions over the last week,

How have you been meeting people?

How have you been telling people you are a coach/giving value?

How have you been making offers?

What IS working?

Maybe it’s that you are meeting new people. Or having people engage with you. Maybe it’s that you are planting seeds or getting better at sharing what you do.

What’s not working?

For example, maybe you are posting 4 times a week and wanting to create new followers, but you’ve only had 5 new followers this week and they were all business coaches.

What can you do differently to solve what’s not working?

If we take the example from above, you’d want to create more new followers who are your ideal clients. This is where you start to brainstorm some ideas and then decide on what you want to test out. We can brainstorm with you too.

This is going to help you restrain too. It’s going to have you focused on figuring out that platform vs. just trying something totally different. It’s more focused vs. throwing spaghetti at the wall.

## Impact of an Unintentional Thought

**Date:** 2025-06-18  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

“You’d like to be making more money. You’d like to have more than enough to cover the bills. Let’s work on being that person who makes more money – starting with an extra £250 a week.”

This makes a lot of sense, and thank you for putting it so plainly and clearly for me. It’s kind of “snapped” me out of dwelling on it.

Q: How many clients would you need to hit that £250 a week?

I would need 3 or 4 new clients per month, 1 new client a week.

Q: When you think about signing that many new clients – what thoughts come up? That’s going to help you see that pathway to creating those clients. We want to look at that. That’s going to help you get where you want to be vs. being stuck working on this thought.

Thoughts that come up for me are:

* I’ve signed 3 clients in one month before, I can do it again.

* I believe in me, I can do this!

* I get things done! That’s just who I am.

* This is working, I am exactly where I should be.

* My offer is amazing and people want to hear about it today!

* Clients come out of nowhere because they see the value I give.

I’m definitely in a high-value cycle at the moment and so my thoughts feel good. I was using the model for self-coaching this morning with the thought “This is working” and the circumstance of 250k revenue (I had it as a journal prompt from one of Stacey’s videos to add 0’s on to what you want to make and see what comes up for you).

When I was thinking that thought, an unintentional thought came up around “Don’t break this” or “Don’t ruin this” that created pressure. I decided to redirect my brain to the intentional thought rather than spend any time on it.

Here’s the intentional model:

C: 250k of Revenue

T: This is working!

F: Serene (Certain & Calm)

A: Journal/Self-Coach Daily

A: Get Coached as much as possible

A: Keep my marketing essentialist – Instagram, LinkedIn & Emails.

A: Focus on connection everywhere I go

A: Get out of my home and go and meet people

A: Continuously grow my skillset and capacity, in a focused way (I.E. Don’t try to change everything at once)

A: Schedule in lots of R&R time

A: Enjoy being me in my life, enjoy life

A: Focus on service & giving people transformations

R: I create opportunities for this to work.

**ANSWER:**

You noticed that when those thoughts came in – it took you off course so you re-directed back to thoughts that help you hit this goal.

In you actions in this model you have all the parts – you have meeting people, telling them you are a coach and making offers thought your marketing. You have looking after yourself so you can keep going and keep showing up in the energy you want to be in.

It also feels so much better to be in this model too. It helps you be who you want to be.

## Changing Offer slightly and unintentionally

**Date:** 2024-11-22  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

Had follow up chat because last time couldn’t tell her what I did. I told her that I help BIPOC women of faith who have service based businesses stop hustling and create results from a place of peace and calm and I can get 3 clients in 90 days or money back. I didn’t believe what I was offering. I don’t believe I can guarantee peace and calm. Looks like the other coach got her clear vision, mission and her profile. I don’t think I can really do this.

I’m starting to question my offer and I want to drop it instead build belief I can do this. All of sudden I’m thinking nobody wants this and who am I kidding I can’t get them this result and that I’m lying when I say you can have peace and calm that’s not true all the time and I the only reason I said that was to exaggerate it so it seems more than it is. It seems nobody will give me a chance to prove I can do this. I’ve stopped doing my intentional belief work also. I’m taking every no to mean there is something wrong with my offer and I need to change it. got thought should I start giving free session for her to see value then I stopped and realized I need to build belief in my offer.

Also my offer keeps shifting ever so slightly. It was originally I help BIPOC women of faith with service based business stop spinning their wheels and getting nowhere take consistent action to get real results. I help them create next 3 clients in 90 days or money back.

To I stop them from hustling and find peace and calm. It seems to be a play on words but it’s also speaking to my lack of belief in what I sell. It seems I have to offer bigger results to get someone to work with me because what I offer isn’t enough.

I want to keep same offer and build belief. Any coaching?

**ANSWER:**

When people don’t say yes to your offer – you think that means the offer isn’t big enough or good enough and then you change your offer.

But then you end up with an offer that you don’t feel confident in. An offer that you don’t have belief you can help them with.

Let’s go back to this base of helping people with service based businesses stop spinning their wheels and getting nowhere take consistent action to get real results.

Let’s say I own a business. I do lash extensions. And I want to grow that business, but every week I tell myself that I need to get out there and take action but I keep not doing it. How do you help me?

## Unintentional Marketing – Upsetting Thoughts

**Date:** 2024-10-16  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

I am hearing from Stacey that

1. Some people don’t have the money to invest in coaching

2. Some people have to think it over (Stacey mentioned some Human Design type!)

3. Yesterday, I think she actually said: Some people are not ready!!!

all of which are a great relief to me because my past 18 consults have been one of these.

At the same time, Stacey mentions that if Oprah called, people wouldn’t have to think and would make it happen.

Here are my thoughts:

I am not coming from serving energy

What I’m talking about in my marketing is not hitting the “right” kind of people

People come on consults because they are curious and have got plenty of time. They have got no intention to buy

All of this is having me feel upset.

Here’s the action I can take from these thoughts (although not the feeling: I’ll have to change that):

1.In the past, I invited people on a consultation. Nobody booked. Now I’m inviting people to a Life Audit Session: many people book but have no intention to buy. I can revert to calling my consults consults and accept that few if no people book.

This has me think that I’m missing out on opportunity to help and to make an offer. I could be wrong.

2. I can change my marketing or get better at discerning my ideal client (I’m a general life coach).

I feel that I’m already speaking to her.

3. I can sit back, meditate on this and have it be OK.

You’ll probably tell me: “All the above!”

My comforting thought is: my current clients did not have to think about it, they were ready, they keep re-upping and pay in full. This feels so easy and natural.

What can you see?

**ANSWER:**

Where you want to start first is understanding why each consult is saying no.

It’s so different to be really overcoming objections and have the client come to a decision that the cost of coaching right now actually isn’t what’s best for them. Vs. Them saying they don’t have the money without really looking at or understand where that’s coming from. It’s easier for someone to say they don’t have the money than to say no to you or themselves.

Do you feel like you are nailing the overcoming objections part of the consult?

When someone gets off the call, do you know that the decision they made really made the most sense for them and their circumstances?

## Unintentional Sales Pitch/Marketing

**Date:** 2024-09-06  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

I’m in served and I’m stuck here with yesterday’s call about Unintentional Sales pitch.

I was answering the first question about “What are my disconnections that create my unintentional Sales pitch… and this is what’s happening for me

I have a disconnect that’s causing an unintentional marketing and sales pitch because the results I offer aren’t results that I have! So I help female entrepreneurs with marketing and mindset. I help them market from a place of service where they understand how the subconscious mind of their ideal clients work and how to utilize that in your content (and then also networking etc) and so I help them get clients and make money

But

I don’t have those results myself because I haven’t been putting in the work. So I get all choked up before I start marketing and the cycle of not creating the results continue. I ruminate constantly about who I target, what I offer, what challenge/workshop to offer because of this

I’ve been considering to only help with mindset for female entrepreneurs, like overwhelm, time management etc because I feel more aligned with that and that I have those results and can help. Also, because I feel like it’s more coaching than teaching for newer clients.

But then again, for a newer entrepreneur, I have a lot of value and knowledge to share around marketing. I can really help with all the basics and help them focus on the things that’ll get them clients and not get distracted with all the other things.

I feel like perhaps that’s also just what it is, is speaking about all the different things I do, but then I’m worried I confuse my people.

So I feel like I can’t even go to the second question about what my intentional sales pitch is before I just make a decision here.

Please help

**ANSWER:**

You help female entrepreneurs with marketing and mindset. You help them market from a place of service where they understand how the subconscious mind of their ideal clients work and how to utilize that in your content (and then also networking etc) and so you help them get clients and make money.

What led you to this niche initially? Let’s look at that.

Sometimes leaning into that can be really helpful when we have doubt creeping in. It can also be good to see what thoughts/feelings led to that decision so we can decide if we like the decision we made or not. For example, if you chose this niche only because you thought that T: Only business coaches can make money, then likely we want to look at that thought and decide if that’s really true. Vs. if you decided on this niche because you’ve always loved business and you worked in marketing for 5 years – that’s likely a decision you are going to want to stay with.

You also shared you don’t have these results because you haven’t put in the work. It sounds like you believe you COULD have these results. That you know how to get them. What do you think?

What do you think has kept you from putting in that work so far?

R – 12 clients fully booked and paid $100 per session

## Intentional thought creation – a reference by Stacey

**Date:** 2024-06-17  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

I would love help on intentional thought creation for the following:

1) I have the belief that nobody will come on my FB live

2) I believe that nobody will find value in my MasterClass

M

## Unintentional models

**Date:** 2024-06-11  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

What is your current, unintentional model you have about helping people right now, where you are with what you have?

C – Getting and helping clients with what I have right now

T – I have to know the entire process before I can help anyone

F – inadequate

A – don’t offer my coaching as often as I can, talk in lower voice and hide my coaching business from people, make up my mind that know one will buy my coaching, beat myself up from not being able to figure it out in my head, compare myself to other coaches and tell myself there is no point I will never get anywhere and keep debating if I should could and go back to a job I don’t like, build evidence of how much money I have invested in my business and not made a return as proof I am inadequate.

R – I don’t gain the experience that will help me learn the process

What is your current, unintentional model you have about helping people right now, where you are with what you have?

C – Getting and helping clients with what I have right now

T – I will hurt them

F – fear

A – protect my clients by not offering coaching to them, hide by doing Ask A Coach all day and consume coaching videos, don’t meet people, don’t tell them I am a life coach, don’t make offers to help, keep going to a job I don’t want to be at, invest in more certifications,

R – I hurt myself

I’m starting to see that I really want to know “how” before I start as like damage control because if you let me loose with a client without knowing the entire way, I will hurt them. Hurting them means I will say something that will make their emotions worse. Maybe tell them they are doing it wrong and that’s why they are getting nowhere. I see it comes back to what I believe about myself. I think I am a bad person who makes life miserable for others and therefore no one likes me. As I write this down, it’s not true, I am helpful to a lot of people but it seems I can’t trust my own judgments and will leave people worse then they found me. The more I write the more unsure I am about what I mean I will hurt them.

I know what I will do is: check in, seek to understand, listen and adjust and change course based on feedback. This of course isn’t guarantee that I will get it perfect, I may “hurt” them unintentionally but I will pause and apologize and reassess and even stop the coaching relationship if I see I am doing more damage then good and of course will return their money.

**ANSWER:**

Have you had practice clients or paid clients? Have you coached people?

Have you hurt someone?

Your mind is offering that you don’t know what you are doing and that you are going to hurt someone. But how can you know what you are doing without doing it? That would be like trying to learn how to ride a bike but never getting on a bike.

You don’t want to be coaching people in situations where you could hurt someone. What people do you NOT want to coach? For example, someone in an abusive relationship. Someone dealing with depression. Who else? When might you not want to coach someone?

And what are some people you could coach? For example, if you are helping someone lose weight and you say the wrong thing – would it really cause them harm? What would happen?

## Question Title: Stick with Unintentional model….what does that look like..Part 3

**Date:** 2024-05-14  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

Yes, you’ll know what actions and feeling-fuels were enough AFTER you’ve reached your goal. Until that time, you get to decide who you want to be on the journey to reach the goal.

It’s important to understand how we’re measuring our success. Currently you’re fueling your work with, “I’m not doing enough,” but until you clearly see WHY you’re thinking that in the first place, you can’t get rid of it.

* When will you believe that you’re doing enough? How will you know?>I still wanna say I don’t know but I guess when I have any client, or fully booked, Very different I know. I just wanna coach and all this before stuff gahhhh. But I also could get on board with I am doing enough>and then shift my belief… reminding myself with the list of ways I am doing enough daily…that seems like it makes sense to do.

* What will you make it mean when you’ve created those results?… I will make it mean I’m doing it right, I am doing enough, the right things…

I feel confused with this question and the difference between doing and believing. I think I can believe I am doing enough. But I don’t believe it yet…

And to also continue working on this unintentional model, what do I do? Sorry to be slow here and I appreciate your help.

How do I work on unintentional thoughts and wiggle them to intentional thoughts…I want to just decide but them I know my bridge isn’t strong enough yet..and want to understand how Stacey does it..hrm

**ANSWER:**

You’re learning a new way of thinking – and it takes the time it takes. Your pace is your own – which means it can’t be ‘slow’ unless you’re unhelpfully comparing it to someone else. You’re good, coach.

When intentional thoughts don’t make sense to believe, it’s because there are too many obstacles.

Once we understand the obstacles, we can move past them. We do this by exploring unintentional models – why are they there? What are they telling us? When did we first remember thinking them? And as we understand the obstacles more clearly, we’ll see them as the optional thoughts they are – and they’ll no longer make sense to believe.

Then, believing intentional thoughts comes naturally – because we’ve cleared away all the distractions. Much like cleaning a house allows you to enjoy the actual living space – what is really there.

What do you think?

You’ve been thinking you’re not doing enough, and it’s been putting all kinds of pressure on your life and business. And it makes sense that your business journey has been challenging, because you didn’t even have a clear picture of where you were going. AND because no matter what you did, you weren’t seeing your progress, so you couldn’t measure how far you’ve come.

This is where you can decide, on purpose, what your goal is, AND to pay attention to what you’re doing well, so you can see your progress and feel good about the work you’re doing.

What do you think?

You shared, “
I feel confused with this question and the difference between doing and believing. I think I can believe I am doing enough. But I don’t believe it yet…”

* We are always acting on our beliefs. Your actions (and how you feel as you do them,) will tell you what your beliefs are. If you don’t like what you’re believing, you can examine your obstacles to believing what you want.

When we’re thinking we’re not doing enough, it’s because we’re focused on our actions and missing the other part of the success equation – our FEELINGS. Useful actions + helpful feeling-fuel = our desired results.

And the whole time you’re on your journey, you’ll be making progress. There will be milestones reached, and mini-goals hit. You don’t have to worry about whether you’re doing enough when you’re paying attention to those. Because what matters more is your trajectory – if you’re getting closer to the goal. Doing ‘enough’ has nothing to do with it.

FOR YOU:

Consider when you started your business:

* What are the skills you’ve developed?

* What are the lessons you’ve learned??

* How many people have you served?

* What are the milestones you’ve reached?

* What comes more easily to you now that was previously challenging?

When you consider the above answers, how easy is it to feel your progress, and believe that you’re on track to reach your goal of signing a client?

## Stick with Unintentional model….what does that look like

**Date:** 2024-05-08  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

Stacey said, “Stick with the Unintentional model. Stay with it until it wiggles.”

I wanna do this.

Let’s use the thought, “I’m not doing enough”

C: showing up in business

T: “I’m not doing enough”

F: pressure

A: show up because I’m trying to create a specific outcome/clients/ Needing to do/connect/produce/do more…. spinning about if what I am doing is right/working, thinking these thoughts when doing what I am doing, meeting people, thinking I need more than I have, need to be better or learn more, etc.Is it, to find evidence where it isn’t true?Decide to think different thoughts?>>Isn’t that shifting to the intentional model?

I am not sure?

Is it sitting with the pressure, feeling the feeling, and accepting it and getting to know it so that I can recognize it and be onto it’s BS more and more quickly and realize that it is creating the feelings and that is the worst it will do And then it wiggles and I can get back to more useful thoughts and reminders of evidence proving this is all untrue? Or unhelpful and to get on with constructive evaluation and progress?

**ANSWER:**

You’ve asked a great question, coach.

Staying with an intentional model can look like all of those things.

The more you gain awareness of a model that doesn’t produce your desired outcome, the less sense it makes to keep thinking those thoughts.

The more you see the thought as a choice, and an unhelpful one, it won’t make sense to keep thinking it. You’ll naturally switch to a thought which makes more sense.

Your circumstance is ‘showing up in business,’ and you’re consistently thinking you’re not doing enough in all the business things.

Here’s the truth – you won’t know exactly what is enough until after you’ve created your result. Then, you’ll have the benefit of hindsight to clearly identify the specific things which created your desired result. Along the way, the best you can do is make your best guess of the specific actions to take 
and the way you’ll need to feel to take action in the most helpful way.

Your results aren’t merely produced by action alone. Anytime we’re thinking, “I’m not doing enough,” indicates we’ve forgotten that the goal-reaching-equation is: 
Useful
 
ACTION + Helpful FEELINGS = Our Desired Results.

Sometimes we don’t need to change the specific actions we’re taking – and instead tweak how we feel as we do those actions.

What do you think?

Let’s take a look at the models happening underneath this model you’ve shared:

You said you’re, “
Needing to do/connect/produce/do more.”

* How will you know when you’ve done more, connected more and produced more?

* What will you make it mean about your life and business when you’ve done those things?

## Model order for intentional thought “I do what I say I will do.”

**Date:** 2024-05-03  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

My question is, do I start with the feeling, action or result I’d like to create when building my new model?

I have “I do what I say I will do.” as the C (or the desired C?) but my unintentional thought is, “if I do what I say I will do, people will take advantage of me.” So, I think the actual C is “I resist doing what I say I will” or “I choose not to be dependable” or “I sometimes miss deadlines/don’t complete projects.”

Feeling- trapped, powerless, then guilty.

Action – drag my feet, self-sabotage, miss deadlines or don’t complete projects (e.g. posting weekly or sending offer emails)

Result – no progress and low confidence.

I think “I do what I say I will do” is the R for a new model? Or a true C rather than the “should” C?

It feels ineffective to start with a new thought before determining a desired feeling or result, but how do you determine which one to fill in first?

[I’d be grateful for the actual coaching and new thought creation, AND I’m more curious about how to work with the model.]

Thank you!

**ANSWER:**

You can start anywhere in the model, depending on what you’re exploring in the moment.

It seems like you want to believe, “
I do what I say I will do.”
  This is a belief that goes in the Result line of your intentional model. It’s an intentional model because you’d explore the actions, feelings and thoughts you imagine you’d need to have to create that belief result. Play around with it – there’s no specific order. It’s not set in stone, because you won’t actually know which thoughts, feelings and actions are accurate until after you’ve created the belief about yourself, “I do what I say I will do.”

(Many times beliefs are in our result line, and it’s helpful to identify them as such – e.g. “I believe I do what I say I will do.” When you look at the list of actions you take, you’ll see that you’ve created more belief from the actions you took. What do you think?)

Your unintentional thought is, “
if I do what I say I will do, people will take advantage of me,”
 yet that doesn’t quite explain what’s happening here. There’s a model happening underneath that one, so let’s explore it.

What are the actions you hesitate to say you’ll do?

How, specifically, will people take advantage of you when you do what you say you’ll do?

## Sitting in an unintentional model? & Creating safety in my body?

**Date:** 2024-01-09  
**Topics:** intentional

**QUESTION:**

This model may seem unrelated, but I read some advice to stay in the unintentional model, and really feel and process that model, before trying to move onto an intentional model, and to do so by concluding every coaching session by creating a sense of safety in my body. So here is the unintentional model I just uncovered through my self coaching:

C: Instragram posts

T: My content isn’t very valuable yet.

F: ashamed/diminished

A: Tentatively posting “crappy” content. Posting a first attempt without reviewing the video. Not watching it or opening Instragram for hours or days afterwards, to prevent feelings coming up around people seeing my “crappy” content.” I imagine coworkers seeing my content and judging me. I dread creating and posting any more content. I forecast continuing to post “crappy” content for years into the future, without making a dime from it, EVER.

R: I create content that isn’t very valuable! I SLOW MYSELF DOWN from getting better at making my content valuable and relevant and impactful!

My question is 1. how do I “sit in” the unintentional model? and 2. how do I create safety in my body?

My beset guesses to the answers are 1. treat it like processing that feeling in the F line. Notice where I feel it in my body. Have compassion for the mammal I am, experiencing that model. Breathe. Not try to rush out of this feeling / change it. Accept the discomfort. Be willing to bring that discomfort along with me for the ride, for hours, days, months, if needed. Much like one would with grief.

2. I was told to remember a time I felt safe… but that feels challenging. So my best guess is breathe, and imagine connecting to my past self, and to my future self, and consider what my future self would say to me, how she would treat me, what she would think of what I’m saying, thinking, feeling, how I’m behaving, etc. She can probably give me exactly the safety however I need it in every moment, the same way I probably can for my past self. <3

**ANSWER:**

Of course, coach – those are fantastic ideas.

And yet, sometimes the source of our pain is a thought error, and continuing to believe it doesn’t move us forward. So let’s take a closer look together.

Anytime we start building new skills, we’re supposed to not do them well. It’s literally the first step of the process that we can’t skip. And it’s not a problem.

Yet this seems to be a problem for you – that your content might not yet be highly valuable, and that you’re going to grow in this skill as you practice, evaluate and try again.

Why do you feel ashamed and diminished when you’re thinking about your content (the beginning stages of this work you’re practicing,) not yet being highly valuable?

What’s preventing you from appreciating yourself for trying something new, appreciating that you’re not taking the easy route of sitting on the couch eating donuts and trolling others who are taking risks, and feeling confident that you’re going to keep growing and improving as you keep trying and evaluating?

## Niche change and mindset drama 6

**Date:** 2026-02-18  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

C- older parents and kiddo with health challenges

T- I have the capacity- I make things happen, I am a dog with a bone,

F-I feel empowerment/ pride that I am able to get stuff done

A- appointments, referrals multiple calls to doctors, providers etc, follow up emails, and more calls, all the things, working on my business, while working a full time job

R- older parents and kiddo are optimized and well connected with many resources and my life is amazing,

It has been a minute. Thank you for the wonderful and deep and thoughtful coaching I have referred back to this coaching many times over the last few weeks and months. I decided that I would not let life circumstances hold my dream hostage. As it turns out, I am very determined to have a successful business and have autonomy over my day to day. I think part of it stems from the sense of lack of control for I am constantly being pulled in different directions , self, kids, parents, business, work and there are times when my brain wants to tell me it is too much. I have decided that I can manage it and it will be temporary and that I will get to come out on the other side and be happy for I will achieve what I wanted for myself, truly for myself. That would mean time freedom and the flexibility.

At this moment,I found that by taking massive action, it beget more action and so I have been taking action non stop since December. Consistent postings, attended networking events, hosted webinars and about to host an in person event. I am currently moving very fast. The idea of stopping and losing momentum is not even an option. Instead I am thinking, there are people waiting for me and I can help them and they may not get the help they need if I slow down and left life decide the next step. Plus I am digging into the belief that I would like to choose as much as I can and not have life decide for me because I did not make a decision.

I think the protection is “choosing the business over the other stuff” and that is where I am with my belief right now. I will do as much as I can give value and put value out there for people are waiting and they need to hear it. As a result, I find myself feeling irritated when life occurs, like it did today and I did not send out emails like I wanted to. Appreciate any coaching and feedback as always

I am still working on time blocking and it is not as consistent. A s a result I do get irritated and resentful when I find my time block interrupted due

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach,

Great job taking a minute and applying the coaching over the last few weeks. And also using the self reflection to make a decision to not let life get in the way and hold your dream hostage. Also noticing you do have autonomy over your day to day.

Powerful thoughts in your model to keep believing.

You have shared a lot that is working and massive action begets massive action.

Let’s look at a few things you said are not working.

“I find myself feeling irritated when life occurs, like it did today and I did not send out emails like I wanted to.”

Let’s dig into the thoughts you are thinking when you feel irritated when life occurs that take you out of your plan to email specifically in those moments.

Looking at them as neutral not right or wrong or true or false. They are on auto pilot. And without internal guidance redirecting them they will take over.

Take some time and list out the thoughts that cause irritation.

T —
T —
T —

Do you notice any pattern to the thoughts?
What do you notice about your thoughts?

Is it a problem that when life happens you feel irritated?

What if you stuck to your schedule when life happens? What would that require?

What are the most predictable things that can happen? How can you anticipate them and still get your work out?

What would that look like? Walk me through how you could make a back up plan.

What would be different if you believed 1000 people were waiting for your email?

“I am still working on time blocking and it is not as consistent. As a result I do get irritated and resentful when I find my time block interrupted.”

What do you think gets in the way of time blocking?

What is the thought behind you ceding control of your time when there are interruptions? Do you like your reason for interrupting yourself?

Is it a problem? Or could there be a better way to respond when you notice interruptions?

How would you respond to this if you already believed you are consistent and this is just who you are, you time block and you are the kind of person that is consistent?

What would you make interruptions mean?

Let’s look at some of the answers you explore and then ask  who do you want to be when you notice life happening?

What would you like to believe about yourself when it comes to your time?

You can keep nurturing the thought “I have autonomy over my day to day.” How is that true even when you are get interruptions from life? The way you talk about your autonomy is either useful or not , how do you think this thought is impacting your experience?

Even if you choose to take care of life things, you do not have to feel resentful. You can choose to be responsible. That will feel less out of your control when you recognize you are choosing to do the life things. What comes up for you with this awareness? Take your time reflecting on this and bring your questions back for coaching.

## Niche Change and Mindset drama 7

**Date:** 2026-02-18  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

The previous coaching reply asked the question, what is it that keep me moving forward? What is the thought? I had said in my last response that it was “helping others” which is true. However, even more powerful below that is the thought ” I am creating the life that I want” That statement has more excitement and more push for me to keep moving in spite of life. I would like to build a life with time freedom and flexibility and the drive for that is very strong as I am getting older, this has become more and more important to me.

And yes, if there are any recommendations for time blocking or making time for CEO hour as I am struggling to build that into my routine. However, it maybe that I am not giving myself permission to block the time. I am thinking that I am missing out fromm time with the kids and family, but even when I am with them, I am distracted for I am thinking about what I am not doing to move my business forward. This is the messy middle and I want to dedicate the time, but I have to give myself permission to take that time without guilt to do the work that is required. Ideally, not the left over time and energy at the end of the day. Thanks as always for your input and recommendations

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach,

Thank you for going deeper with the questions. I love that you caught some more powerful thoughts underneath “helping others.” like, “I am creating the life that I want.” That one has energy. That one has ownership. That one pulls you forward.

Now let’s look at the competing desire you’re noticing.

You want time freedom and flexibility. And you also don’t want to miss time with your kids. At the same time, when you are with them, your brain is half at work.

This is the messy middle. And messy middle is not a problem — it’s a decision point.

When two desires compete, one will win. The question isn’t 
which one is right.
 The question is: Which one do you want to lead?

You asked for time-blocking recommendations. I’ll give them. But first, we have to address the real issue underneath it  permission. Why you think permission is the problem and how to make a decision about your time.

If you fully believed, “Building this business is how I create time freedom for my family,” would blocking CEO time feel selfish? Or would it feel aligned? Why or why not?

I’m asking that because the thought you attach to CEO hour will determine whether you follow through.

Right now it sounds like this:

“If I work, I’m missing time.”

And also,

“If I don’t work, I’m falling behind.”

That’s a no-win loop. Of course you feel split.

So here’s a more useful question:

Are you willing to allow guilt to be there and still direct your brain toward what you truly want?

Guilt isn’t a stop sign. It’s just a sensation. If you wait for it to disappear before you act, you will always hesitate.

The stronger thought  “I am creating the life I want”  needs to become louder than “I might be missing out.” Increasing desire lowers resistance.

Now practically, about CEO hour. All calendaring systems work. The tool is not the issue. The mindset is.

Here’s what I would suggest:

Plan your week in advance. Decide first  when CEO time goes.  Then when it’s CEO hour, it is CEO hour. When it’s family time, it is family time. But this requires mental boundaries, not just calendar boundaries.

When your brain brings you work thoughts during family time, you gently say, “Not now. I have a container for that.”
When guilt shows up during CEO hour, you say, “This is how I create freedom.” You are training your brain.

Let me ask you something important:

What would be the benefit of becoming the woman who trusts her calendar?
Who follows through even when guilt whispers?
Who creates freedom 
because
 she is disciplined with her time?

That version of you has time freedom. Not because she avoided discomfort but because she allowed it.

This is hard. That’s why it’s worth it.

You are not choosing between family and business. You are choosing to become someone who can hold both without fracturing.

Why do you want that version of you to win?

Start there.

## Money mindset continued….

**Date:** 2026-02-11  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

I loved everything you asked. The main thoughts I have when it comes to making 100K as a life coach are that right now it seems so far away. Not in a discouraged frustrated way but based on the fact that I not currently getting consultation. I have finished the modules and am putting in the action. I am loving putting myself out there, meeting people…all the things. But when it comes to making money, I am falling back on the nursing. Sometimes it seems like it too good to be true. But really I am talking about making more that 100K in general. I am super good with money and have no debt, retirement finances in place and all the things. But I want to think like a millionaire. I want to be open to it and make decisions from there. Even as a nurse, I can’t imagine allowing more than 100k into my existence. I think this is where I am comfortable and don’t know how to welcome more into my life. I hope this is more clear (as mud haha).

To answer you regarding coaching as a side gig, I don’t necessarily consider coaching a side gig, but more a career that I have been working on. I am working on getting 1:1 clients, consultations, workshops. Everyday I am not working as a nurse, I am doing something for my coaching business whether it is networking, meeting people or planning an event. And I do introduce myself as a life coach, not a nurse. When I start getting clients and make enough money to peace out of nursing, I will. I could walk away tomorrow! But because it has been a slow roll, it is hard to imagine. Any ideas on visioning, thought work, anything would be very helpful. Have a great day!!

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach,

What you’re really asking is how to imagine making more than 100K and how to start thinking from that place now. That’s a great question, and I want to slow it down a bit because the answer isn’t actually about vision boards or trying to convince yourself of something your brain doesn’t yet believe.

For starters, it helps to remember this: the way you think determines how much money you can create. And right now, 100K feels like the upper edge of what your identity knows how to allow not because you’re  doing anything wrong, but because that number represents what feels familiar and safe.

So instead of trying to imagine 
how
 you’ll make more money, I want you to spend time with the future version of you who already has it.

Not the logistics the 
identity
.

Who is the version of you that makes more than 100K?

How does she carry herself?

How does she talk about money?

What decisions does she make differently?

Who is she being as a coach with a full client list?

I’m asking this because visioning works best when it’s about identity, not outcomes. The way you think about yourself will open up seeing opportunity everywhere.

You shared that when you’re not working as a nurse, you’re working on your coaching business networking, meeting people, planning events, creating workshops. That’s important. Now the next step is evaluation.

How is that going?

Are those actions creating consultations?

If yes, what’s working?

If not yet, what are your theories as to why?

These questions matter because thinking like a higher earner isn’t just about belief  it’s about looking at your actions cleanly and making intentional adjustments, without making it mean anything about you.

Another question I want you to sit with:

What do you think is different about you when you are fully booked?

Who do you help?

What problems do you help them with?

How would you plan your day if you were already that coach?

Because here’s the shift: instead of waiting to become her 
after
 the money shows up, you start practicing being her now.

So I’ll leave you with this: Who is the version of you that shows up in 200K energy today?

And how can you begin becoming her sooner than your brain thinks is allowed? It’s about gently expanding what you’re willing to allow.

Hey coach – thanks for the clarification! That does help. Let’s go deeper into one of the things you said.

But first, I just want to point out something that can be quite powerful if you let it. You talked about how you make 100k as a nurse. That’s great. Something to consider reinforcing into your mind 
(as it relates to money mindset)
 is that your brain has “an advantage” it may not realize yet. And that is, that you already know how it feels to be a six-figure earner. You know how it feels in your body to bring in that amount of income. This means, there’s a piece of your “self-concept” that already has familiarity with earning 100k – it’s just that your brain currently associates that amount of money with your nursing identity. Now it’s just a matter of getting your brain on board with that being your self-concept for your coaching identity.

So when you say: 
“
I am having trouble imagining that it is possible to do 
(make 100k)
 as a coach.”

Share more here.

1. Why/how come?

2. What thoughts specifically do you notice when you think about making 100k as a coach?

Another area to get curious with yourself on (thinking about your self-concept as a coach) is when you shared:

“I want to turn it from
 a side gig
 to an incredible money maker.”

Do you currently think of coaching as a “side gig”?

If so, what might shift in your self-concept if you considered it your full-time job, even though you’re still a nurse?

When people ask what you do for a living, do you say nursing? Or coaching?

Just something to think on as it relates to self-concept and helping to strengthen who/how you show up in your coaching business.

What are your thoughts? Bring your answers back here and we can keep coaching!

## Student Mindset

**Date:** 2026-01-28  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

Hello coach.

I notice I have a student mindset when it comes to consuming courses/information.

I have loved Staceys behind the scenes program. And as it comes to an end I feel myself panic that I haven’t used it to the full.

that I haven’t watched it enough.

that I am missing out when it ends.

This is reoccurring pattern.

I try to practice the thoughts:

– take what you need and leave the rest.

– trust yourself to have taken the knowledge I need at the time.

– what I have learned is perfect for me

– I will recall what I need to in the future.

– I trust myself to find my own way.

But I notice it is not true. I dont trust myself to become as good as her.

I don’t trust myself to have taken what I need

I don’t trust that I willl recall what I will need

and that creates a level of panic and stress and the action that results is I want to re-cram the information.

How do you suggest building the muscle of self trust so I don’t have a consumer or student mindset around programs.

Your thoughts would be much appreciated.

Thank you

**ANSWER:**

Hi coach,

You want to build the muscle of self-trust so you don’t stay in a consumer or student mindset around programs. And you’re already doing something important that I want to highlight, you’re noticing the pattern while it’s happening.

Awareness is always the first step. You’re not failing at trust  you’re seeing where it breaks down for you.

What I want to look at with you isn’t 
how to consume information better
, but why you don’t trust yourself with what you already have.

You’re practicing thoughts like “take what you need and leave the rest” and “I’ll recall what I need when I need it,” but they don’t land because, underneath them, there’s a deeper belief running the show: 
I don’t trust myself to become as good as her.

When you say you don’t trust yourself, I’m curious  not in a vague way, but very specifically 
what has actually happened that proves you can’t take what you need and find your way with information?
Where is your brain going to look for proof that you can’t be trusted with the information?

Another important piece to contemplate here is that trust is a decision. And not deciding to trust yourself is also a decision. So I want to ask  
how is not trusting yourself currently protecting you?
What’s the payoff of believing, “If I don’t cram everything, I’ll fall behind” or “If I don’t absorb it all, I won’t be good enough”?

You’re not just trying to learn from her. You’re measuring yourself 
against
 her. And in that frame, there’s never enough time, content, or certainty. So here’s a different angle to consider. 
What if the goal your trying to accomplish is not to become 
as good as her, 
 but to become 
more solid as you
?

What if you decided that your job isn’t to replicate her  mastery but to trust that 
you are the person your people need to hear from
?

Notice how that shifts things. When you’re trying to become someone else, consumption feels urgent. When you’re committed to being you, application becomes the focus.

If you trusted yourself to handle whatever feelings come up when you apply what you’ve learned possibly confusion, discomfort, imperfection, how would your relationship with programs change? How would your trust build if you were willing to test it?

Because often we don’t distrust our memory. We distrust our ability to tolerate being a beginner.

The way you build trust isn’t by consuming more. It’s by putting yourself in situations where trust is the plan  and letting yourself respond as the version of you who can be relied on.

Small application. Imperfect execution. No cramming.

That’s how the muscle builds. What comes up for you when you hear this?

So the next time the panic shows up, instead of asking, “What else do I need to watch?” try asking:
“What would it look like to trust myself 
right now
?”

That’s where this actually shifts.

## In response to Mindset Work

**Date:** 2026-01-20  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

With my “nurse” hat on, I tell my patients what they have to hear in order to get better or make end of life decisions. I am truthful even when it is painful and a surreal conversation that no one wants to have. I know that I am going to get paid regardless and that I am supported by my manager and the team. I am braver and more outspoken because I have to be. I seem to have a way with words and have been told that I can say things in a way that others wouldn’t “wouldn’t get away with”. I know sometimes the truth hurts. I see the writing on the wall and am very well spoken.

With my “coach” hat on, I feel more timid. I don’t want to hurt my client’s feelings. I fear I might get “fired” or get bad reviews. I feel like I want to be/stay positive instead of honest and this leads me to skirt around issues. So, then I feel tongue tied and I feel my words are jumbled because I am holding back and dancing around what needs to be said.a

**ANSWER:**

Hi Coach,

Thanks for coming back with your answers. Based on what you shared, it is understandable why you feel some hesitation to be more direct when your coach hat is on. As a nurse, you shared, 
“I know that I am going to get paid regardless and that I am supported by my manager and the team.” 
If you were to put your roles into a model, it might look much like this:

C: Nurse Self

T: I am going to get paid regardless. I am supported by my manager and the team.

F: Brave

A: Show up more outspoken with patients. Tell patients what they have to hear in order to get better or make end of life decisions. Show up truthful even when it’s painful. Show up very well spoken. Get feedback that I can say things “others wouldn’t get away with.”

R: Patients are met with transparency. You get a paycheck and feel supported.

C: Coach Self

T: I don’t want to hurt my client’s feelings. I might get “fired” or get bad reviews.

F: Fear/Timid

A: Approach clients more positive vs. honest. Skirt around issues. Show up feeling tongue tied. Words feel jumbled. Hold back. Dance around what needs to be said.

R: Clients feelings are protected but they don’t get the coaching you’re capable of. You don’t get fired or bad reviews.

Just notice what your brain may be trying to protect you from here.

Your brain is doing its best to protect you from any discomfort you may feel if your clients don’t respond to you like your patients do. It also wants to protect you from what you may feel if your client stops working with you or leaves unpleasant feedback. This is something Stacey refers to as “failure ahead of time.” It’s your brain protecting you before you’ve even shown up this way for your clients.

1. What would happen if a client fired you for being transparent like you are with your patients?

What would you make it mean?

2. What if you received a bad review? Then what?

3. Is there a happy medium you can find to be both honest and positive? Truthful and loving? Direct and gentle?

4. Is it also possible that every client may need something different and you are wise enough to feel into that and meet the client where they are?

As a client, there have been times where I’ve needed to hear the hard, honest, painful thing when coached. There have also been times where recieving a more gentle approach has been appreciated. Coaches tend to be good at noticing what may be needed for the client… It’s kind of our superpower – 
what do you think?

How might your experience in healthcare – especially in the setting you’ve worked in, 
serve you
 as a coach?

How might it help you recognize when you can take a more direct approach with certain clients, and when you may want to approach differently?

Keep in mind there doesn’t have to be a “one size fits all” for every client.

How you may approach some of your clients (honest, direct) may not always feel “right” to do with every client and that is also okay.

What would it feel like to trust yourself, your skills, and who you are as both a coach and a nurse?

What do
 you
 think?

What are 
your
 thoughts?

Bring back anything that may be coming up for you as your reflect on this and we will continue to help you.

## Mindset work

**Date:** 2026-01-05  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

Before I became a Life Coach, I have been an ICU nurse for 30 years. As a nurse, I have had the most raw, life changing, surreal conversations with patients. I have dragged them back from the claws of death and wanting to give up on themselves by having hard talks about life and death and having the right mindset to literally fight for their own lives. I have had so many patients and families tell me that I was the turning point for recovery. I know I am very good at this. But when it comes to coaching, I don’t have that same level of confidence and authority as I do as a nurse (YET). I am working on telling myself that I am that same person as a nurse and a coach. But where does that nurse in me go when I am coaching?? I know it will get better with time and practice. Sometimes as a coach I do worry about the client “liking me” and saying the right thing. Is there anything else you can suggest that will help me with this? I have worked the model through with this several times and it does help. Thank you!!!

**ANSWER:**

Hello coach!

It sounds like you’ve had an incredible nursing career that has well prepared you for everything you’ll do as a life coach! It’s quite exciting that your “people skills” can transfer over into what you do now.

You shared that when it comes to coaching, 
“I don’t have that same level of confidence and authority as I do as a nurse (YET).”

1. Why do you think this is?

2. If we go deeper, where does your level of confidence as an ICU nurse stem from do you think? What helps you feel like an authority in that space?

You’re correct in that you’re working on believing you’re the same person as a nurse and as a coach. You mentioned that when you put on your “coach” hat you notice that you want clients to like you and to say the right things…So let’s dig more into THAT.

3. When you have your ICU nurse hat on, do you care that clients like you and that you say the “right” things? If yes, explain. If no, explain.

4. What thoughts do you have when you’re communicating with your patients? List them.

5. What thoughts do you have when you are getting ready to coach clients? List them.

Let’s see how they may differ and what else comes up for you. Take some time to answer each of the questions and bring your answers back to AAC so we can continue to help you! If, as you do these exercises anything else comes up, submit a separate AAC and we can dig into it further.

## Niche change and mindset drama 5

**Date:** 2025-12-02  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

C- older parents and kiddo with health challenges

T- I have the capacity- I make things happen, I am a dog with a bone

F-I feel empowerment/ pride that I am able to get stuff done

A- appointments, referrals multiple calls to doctors, providers etc, follow up emails, and more calls, all the things, working on my business, while working a full time job

R- older parents and kiddo are optimized and well connected with many resources and my life is amazing.

That is my new thought model after listening to Identity shifts and circumstances resistance. I could not find the other two episodes.

The identity shift resonated on many levels for it refers to capacity and I took the capacity workshop and it really resonated with me. The circumstances one made laugh for I have used some of those excuses myself. It brought back so many of the feelings and thoughts that I had about my circumstances and that being in the messy middle. I think a part of me thought that I was going to have to give up on my own dreams, it was a stepping stone. The experiences were building me up to the next level to become the person I would be when my business was fully launched and my primary business and work.

I also realized that I was starting and stopping a lot and losing the momentum as “life” got in the way. And so looking at those now as steps to minimize the lost time. Plus it has me thinking of the ways I can protect myself from those occurrences. I know one of the things is content blocking which I know about but I do not do regularly. When I do it I am always super pleased. But I am not consistent.

Those were takeaways from the episodes. Appreciate any insights or coaching

**ANSWER:**

What you’re describing is truly the work of becoming the version of you who has a full, complex life 
and
 still shows up for your business.
It’s growing your ability to see your circumstances clearly and still see the choices you have:
Do I keep my commitments to myself here? Or do I let life decide for me?

Because for every circumstance we have, someone out there has something similar ,  and they still do the thing.
Not because their life is lighter, but because their thinking helps them move through the weight.

So let’s look at that. You already identified your empowerment thought,  “I have the capacity” ” I make things happen.”
Beautiful. That’s a signal thought. It turns things “on” for you. Every time you think those thoughts you step into empowerment.

Now… when you start and stop, there’s a different thought happening in the background. A different switch getting flipped. The What thoughts stop you and have you step out to empowerment?

What do you imagine someone who keeps going thinks about stopping and starting?
You borrow a new perspective. Sometimes seeing what someone else would think creates clarity about what you want to think on purpose to stay in it.

And what if instead of judging the stopping, you saw it as proof you’re learning to restart faster and with more awareness?
Every restart builds a muscle. Every restart is a rep.
Every restart grows your capacity.

When “life gets in the way,” what do you think those consistent people feel?
I ask because there’s always an emotion underneath the action  and naming it helps you redirect instead of react.

What belief do you have access to about life interruptions that helps you keep moving toward your dreams, even in small ways?
This question matters because you already know you can do hard things you just need a thought that 
brings that version of you
 more often.

Now, about your mention of “protecting yourself from occurrences.”
Let’s get curious with that, because “protection” usually means there’s something we don’t want to feel. What are you meaning by protection from occurrences? Why?

What exactly are you protecting yourself from? 
Other people’s requests?
Having to say no?
People’s opinions about how you spend your time?
Or the discomfort of choosing your business when your brain prefers urgency elsewhere or feel obligated elsewhere?

Knowing 
what
 you’re protecting yourself from tells us what tools you actually need.

Then there’s your time blocking.
You know it works. You like how it feels when you do it.
So instead of aiming for perfect consistency, what if you built consistency like layers? If you’re time blocking twice a week, what happens if you add one more block , just one and let that be enough until it feels natural?
It’s powerful to see the thoughts that make you start… and separately, the thoughts that make you stop.
Once those are clear, you’ll know which thoughts to give more power to and which ones to gently release.

That’s how identity shifts not by chance, but by repetition.
By choosing the thoughts that move you forward more often than the ones that stall you.

Those other podcast were topic suggestions and another one I highly recommend is  The belief plan episode,  is a great support for this exact work. It helps you intentionally choose which identity gets the mic each day.

You’re doing beautifully.
Let’s keep strengthening the version of you who can do both: hold your life and build your business without losing yourself in the messy middle.

quiet confidence
 that was there all along. It makes perfect sense that once you slowed down and paid attention, you saw what you’ve actually been creating. Most people miss that part because they’re busy surviving the circumstances… and you’re finally seeing the impact of what you do inside them.

Can you feel how different things look when the thought shifts from
“This is a lot of work” to
“I am a badass 100%”?

What version of you shows up when you think “I’m a badass” compared to the version who thinks “This is a lot of work”?
—Same circumstance.
—Two completely different leaders.

You mentioned the “magic.”

Circumstance Resistance
 is perfect. Also consider episodes that help anchor belief:

Believing Hard

Identity Shifts

Your Future Self Is Counting on You

## Niche change/mindset drama pt 3

**Date:** 2025-11-07  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

C- new practice with zero patients

T- I get to guide my brain to belief daily

F

A- meeting people, webinar, posting, talking to people,

R

You asked how I want to feel. I want to feel empowered. I want to feel light. I want it to be easy. Though it will not always be easy. I listened to Value Bank 2.0 and that resonated on many levels. I am okay with doing the stuff to add value- posting, meeting people, webinar, etc. However, I think my circumstances in the rest of my life can pull me down and make me doubt and wonder if I am doing too much and if I should wait. Between older parents with health challenges, special needs kiddo and working a full time job. I often have to remind myself that I am doing this to have time , location and financial freedom. I like how she talked about being very intentional about the time that she did put in. I did something similar this weekend. I went to a coffee shop alone for 55 minutes. That was all the time I had and I did a newsletter, worked on posts and worked on my offering. Super productive. However, I also want to show up without thoughts about those things hanging over me, which can happen as I engage on social or with clients. I don’t want to create that “aura” around me of fatigue, desperate, pick me, I need this kind of vibes.

So this is the other model in the mix

C- older parents and kiddo with health challenges

T- this is a lot of work

F-I am tired, resentful ( did not realize this was there ) –> pride as well that I was able to get this done

A- appointments, referrals multiple calls to doctors, providers etc,

R- older parents and kiddos are optimized and well connected with many resources.

Thanks for wonderful insight as always

**ANSWER:**

Let’s lean into empowered. Empowered means you are capable. You’d like it to feel light and easy and you can totally work toward that. It can feel that way. Right now though, it feels like empowered is the most accessible. The easiest to step into.

What would it be like to see how you are empowered during this time when you are balancing all of these things and growing your business? How is it true that you are capable? And what grace do you give yourself right now now while you are building your business?

I want to offer something to you. I bet you already know this too. Our patients/clients can’t always tell what we are thinking and feeling. You can have a really rough day, and they can have no idea. You can be tired, and they won’t even know. Or you can be unsure, and have them think you know exactly what you are doing. Have you had that experience with patients before? Where you aren’t feeling 100%, but you still do a great job with them and they really have no idea any of that was happening for you? Knowing that can take the pressure off. You can be tired and they don’t even know it when you are focused on them and in the moment. It can be the same for those 55 min of work that you did.

## Niche change and marketing/mindset drama

**Date:** 2025-10-31  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

C- new wellness practice with zero patients,

T-they are coming,

F- excitement, nervousness,

A- posting on social media, telling people,

R- new practice with zero patients YET.

This is the model I am working with now. I am posting on social, I am going to do a webinar I am meeting people. I am excited. However, as was asked in my last question, there is also some doubt in the corner of my mind. The what if… no one buys, takes me up, don’t benefit. There is that undercurrent. I have implemented all the practical stuff, but now I really need to clean up my brain and work on my mindset. I want to embody the belief that they are coming, they just need to find me. I am thinking I will need to do daily self coaching around this. Any suggestions including podcast episodes r specific modules to listen to. I am currently listening to clean selling . Thanks

**ANSWER:**

Right now, your brain is doing what every human brain does when you’re creating something new  it’s toggling between belief and doubt. That’s normal.

What if doubt wasn’t a problem at all?
What if it was just a sign that you’re building something that matters?

Your brain is wired to predict “what ifs” as a form of protection. It’s not evidence that something is wrong  it’s evidence your brain is doing its job.

You’re already taking action  posting, meeting people, doing webinars. That’s belief in motion. Staying in belief is useful, and tapping into 
Clean Selling
 is a great way to direct that belief intentionally.

You might also explore a little strategic pessimism asking yourself:

What if they don’t buy right away?

What if it takes longer than expected?
 When you answerer your “what ifs,” your brain can settle down and focus on showing up powerfully now. Create safety by showing yourself you are ok and then can look at what if it works?

Then ask yourself:

How do I want to approach my marketing and selling even when I feel doubt?
This helps you see that confidence isn’t the requirement  commitment is. The only way you don’t create patients is stopping.

Let’s also look at the model you mentioned:

C:
 Zero patients
T:
 “I need to do daily self-coaching.”
F: how does that thought feel?
Does it create pressure, motivation, curiosity insecurity, ?

A: what do you do? what do you not do?

R:  Need to do more self coaching

Sometimes, “I need to self-coach” can subtly come from lack or self-doubt  which leads to more spinning. 
Self-coaching is powerful when it’s intentional, not reactionary.

You might try shifting to: “I get to guide my brain back to belief daily.” That small change creates agency instead of fear of your thinking.

some support tools, I recommend:

Podcast #313 – Believing in Magic
 (amazing for embodying belief)

 (helps you see the results you’re already creating)

## My mindset before the consult

**Date:** 2025-10-24  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

My question: What is it about the initial meeting that scares me?

I have three consults set up for today, and while I’m grateful and excited, I can feel that familiar sense of tiredness and even fear creep in. I work full-time while building my coaching business, so days like this can feel heavy — not just physically, but emotionally too.

When I did a thought download, something interesting came up. A little backstory to explain where this might be coming from: I didn’t date much, mostly because I never liked the early stages of dating. Those first few dates always felt uncomfortable to me. I’m at ease once I really know someone — when we’ve built trust and it feels natural, like they’ve become part of my world.

I’m noticing that same feeling showing up now in my coaching consults, especially with women I’ve just met through networking or haven’t met at all.

So, my question is… how can I shake this nervous energy and just be myself in those first conversations?

**ANSWER:**

You didn’t date a lot – so you didn’t have a lot of experience with first dates.

Is that what’s happening with consults too? It’s normal for us to feel anxious or uncertain when we haven’t done things a lot before.

You have 3 consults set up for today. So it might be that you’ve had lots and lots of consults and that’s not the issue. Either way, what thoughts and feelings do you have going into the consuls that create that nervous feeling?

C consult with new person

T

F nervous.

It might be something around not knowing what they will think of you, since you haven’t built a relationship with them yet. It might be around you not knowing them – you don’t know how they will respond to you or what they will think. What if you DID know them, or at least had a good idea of who they might be because they are people in your audience. You can know your audience. The people who book consults with you likely have some things in common. They are likely a similar sort of person. Who are the people who tend to book consults with you or who tend to reach out to life coaches? What do we know about them?

## Is it my nice or mindset

**Date:** 2025-07-09  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

Your questions about numbers! I need some hard ass coaching on numbers (because it is creating a mindset spin cycle). I am a sign language interpreter. I care about them, there is a HUGE need but can I build a coaching business? sure, I can do workshops here and there, but I don’t want this as a hobby. I don’t want to interpret myself to supplement..

Freelance Sign Language Interpreters make about 50,000-80,00 US. Educational makes significantly less – 40-50,000, depending on the state.. Private/Call Center or Fed Jobs (don’t know if they exist anymore) is about 63,000-90,000 – urban cities you earn much more. Full-time call center work has a HIGH burnout rate. Currently I have a $1500 3-month program (private sessions and membership acces) and a $49.99 membership which has high attrition. I haven’t been selling it because I don’t know if it’s really effective if students don’t come. But that is a catch 22 because they don’t come or join if my energy is m’eh. I’m currently thinking of simplifying to just one offer of 3 or 6 months working privately and then an option to be in the membership. I have $ drama but also they do as well – they don’t know they need this or what they will get from it and it’s an investment. That’s up to me in selling but Stacey does talk about how teachers are hard to sell coaching to. This is why I created the membership thinking the group idea would fly. Then found out so many folks are shy or intimidated – I ask for them to be seen on camera and they don’t want others to see. I sooooo appreciate your tough coaching on whether I am barking up the wrong tree, need to think outside the box of 1:1 traditional coaching or pivot. I might be unsure of many things, but their pain/suffering and how I can help them feel better and not burnout is rock solid. When i don’t get traction or response (I used to get way more engagement and it makes me feel like everyone has moved on ) then I get mad/frustrated at them. NOT GREAT, I know.

**ANSWER:**

We want to keep getting curious about this. Getting into your strategic side and thinking about what you can try and what makes sense to do.

You know this niche really well – you know how much people are making. Do you think that someone making those wages could afford the $49.99/month membership? Why or why not? Think about people you know making that much.

About how many total sign language interpreters are there? You don’t need a million potential clients in order to sign 50, but if there are only 1000 interpreters, it might be hard to fill a membership if you need 300 people to make it viable. I don’t know your numbers, this is just an example for you to think about how many potential clients vs. how many clients you need.

If we look at some of these niches – how might we sell to them? For example, if you know call center burnout is high – would call centers be willing to bring you in if it did reduce burnout? Let’s get creative. You don’t have to do anything like that – but what are some ideas for what you could do.

Expanding your niche could be an option too. Could what you teach interpreters also be beneficial for other problems too? Maybe people who type a lot. Or work with their hands or sew? I’m not sure – but what ideas come up for you?
