## answering your questions about mindset during a launch

**Date:** 2025-02-26  
**Topics:** mindset

**QUESTION:**

The thought that keeps creeping in is that the people you are making this offer too aren’t into it or ready for it.

**YES

Tell me more about that.

Is there a specific reason your mind is thinking that?

**I have had people tell me they can’t afford it.

Or is it just a thought without facts behind it? If there is a specific reason, then we want to dive into that and explore it to find out if it is true or not, so you can adjust what you are doing if you need to.

**I have had students/clients sign up for privates without a question. And I ‘ve had others say they can’t affordi it. But those folks don’t even sign up for the cheaper programs either.

You also want to know what you can do in addition to your plan so far of emailing the group and having these webinars.

What you can do is sell people on the offer. Let them know how it will help them. Address common objections you think they might have. Tell them about the process and what it will be like for them once they are signed up and in the offer. Help them see themselves in the future where they have signed up for your offer and are creating results. Take them through everything you’d do in a consult by doing those things in your marketing too.

**If I can’t get folks to sign up for consults do I just need to approah this like – via my fb grop and email list I need to get it out there. This is helpful…I need to make a list of common objections and hit them one by one.

I have this thought that comes up….I think and know they need me but they don’t – are they willing to put in the time vs just going to a massage table and sitting passively or going ot PT because their insurance covers it. Or they have such big physical issues that really my program isn’t the best for them…they are looing for a magic pill for their 30 year problem.

What do you need to be believing about your offer and your future clients who will sign up for your offer in order to take these actions?

*I need to believe it works if tey put in the work (it doesn’t work unless they move their body and either show up for class or do the recordings). I need to believe it’s like a total game changer And I just had someone tell me that – without asking her to say something – during a webinar. So i have PROOF. I guess not having 10 people running to sign up after a workshop makes me thing alll those other annoying thoughts.

I want this to work as a business sooooo bad!!

**ANSWER:**

Some people have said that they can’t afford your offer.

But others have signed up without any questions.

That means that SOME people can’t afford your offer. And others can.

Which we knew. There will be some people who can’t afford your offer no matter what the price. Or some people who won’t sign up for it no matter what the price is. It just isn’t for them. And there will be people who can afford and do want your offer. That will be true for any offer.

Your mind is thinking you are making this offer to people who aren’t ready or into it. If we really look at who you are making offers to – will some of those people be able to afford it? What do you think?

You need belief in your program.

You need belief in your clients. There are people who want this and will do the work. There are lots of those people.

You need belief in yourself. You can figure this out.

When you have belief in those 3 things – how do you show up today in your business? What are posts or emails like? How do you think about and talk to your clients?

When people aren’t signing up – you troubleshoot and evaluate why. Why aren’t they signing up? You have to answer that logically and then solve for it each time. It’s not that you can’t sell this offer or they don’t want it. You might be talking to the wrong people, not saying what they need to hear to sign up or not showing up really in those 3 beliefs.

 which indicates you might not believe it based on the recent non-renewals and a consult no.

## Stay or quit

**Date:** 2026-05-20  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

I’ve been coaching for about 3 years now, mostly alongside a full-time role because I can’t leave my job yet due to visa constraints. Recently I’ve been offered a full-time leadership opportunity that has nothing to do with coaching, but professionally it’s something I’ve genuinely wanted for a long time. It feels exciting and aligned with my corporate growth goals.

At the same time, I’m having a real moment of doubt around coaching.

I’ve love coaching, but the business side of it, especially in the last few years has felt incredibly hard. Getting clients feels like constant lead generation, LinkedIn activity, partnerships, outreach, and always needing to create momentum. I feel like I’m so behind other coaches – who are also working full-time as coaches. Defnitely, I’m spending more energy trying to get clients than actually coaching.

Lately I’ve also noticed myself comparing myself a lot to other coaches, especially 1-2 coaches in particular who have a similar background to mine, and cater to the same audience. Every time I see their posts, I feel triggered and insecure. Here they go taking my clients (in fact, I know I lost 2 client to them because the clients told me). Intellectually I try to remind myself that there is enough to go around and that someone else’s success doesn’t take away from mine, but emotionally I don’t fully believe it.

I’m starting to feel defeated. I feel caught in comparison and questioning whether I’m behind or whether I’m simply forcing something that’s becoming harder and harder to sustain.

Now with this new full-time role, I’m considering pull back for a few months so I can focus on the employment. But that also brings up a lot emotionally. It feels like giving up, or losing momentum, or questioning whether all of this effort was worth it.

I think I’m sitting in a place of: what’s the point? Why keep pushing? And I want help understanding whether this is burnout, comparison, fear, or whether something deeper is trying to tell me something.

**ANSWER:**

Hi Coach,

You’re asking really important questions here, and I want to start by saying thank you for being so honest and giving context helps so much for us coaching you and for you to see how much suffering this adds to the doubt and hard.  I want to offer you so much compassion we see you.

When your brain keeps layering “this is hard,” “I’m behind,” “maybe it’s not working,” on top of disappointment and comparison, it will naturally create despair. Heavy thinking creates heavy emotion. That doesn’t mean something has gone wrong  it means your mind is spending a lot of time focused on threat, lack, and uncertainty. And honestly, it makes complete sense that your brain is doing that that is what it’s looking for.

Right now, your brain is spending a lot of energy watching these other coaches and interpreting their success as evidence against you. But notice the sentence: “They’re taking clients from me.” I want you to really slow that thought down because your thoughts matter here. Especially if those clients were never actually yours in the first place, how can someone take something that wasn’t yours yet? Where they working with you and then they went to them?

That doesn’t mean it doesn’t sting if you lost them or they chose them, your feelings are true.  But there’s a difference between disappointment and making someone else’s success mean you’re losing.

Your brain is wired to scan for competition and protection first. That’s what brains do. So don’t judge yourself for comparing. Instead, get curious about how you’re using comparison. Because comparison can either fuel inspiration or fuel despair.

You can look at another coach and think:

“They’re proof this won’t work for me.”Or “They’re proof this can work.” Same circumstance and completely different emotional outcome.

And I also want you to notice something else right now comparison is pulling your focus away from your own business. Your attention is becoming externally directed. Instead of asking, “What do I want to create?” your brain keeps asking, “How do I measure up compared to them and how long in business?”

And I think it’s important to explore this gently: what are you getting from repeatedly checking their content, comparing, judging, spiraling? Because if your brain keeps returning there, there’s some payoff even if the result is pain afterward. Sometimes it’s validation for evidence for “See? I’m behind.”

Sometimes it’s avoiding focusing on your own next move. Is that true? If so why do you think you return there vs work on your next moves?

Not because you’re doing anything wrong but because the brain will often choose familiar discomfort over uncertain growth.

I also want to offer this carefully: making a huge decision while you’re deep in discouragement usually creates unclear decisions. Your thinking will always drive your decisions.

So before deciding whether to fully pull back from coaching or go all in on the corporate role, can you first work on getting yourself into a more grounded and honest emotional place? Not a perfect place  just a clearer one.

Because a decision made from, “It’s not working” feels very different than a decision made from: “This is what I genuinely want.”

You asked if taking the role means giving up. I don’t think the pain is necessarily in the decision itself  I think it’s in the story you may tell yourself about the decision. A pivot is not automatically failure, season of focusing elsewhere is not automatically quitting, and continuing coaching while feeling negative emotions doesn’t mean courage either, The way you think about it does.

This is why I’d ask, If you knew you could be successful, fulfilled, and happy either way… what would you choose? Why?  That question matters because it removes some of the fear and scarcity from the equation and lets you hear yourself more clearly. I’d also ask: What do you think you’d regret more?

And be careful not to weaponize this experience against yourself and decide it “wasn’t worth it.” Regret is optional. Three years of coaching doesn’t become meaningless because your path evolves. What if part of the value was who you became while building it? Your resilience, self-awareness, communication, courage, leadership?  Those things don’t disappear because your direction changes.

You also asked such a thoughtful question:

“Is this burnout, comparison, fear, or something deeper trying to tell me something?” I think that’s exactly the right place to explore. Explore those questions answering them for your own discovery.

But emotions don’t tell us truth automatically  they point us toward thoughts worth investigating.

So instead of labeling the feeling, I’d get curious about the thinking creating it.

What thoughts create the burnout? Maybe:

“It’s been 3 years and I should be further along.”

“I’m behind.”

“This shouldn’t be this hard.”

What thoughts create the fear?

“If I stop, it means I failed.”

“If I keep going, maybe I’ll never catch up.”

And what thoughts create the comparison?

“Their success means less opportunity for me.”

Notice how each feeling is attached to interpretation. If your coaching business was currently growing quickly and creating momentum would you still interpret fear, comparison, and exhaustion as signs you’re on the wrong path? Or would you call them “part of the process”?

Fear and burnout do not automatically mean you’re headed the wrong direction. Sometimes they’re asking for a different pace, better boundaries, clearer thinking, more support, or a reconnection to why you started in the first place. And sometimes they’re inviting you to evolve.

Great work here coach exploring this here.  Feel free to come back if you need anything further or coaching on what comes up here.

---

## Changing a habit of quiting

**Date:** 2026-02-09  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

Hello,

How do I go about changing my habit of quitting on myself. Starting and stopping. I have fears of not being good enough, not knowing how to coach if I myself didn’t achieve any breakthrough yet, my life didn’t dramatically change. I’m noticing watching my brain more but, how can I ask someone to trust me to deliver them results yet not having achieved any results myself? I feel like I’ll never get there.

**ANSWER:**

Hi coach,

First, let’s slow this down for a moment. Take a breath with me. Before we try to “fix” the habit of quitting on yourself, I want to get us into compassion  because habits don’t change through pressure or judgment. They change through understanding.

You shared something really important here:
“How can I coach if I haven’t had a breakthrough yet? My life didn’t dramatically change. How can I ask someone to trust me to deliver results when I don’t feel like I have results myself?”
That tells me the quitting isn’t the real problem  it’s what you’re making your progress (or lack of it) 
mean
 about you.

So let’s get curious about the habit itself, not to criticize it, but to understand why it’s there.

When you say you “quit on yourself,” what does that actually look like?
What do you stop doing  specifically?
And if you weren’t quitting, what 
would
 you be doing instead?

I’m asking this because habits don’t exist in a vacuum. We don’t stop and start randomly. We stop when a thought shows up and creates a feeling we don’t want to feel. The action (or inaction) is always the last step.

Help me understand this part:
Why do you believe you haven’t achieved results or that your life hasn’t changed?
In what ways 
have
 you changed  even subtly  since you started watching your brain?

Often we dismiss progress because it doesn’t look dramatic enough, but internal change is usually quiet before it becomes visible. That doesn’t make it insignificant it makes it foundational.

I also want to come back to this question about coaching.
What comes up for you when you imagine delivering results for someone else while telling yourself you haven’t achieved results yet?

This matters because coaching isn’t about being “done” or having a perfect life. It’s about understanding the process especially the part where the brain doubts, resists, and tells you you’ll never get there. That’s not a disqualification. That’s actually the work. How can you find what you have had a breakthrough?

And finally, I’m curious  why did you want to coach in the first place?
What was the pull for you?

Let’s start by getting clear on what’s really happening underneath the quitting. Bring your answers back here, and we’ll keep coaching from there.

You’re not behind. You’re in the middle  and that’s a very different thing.

---

## How do I quit using what I haven’t done against myself and get back into motion?

**Date:** 2025-09-19  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

How do I quit using what I haven’t done against myself?

I keep using how many consults I’ve had and how much money to determine what I think about my business. I’ve had my business for 3 years. Year one made 18k, year two 12k, this year thus far 9k. I’ve had 3 consults this entire year. I haven’t posted on social for a year. I haven’t been showing up as a coach when I’m meeting new people. I’ve just taken referrals and asked for referrals from them and waited for them, telling myself this just feels better for me. BUT deep inside I want more. I’m jealous of other’s successes because I want my own. I am not doing the work to create the results I want. I pushed hard the first 2 years, but was stressed and wanting it so bad without creating it. So, I stopped marketing to get back to what felt better.

I want to help people and make money and I am here showing up again in this space to apply. I’m going to focus back in, coach myself, come to ask a coach etc. My mind is uncoached. Some T’s I have:

I don’t want to do the work

It doesn’t happen for me

I can’t seem to figure it out

I’m lazy with marketing

Social Media didn’t produce any clients (not a fact)

I’ve been trying for 3 years and stayed the same

I’m slow at this

C: 0 Consults on the books

T: Let’s make it happen (create consults)

F: Optimistic

A: Showing up in the 2k space, remembering to show up as a coach, thinking about how I can meet people, coaching myself today

R: Creating movement to booking consults

**ANSWER:**

This is what you are wanting to shift: “I keep using how many consults I’ve had and how much money to determine what I think about my business.” You want to stop using the past and what’s happened in the past against yourself.

Let’s start by looking at what you have learned so far. If you think about back to 3 years ago – how have you grown? What are you better at now or what do you know now that you didn’t 3 years ago?

We want to do this so you have some other options for how to think about the last 3 years. It wasn’t just you not showing up. Or you not creating results. It was also you SHOWING up, you growing, you making money, you having clients and all the learning/experience that came with that.

And then – we can also look to what you want now. If we use how you’ve grown, what do you want for yourself in your business for the next few months until the end of the year? What goal could you set and feel proud about? A goal you can focus on instead of looking back.

## Client quitting

**Date:** 2025-03-10  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

Such an interesting day. I had a 6-month client resign in advance at double my rate. Paid in full already. Awesome! 🥳

And a new client who has attended 2 out of 4 sessions so far and just sent me the below reply to my message confirming our session on Friday.

(The second session she attended she was late to and then said she needed to go eat so we didn’t have the full hour.)

“Hi, daycare is closed on Friday so I’m actually unable to meet then. I think I need to place our work together on hold at this time. I am so far behind on deadlines and my son’s appointments and sick days keep stacking up. I simply don’t have the capacity to commit to this right now.”

For context: She’s paid for the first month. Contract states that 30 days’ notice is required to end the coaching.

I want to get in a clean space before responding. What’s clouding it for me is that I don’t want to try and drag someone along if they aren’t ready or willing to be committed to coaching.

I don’t need the money, I’m technically fully booked, and I’m confident I can sign a new client to replace the spot if I choose to.

But I also know that the reasons she’s given are reasons TO commit to and prioritise coaching! I know that if she does that then she’ll make great progress. To me, the message comes from a part of her that wants to keep her safe by staying where she is, and I feel like it would be a disservice not to point this out 🤔

I also have the thought if I put it on hold I doubt she’ll ever come back to it.

I’m thinking about the belief triad here too and perhaps she doesn’t believe in her ability, the coaching process and/or me as a coach. Though of course she did make the decision initially to sign up.

Grateful for any coaching & insights you can share to help me get in a clean space 🙏

**ANSWER:**

You know that you don’t need the money and you can bring in other clients. You always want to check in on that first because that can get in the way of really serving and replying in clean energy to this client.

Think about the results she wants to create. Knowing what you know, are the other things she mentioned more important? Is working on the results she came to you for in the way of her also doing those other things?

You also shared some great insight on what you think might be happening for her. You could offer a shorter call to talk about how long to put coaching off for so you can talk this through with her. That will help you know if this is a fear or if she really just isn’t in a space to do this work right now. If she isn’t open to the call – she likely has made her decision.

And you also know it’s okay if she does decide now isn’t the right time. You are okay either way.

## Quitting my job 3

**Date:** 2024-11-25  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

What’s keeping right now from signing them is I just really started building the momentum in August. Up until that point all I was doing was weekly posts. Since then I’ve been having (4-5) coaching sessions with different people weekly. So I’m constantly getting new people in my circle, some are very interested in coaching but is new to it so I think they need more nurturing or touch points.

If I quite my job I’d spend the time creating more videos and in person networking. I’d book more free coaching sessions to build more connections and do them everyday vs once or twice a week. I’d spend quality time studying coaching material vs. Just listening to podcasts.

Were also starting a family business in January so I’d also use that time to build up customers for that business as well.

I’m constantly thinking of my business. While I nanny I create posts, nurture clients via messenger, taking note of ideas or listening to trainings, coaching etc. I love working on coaching so I do feel like any chance I do get I work on my biz. My time constraint is having a part time job working 3-4 days a week, kids practices after work during the week and then tournaments some weekends.

I did think about just taking a 1 week vacation during January to push it really hard to get some paid clients instead of quitting that month.

**ANSWER:**

You are wanting to have more time to work on your business – and you know what you’d be doing.

Sometimes when people quit their jobs – it’s so overwhelming and stressful, and the pressure has them shut down or show up in ways that are desperate feeling.

That’s what I was wanting to check for to make sure that you didn’t seem like that’s what would happen for you. And it doesn’t sound like that. It does sound like that extra time would be useful for you to work more on your business.

Let’s also ask – is there a rush feeling to this? What if there is no rush and you could quit in 6 months instead of right now? What comes up?

Responding to my last coaching

The math (finances) doesn’t make sense on my part logically. It would be a risk. I got (1) paid in full client in October so far, but before that I’ve been going on and off as a coach. So I haven’t consistently been booking clients.

What do you think is keeping you from signing more clients now before you quit?

If you quit your job tomorrow, do you know exactly how you’d spend that day working on your business or is there some uncertainty there?

## Quitting my job

**Date:** 2024-11-20  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

I’ve been thinking for awhile about quitting my job to go all in and get clients so I can coach part time and eventually full-time. I’ve been trying go build my coaching for 3 years off and on, but the past 5 months I’ve been building a lot of momentum.

Time is a constraint as I work 32 hours a week. I just need (1) client paid in full a month to make what I make now.

Part of my brain really wants to go all in for 3 months as I quit my job and dedicate all my time in building my coaching practice.

But another part of me is scared to quit my job even though I know it wouldn’t be hard to find another as I am a nanny.

I’ve done the facts, and it would put is in a really difficult position financially which is why I’m scared.

**ANSWER:**

You need 1 paid in full client a month to make what you make now. Let’s explore this more together.

Have you been signing 1 paid in full client a month for awhile? What’s been happening?

How many clients do you have right now? And how long is your program? We want to look at the math and see if it’s possible for you to sign some clients now before you leave so you have that money. That can make it easier. Can you do both for a bit? Why or why not?

For some people, when they are stressed they shut down. Putting themselves in a tight spot works against them. For others – they like the pressure and it helps push them. What works best for you usually?

Are there other options too – like could you take a leave from your work? Or could you work a few days a week? You get to do this in a way that works for you. There really are so many options as you transition from where you are now to being a full time coach. It can be step by step if that works best for you.

## Client Quitting

**Date:** 2024-11-14  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

Let’s look at what happened when that client quit mid package. You said that since then it seems like your belief has taken a hit. Why did that client quit mid package? What are you making them quitting mean? Tell me all your thoughts about it.

She quit mid-package because she didn’t feel like she was getting the results that she came for. She came to me for weight loss and I was direct from the beginning that the way I approach weight loss is through mindset and self-image which then drives actions. As we worked together she seemed to want more meal plans and for me to tell her exactly what to do, which is not how I coach.

While ultimately parting ways was the right choice for both of us, I think I made it mean a lot of things. I began to question my coaching and what I help people with. I came from a background in group fitness and network marketing with beachbody, and while I believe in healthy habits, physical fitness and nutrition aren’t really what I am passionate about coaching on.

Somewhere in my mind I have the thought that people want health coaching and that those tangible results are easier to sell, but that’s not what/who I want to serve. Then who or what do I want to serve? I am more about relationships…your relationship with yourself, with others, and with your environment/consumption (food, alcohol, social media, TV, shopping etc) but I don’t know how to market that.

She came to me naturally/on her own just based on how I was showing up in the world (sharing my workouts and inspiring messages) and I’m no longer sharing those things and I don’t really know what to share.

**ANSWER:**

You know that you help with weight loss through mindset and self-image which then drives actions. You don’t give meal plans or start with the action part.

For this client, that wasn’t what she thought she needed. Why do you think that was? Did she really need a meal plan to lose weight? Why wasn’t she losing weight?

No matter how great your marketing is, sometimes people will sign up that don’t end up being a fit. It’s like how you might return a pair of jeans to a clothing store. It doesn’t mean the jeans are bad – they just don’t fit you. Was this client a one off situation or have you had a lot of people come to you and they ended up wanting things you don’t offer? We want to make sure we don’t re-arrange your whole business based off of one person.

## My client wants to quit PART 2

**Date:** 2024-10-24  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

My client’s goal is to sign more clients. It’s been one year since she signed a client. Then she signed her first client 11 weeks into working with me.

1.Why do you think she hasn’t signed more clients in these past 3 months?

We were working on building her belief in herself as a coach, and showing up strongly and confidently. She began posting more regularly on socials and then started doing lots of free calls (to build the belief). Then we were working on making offers and how the consultation process goes. Simply- she is learning all the micro skills of being a great coach and building her business.

What would need to change for her to sign more clients?

To stick with what we are working on and keep being consistent with it, as we continue to build on her ability to make strong offers, and close consultations.

Let’s also look at where you are at with signing clients. If she quits, do you know that you can sign another client this week? Why or why not? Yes, I can. I am fully booked already and have a wait list.

Based on what I have shared above, how would I be framing the call on Thursday? Do I help her evaluate where she has been, what is working/what is not working and how she believes she will get there?

**ANSWER:**

Based on what you wrote, you know that you and her have built that foundation and now as long as she “sticks with what we are working on and keep being consistent with it, as we continue to build on her ability to make strong offers, and close consultations” that she’ll be signing clients. She’ll grow her business. You have that belief in her and in the process. You know how it works.

She doesn’t have that belief. That’s why she’s wanting to quit.

Your call on Thursday is about you bringing that belief for her. It’s about you showing her how far she’s come and that she’s so close and just needs to keep going.

You already, by answering these questions, have evaluated what’s happened so far. You know she’s right on track. What you want to do is hold belief for her and coach on her hesitations. We want to get curious about what’s happening for her. Helping her see she’s right on track and allowing her to talk through her doubts about making money.

You want to see where you both agree too. You also don’t want her spending all of her earnings on coaching. And you know that isn’t what will happen from what you said above. She’s going to fill up with clients. This 1 client was just the start.

We also want to drop any judgment. You see this pattern where she goes from course to course. We want to get curious about that. What’s it protecting her from? She’s a coach – but in her coaching relationship with you she’s a human. She can have all these thoughts and you can help her start to see what her thoughts/patterns are creating.

## Client wants to quit

**Date:** 2024-10-23  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

I have a client that signed on for a 6 month package. She has done 13 sessions so far and her next monthly payment is due in 2 days. She just sent me a message saying she only had 3k to invest initially and (What she has paid so far) and that she isn’t going to coach anymore. Before we began the coaching she shared that she had 3k and that she would need one client before her next payment was due to keep paying me (she is a coach too). She signed a $2500 client two weeks ago. Now she is saying she needs more than 1 client, and that she doesn’t feel right about investing all that money back into coaching before she’s made more money. Then she said she wanted to do another coaching program that would cost her $1,000.

My initial unintentional thoughts: #1 She signed an agreement to complete the whole package- and now she’s quitting. What is the point of an agreement?

#2 She is a coach- why is she acting like this?

#3 I have observed her (as a colleague) jump from program to program over the years and not get the results she wants- she seems to be doing that now too.

We have a call on Thursday (her last paid up call) and I told her we could talk about her wanting to quit on the call. She texted back that she won’t be convinced, that she wants to make more money and then she will come back to 1:1 etc. So on Thursday I want to be neutralized and help coach her through this. But then I see myself wanting her to see that she paid me $3000 and I helped her get her first client at $2500 and that she tends to leave programs for the next flashy thing. But the energy right now inside me isn’t helpful- I want to be unattached but help her fight for her dream of being a coach with a full roster. Please help me.

**ANSWER:**

Let’s get into a more objective place by evaluating her results so far.

Her goal is to sign more clients.

Why do you think she hasn’t signed more clients in these past 3 months?

What would need to change for her to sign more clients?

Looking at that is going to help you help her whether she continues with you or not. Getting really clear on what’s keeping her from her goals will help her. It’s going to have you showing up more confidently no matter what her decision is.

Let’s also look at where you are at with signing clients. If she quits, do you know that you can sign another client this week? Why or why not?

## Ready to quit

**Date:** 2024-06-07  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

You’ve changed your niche a number of times and haven’t created the clients you’ve wanted – yet you think niches create results? Why is that?

I change niche after weeks and months without investing any time and effort into it. It’s not niche’s that create results it’s my ability to understand the model the client is in and slowly help them shift models, which I am in the beginning stages off after 4 years of being a certified coach. I’m not progressing or learning. This is concerning

Yes, telling people you help women lose weight and reverse prediabetes are specific results that most people understand.

Saying, “I help women of color break the chains of the past and help them create futures different from the ones handed down to them,” doesn’t tell people a specific result they’ll create from you. It’s much higher level – an idea, instead of a concrete result.

If you said, “I help women overcome their past and create new health futures for themselves,” it would be a higher-level idea.

When you help women achieve results that are significant to them, you ARE helping them break generational patterns. Yet it seems you think problems like pre-diabetes aren’t significant generational patterns that they would be grateful to break. What do you think?

I’m using it to cover up for the fact that I actually can’t coach anyone to a result because my mind trips up and I can’t stick to one thing. I go off on tangents in questions, in building models. I am hiding my insecurity of my lack of skill as a coach and unfortunately this seems like a skill I can’t develop because if I can’t get people on a consult call I can’t learn to do the before and after models and learn to bridge the gap. And saying big things like I break generational patterns makes me feel important but reality is a coach can help clients creates results and any result can be breaking a generational pattern.

From a realistic place being a diabetes prevention coach meets a real need that people are willing to pay for. But just because they want the solution doesn’t mean I can provide it to them. Ugh I can’t do this. All I see are blocks. I can’t figure out how to even progress a little bit. I am ready to quit and just go back to my day job.

**ANSWER:**

Let’s take a look at your coaching skills.

Right now you are feeling like you aren’t progressing as a coach and that you can’t help your clients create results. And you aren’t sure how to work on your coaching skills.

You shared: “my ability to understand the model the client is in and slowly help them shift models, which I am in the beginning stages off after 4 years of being a certified coach. I’m not progressing or learning. This is concerning.”

If you look at the last 3 months, what do you think has kept you from feeling like you are progressing as a coach? Let’s make a list of all the thoughts so we can start to practice some models on them and see where you are at.

We also want to develop a plan for working on your coaching skills so that you ARE progressing as a coach. One of the things that you are feeling stuck on is being able to see a model and help your clients shift out of that model. Are you doing models on yourself? That is one way that you can practice coaching and getting more comfortable with the model. You can also bring your models here too.

We can also practice client models.

Let’s look at one of those examples of how you might help – with diabetes prevention. What actions can contribute to someone getting diabetes and not preventing it?

Then let’s work backwards. What are they thinking and feeling that has them taking those actions? What are some examples?

C goal: prevent diabetes

T

F

A actions client’s take that increase their risk for diabetes

R the client increases the chance that they have diabetes

## Overcoming doom-and-gloom thoughts when clients quit early

**Date:** 2024-06-04  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

I just “lost” another client (3rd in a row), who quite early, and I’ve gone into a head spin and drama.

I had just decided that 2024 would be the last year I’d work full-time in corporate so I could start coaching more hours with a view to becoming a full-time coach asap. My confidence & optimism were up.

I’ve been posting more regularly on social lately, I’ve been interviewed for a podcast relevant to my niche (not live but they’ll be quoting me). I’m more visible than ever before and being perceived by my public as an “expert”. Then this happens. I’m gutted!

Because of my day job, 5-6 clients is the most I can take. Earlier this year I did have 5, but 3 out of those quit early, after about 4 sessions (out of 12), because they weren’t sure what they wanted to work on or if coaching was helping. They never had a clear goal.

The two that remain have paid in full (the others only paid me partially) and are 100% committed. Their pain points are also much deeper and defined.

In my heart of hearts, I know “nothing has gone wrong”, as Stacey says. Maybe this is “natural selection”. The non-ideal clients are peeling off naturally to make room for “ideal” clients. I’m telling myself I don’t need to panic, as this is just a phase (low cycle?), but there’s also a part of me screaming in frustration.

This is what’s happening in my brain.

C – 3 out of 5 clients quit early.

T – “Clients don’t value me as a coach”. “I’m failing as a coach.” “I wasn’t able to help them.” “I’ll never be a full-time coach.” “Coaching will always just be a side hustle.” “I’m not cut out to be an entrepreneur.”

F – Heartbroken, despondent (about the future), self-doubt.

A – I stop making offers, stop posting on social. I hide. Give off desperate energy.

R – No new clients. Zero $$.

The intentional model I want is:

C – some clients quit early.

T – “Nothing’s gone wrong.” “More ideal clients are coming.” “How great that I now have room for the ideal clients who’re coming.” “New clients are coming with a burning passion to be coached.” “My diary is full of ideal clients who can’t wait to work with me.”

F – Relieved. Curious and excited about what’s to come next.

A – I become more visible, make more offers. I don’t spend any energy thinking about the clients that quit.

R – Lots of great new ideal clients.

But it feels like too big a leap from the current Model to this one. What do I need to work on in the interim please?

**ANSWER:**

In your first model you are making these clients quitting mean that they don’t value you as a coach, you are failing etc.

But you also told me that the clients that have quit didn’t have clear goals. There wasn’t clarity around their goal or how coaching would help them get there.

If you look at those clients, what would you do differently next time? How could you establish some clear goals even if they client is feeling unsure? Why do you think they signed up for coaching even if they weren’t sure what they wanted to work on?

Let’s do that work first and then see if an intentional model that feels more believable comes once you’ve processed and evaluated why clients have left.

---

## How do I stop feeling mad about a client that quit early and wasn’t upfront about it?

**Date:** 2024-04-22  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

Earlier in the year I lost one client, after session 4, who stopped booking sessions after a few months’ break, allegedly because she had to “sort out her finances”. Basically she ghosted me. As she was paying in 3x instalments for a 12-session container, I only got paid 1/3. I reckon she realised coaching wasn’t what she needed. Every session she had no clue what she wanted to be coached on! I was sad but even a bit relieved in her case.

Then last week I had a 2nd client tell me, after session 4, that she couldn’t do the next 2 weeks, she had no outstanding issues atm, etc. She left it vague as to when or if she’d return in May. Prior to that she’d taken a 4-week break over Easter. Something felt off, so I followed up and asked her by email to pay her 2nd instalment to secure her place if she was taking a break. Only then did she confirm she didn’t need to be coached any longer for now but she had got a lot out of the sessions she’d had.

I didn’t mind that she didn’t want to continue, as she’d had big shifts, but I felt triggered that she didn’t tell me so in session. I’d have done a general reflection and asked her for feedback had I known it would be our last time. This client also only paid me 1/3 of her fee.

I tell all my clients at the start that it’s okay to finish early, with a week’s notice, but it seems like they seem to find it awkward to be upfront about that. I hate the vague excuses they give me! It doesn’t feel “clean”!

I’m mad about two things. I need your help with no.2 as it’s making me have self-sabotaging thoughts:

1) my payment plans are too generous + leave me financially vulnerable. Rather than letting clients pay before each block of 4 sessions, I need to ask them to pay every month on a set date, regardless of breaks.

2) I’m mad that my clients are not being upfront about wanting to quit early, and disappointed in myself that I didn’t create a safe enough container in which they could be more honest.

I have 3 more ongoing/satisfied clients but need to replace the lost clients.

C: client quitting early

T: they’re not being upfront + I failed to create a safe container?

F: heartbroken, disappointed, angry, vengeful (argh)

A: I’m tempted to write her a tart email saying ok but she needs to stop people pleasing and be more upfront with everyone because that kind of energy doesn’t feel clean

R: they’ll probably feel lectured, judged and hurt and may never come back for more coaching or refer me to others

**ANSWER:**

Let’s get curious about why you think they should be upfront. That’s going to help you see what’s going on here so you can process some of those feelings.

What would change for you if they were upfront?

What would you want them to say?

If we look at that feeling of heartbroken – tell me more about that feeling. Why is it heartbreaking that they left how they left? What makes it heartbreaking?

## Client quits

**Date:** 2024-03-19  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

Some words client said about my group program.

“On one hand, I have the impression I took on myslef all your fears and negative thhoughts (I am an emptah, I sensed them I guess) and I was the physical manifestation of them.

I believed I will make the 5K in the first month and pay the rest of the program in full at the end of first month, this was the goal I declared since the first call.

I can’t continue unfortunatelly if it does not work, if I don’t get results. It’s been 4 months since the 5K in 5Days challenge.” *note the program started in Feb so it’s been just 6 weeks of the PAID program

My thoughts: these are all her thought errors this is good info for the next time I run this group (background thought: this is very annoying, she has tangible results bc she was not booking any consults before she stared with me. She’s had more consults in the past month then in the past year – she got results)

F: logical (then judgmental / then hurt heart)

A: think about how to communicate with the rest of my group. 2 ppl quit and the other 2 ppl I’m moving to 1:1 , I kind of want to tell them hey I’m in the middle of a public failure, but that doesn’t mean that YOU can’t get results, just notice what judgments come up for you about me as your coach – notice if your perfectionism is kicking in and wanting to judge the delivery of this program, of course you can provide feedback and of course there’s always things that can improve about any program – but don’t let that get in the way of your results, for example I went into a program and had an absolute meltdown after the first meeting, but I never ever considered quitting because I always get what I came for, for me I always admired people who were willing to put themselves out there imperfectly and were willing to fuck it all up in order to help more people, so we are switching both of you to 1:1 coaching and we can definitely coach on any thoughts you have about the program, and ME! Don’t let that get in that way of your results. Last year I made $160k and the entire year I thought the coach hated me! I thought she was mean! I thought everyone in the group hated me.And didn’t care about me. I asked myself how that was serving me and got to work on my deeper beliefs about myself

-hire a client success person to help me with all the feedback and onboarding for the next group of people

R: transition clients to 1:1 and get them even better results, work to do an eval on this program for relaunch in July

**ANSWER:**

First, it’s important to have a model with a single feeling, so you can see what specific actions you take and the results you create from that one original thought.

Once we’re in a feeling, it sometimes creates a loop of other feelings that we normally wouldn’t experience. That’s why we need the clarity here.

Clients quitting it part of running a business. And how you handle it and what you learn from it will determine the results you create moving forward.

You know how to take radical responsibility regardless of your coach – yet that’s not always a skill others possess. You can teach them that with how you handle it – and it doesn’t come from telling them…but showing them.

If you consider their client experience, and if you believed you had the same thing, what would you want the coach to do? (As a coach with integrity?) What needs to be done here?

* Do you need further communication with the clients who have quit? If so, what? And why?

* The clients who are staying, whom you’ve switched to 1:1 – does anything more need to be said? If so, what? And why?

Anytime we’re experiencing something painful – that we consider a public failure – it’s important to give it proper perspective. Much like instead of being in traffic in a busy city, you climb to the top of a nearby mountain and see the city from below. Consider your future-self’s opinion of this experience. Where is she? What has she learned? What would she tell you about this season of your business? How would she support you?

How do you feel when you consider hiring a client success person?

* Do you enjoy fueling your life and business with that feeling fuel? Why or why not?

## Please help me not to quit – Part 3

**Date:** 2024-01-08  
**Topics:** quit

**QUESTION:**

You said you make a dozen offers to people each month. How many in your audience do you think hear each of those 12 offers each month?

>> Great question / observation. I noticed that I have the same core of people who know and like me who engage consistently. And then it varies. But when put like that, it doesn’t sound like people would see a lot of my offers…

Would it help to make more of those 1 to many offers ? I also want to keep it light and give people the opportunity to engage and comment. And I noticed that when the post includes an offer, there tends to be less engagement.

For the 1:1 offers… I went back on my year and half of business and did notice that I did make more of those. I was fresh out of coach training and I wasn’t really looking for clients – or didn’t believe my offers would transform into clients. I reached out to some people I knew and asked them to play the client to help me work on my consults (pre 2K for 2K). And 2 people signed, much to my surprise at the time.

I want to recreate that. Because when I invited people to conversation from that mindset, I was SO unattached and non salesy. Just passionate about coaching, eager to practice + get better AND share coaching with my loved ones.

Then I made an offer to an acquaintance and she said “no” which I was so embarrassed about. Since then, I’ve done so only a couple of times and to acquaintances too. Wasn’t embarrassed and felt good about it.

I have more trouble making offers 1:1 to my IG people for example. And I don’t want to slide in anyone’s DM as this feels creepy.

I would love guidance on this. TIA

So it’s not that you’re not special enough to create a profitable business, or that you’re not meant to have a business…it’s that you’re not yet meeting enough people, having coaching conversations with them directly, and making offers to help them (offers which they can immediately respond to.)

What do you think?

>> That helps hugely. It makes me want to get creative in the ways I’m meeting people and creating opportunities for conversations.

**ANSWER:**

Yes, coach!

And you’re creative and resourceful and can figure out how to create opportunities for meeting people.

When you think of your IG people, what are the ways you can connect with them to hear more about their needs (and therefore learn how you can serve them?)

You are great at connecting with people 1:1 that you already know – how can you do more of that?

How easy is it for you to have conversations with people and feel completed unattached to them investing with you?

## Day 23 / 30 – instagram fears pt. 3

**Date:** 2026-04-20  
**Topics:** fear

**QUESTION:**

First, thank you for the suggestion to seek support for the depression I’ve been feeling. Just so you don’t worry, I have a therapist I speak to weekly 🤍

I completely agree that the thoughts of others have no power unless I am agreeing with them on some level. I like to think I have been working through this thought for a while now – that I am not an imposter & that my skills are what matter, not my circumstances. I am very inspired by Stacey’s story about working from a motel room – it reminds me of Melanie Ann Layer as well, who started her coaching practice while living in her car. & the thought “I have tools to help people and people just want help,” is truly a very helpful thought for me. I am putting that on my whiteboard in my room, thank you.

Being a 33 year old woman who seemingly had so much success and has since “lost it all,” feels like a story that has been running in me for a while now. I feel like I’m on the edge of finally letting it go. A thought that I believe has been holding me there longer is “I don’t know how to get back to the success I had before.” I have been resonating with that fear of being “found out” because I haven’t seen a way out. I haven’t just been afraid that people will figure out that I’m not a success right now, but that I will never be a success again. So if I’m believing in myself and my future again, I don’t think that story will carry so much weight.

I also like your reframe around the pressure, make it more about being her today rather than thinking you need to be her all the time. I can be her today.

& I cried when you asked what it would look like to be the person who no longer has this obstacle – not even about just showing up on Instagram but about ALL the obstacles my mind keeps creating. I had a coach years ago who tried to get me to see my obstacles like the spoon scene in The Matrix, “your obstacles aren’t real, they’re just stories in your head, there is no spoon,” & it had a really profound impact on me. I only just remembered that now, thank you for shaking that loose.

It would look like feeling relief if I no longer had these obstacles, it would look like progress, it would look like me showing up in confidence and service. I would need to believe that I have something that can truly serve people, and I would do what I can to create more opportunities to serve.

**ANSWER:**

Hey Coach,

Great work around this and thanks for being so vulnerable. It’s good to hear the coaching landed and shook some past stuff loose for you to remember again – that is wonderful.

You shared how it would look to no longer be the woman who has the obstacles your mind keeps creating, and you came up with a brilliant set of steps in terms of belief work.

You shared, “
I would need to believe that I have something that can truly serve people, and I would do what I can to create more opportunities to serve.”

This is great. 
Do you believe you have something that can truly serve people? Why or why not?

And: “
I would do what I can to create more opportunities to serve.”

Wonderful – how do you want to go about creating more opportunities to serve? The beautiful thing to witness inside of yourself is that when you ask yourself these questions, your heart/soul already knows the answer. It just becomes about executing now.

I love that you said this version of you would show up in confidence and service. I could feel your energy behind that, and I deeply believe your offer will do that for others.

With every submission you send to AAC to get coaching, and each time you take action in your business, you work through those mindset obstacles. You embody the work you will ask of your clients in some way. You’re doing the
 very work
 it takes to build confidence piece by piece and to serve your audience.

Another piece of this I’d like to offer is that anytime I am going through something where my brain wants to self-sabotage, I trust it is never being “wasted”. Meaning, when I learn how to walk myself through my own obstacles, or learn by getting coaching/therapy on my obstacles, I am able to help others who eventually come my direction feeling the same way. I can relate to them on a different level… and while that doesn’t mean they have to know my whole story or my exact obstacles, I do believe our obstacles and challenges help us be more “in tune” with our clients who may one day come to us feeling similar. Sometimes that reframe also helps me…
 “My own challenges not only serve my growth in how I figure them out, but they eventually serve my clients because I become the perfect person to help them.”
 That thought can also be available for you too (vs. I’ll be “found out” or “I’ve lost it all.”) Ya know? And how you walk yourself through this may be serving you in a big way. Maybe you will meet a client who feels similar and you will be the perfect person to help them.

Great work here, coach. Feel free to come back with anything more you may need.

## Day 21 / 30 – Instagram fears

**Date:** 2026-04-14  
**Topics:** fear

**QUESTION:**

In response to your questions about showing up on Instagram:

I feel an interesting tension around HOW I think I’ll have to show up. Instagram has always been a place where I show up at my best, never in my pj’s in bed with messy hair all because I had an inspired thought I needed to get out. That’s how I show up a lot of times on Tiktok and In ever worry about the way people will respond to it.

But on Instagram it feels like I need to be polished….I feel a sense of embarrassment when I think about posting some of my videos from Tiktok onto Instagram. Insta has always been my highlight reel and I stop posting when life becomes slow or “boring.”

A few months ago I created an entirely new account on Instagram for my educational content and without even trying I had a bunch of random people from my life start following that account before I’d even posted anything and I immediately decided I wasn’t ready to start cross posting because of that.

I guess the overall vibe I feel is uncomfortable, I feel like I have to perform on Instagram, be a “professional,” & there’s also a feeling that people will “find something out” about me. I relate to what Stacey said during one of her 2k videos about choosing a new identity, meeting as many new people as possible so you can practice your new identity and accept that the people around you may not recognize who you desire to become. My friends and family know what I do to some extent, but they’ve never seen me “in action” before. I don’t know, there’s something about that that makes me uncomfortable. It reminds me of my kindergarten variety show where I sang on stage and afterwards everyone was giving me high praise – I HATED IT. I wanted to be left alone. & even to this day (though I am a musician and artist) I don’t want to sing in front of other people.

**ANSWER:**

Hey coach,

Thank you for taking the time to really reflect on this. You can see so clearly what’s happening for you and that awareness is exactly where the work is.

What I want to gently but directly point out first is this: you don’t have an Instagram problem, you have a thinking problem about Instagram.

You’ve already proven you can show up freely and without overthinking on TikTok, so nothing about you actually changes between platforms only your thoughts do.

The pressure you feel isn’t coming from the platform, it’s coming from the meaning you’re assigning to it. Right now, Instagram sounds like it means you need to be polished, professional, perform, and be judged, while TikTok feels like a place to express and just share. Same person, different story  and when you believe the Instagram story, of course you feel uncomfortable and hold back.

You also mentioned that feeling that people might “find something out” about you, and I want you to get really honest there  what exactly are you afraid they’ll find out?

I’m asking because this gets underneath the surface discomfort and into the real fear. Is it that you’re changing, that you’re not as polished as they expect, or that you’re becoming someone they haven’t seen before?

Because what I hear is that you’re not just posting content, you’re revealing a new identity in front of people who have only known the old one, and that’s vulnerable. But this is also where your work is.

You referenced Stacey talking about meeting new people to practice your identity, and Instagram is the exact same opportunity  it’s not a stage you perform on, it’s a room you walk into as the version of you you’re becoming. So if you fully believed you are a coach with value to offer and people are waiting to hear from you, how would you show up? I’m asking because your actions will always follow what you believe about yourself.

Right now, you’re still partially showing up as someone managing other people’s opinions, and you saw that clearly with your new account you were ready until other people entered the space, and then their potential opinions became louder than your intention.

That’s the moment to notice, because this is the exact pattern your clients will have too, and when you learn to walk yourself through it, you’ll be able to lead them through it.

So I want you to sit with this: what is the actual problem with feeling uncomfortable?

right now discomfort is acting like a stop sign when really it’s the entry point. You also shared that Instagram has been your highlight reel and you stop posting when life feels slow or boring, which tells me you’ve been using it to present a version of your life, not necessarily to serve, so of course this shift feels exposed you’re redefining what the platform is for. When you focus on you its different then when you focus on others.

So what do you want Instagram to be for you now? Not what it’s been or what others expect, but what you want it to be. From there, you get to align your behavior with that decision instead of other people’s imagined opinions. You can keep being who others expect, or you can decide who you’re becoming and start practicing her now.

Both will feel uncomfortable Coach, in different ways one keeps you where you are, and the other moves you forward. The question is, which discomfort are you willing to feel?

---

## Consult fears

**Date:** 2026-02-23  
**Topics:** fear

**QUESTION:**

Hi coach! I have been trying to build my coaching business for a couple of years already. My previous clients came from my in-person Reiki business. I recently reframed my offer- it’s clearer and the price is more sustainable (before it was definitely too low). With my new offer, I’m trying to reach a new audience. It was crickets for a while and now some consults are starting to come in.

I had one consult last week and one the week before – both of which said YES but haven’t paid yet (the first one wrote in after missing call 1 to say she needed a little longer to get the money). A call is scheduled for the second woman for Friday.

Then over the weekend, an interview dropped that I had pre-recorded with a summit host – and 5 new women booked consults – 3 Monday and 2 on Friday!

I’m working on expanding my capacity to hold the goodness and hold the belief that those 2 weren’t a fluke and that the yeses will keep coming and keep getting stronger (while staying positive neutral for the calls!). And creating the belief that these 7 consults are just the beginning vs putting pressure on them like they’re the only ones.

I just feel like I’m at this weird tipping point where I could either spring board into my dreams coming true or fall flat with disbelief and let it get in the way of me presenting well on the new consults.

What can I do or believe to help myself lean into high belief and capacity this week?

**ANSWER:**

Hi coach,

Yes! This is how you create consults, clients, and exactly what you said… belief that 7 consults are just the beginning. Notice who you were being when you created the value. Notice the action plan and execution that created the clients reaching out to you.  When you show up in the frequency they are looking for they reach out.

Let’s call out the thought: 
“like they’re the only ones.”

Wanting more is normal. pressure is optional caused by believing this thought. How is that not true?  Even the two that said yes and then no are proof 7 not the only ones.  What you expect is what your brain is seeing.  Direct it purposefully.

Pressure only shows up if you believe the thought. if you don’t challenge it, your brain will run with scarcity. but is it true these are the only consults you’ll ever have? or is that just your brain trying to protect you from possible disappointment?

You can show your brain how that thought isn’t true.

Five women booked from one interview. that’s not random. that’s value creating response. If you make it mean you’re creating value  and value creates clients then what does that say about your capacity? What does that mean about your ability to create consults? How did you create them?  Show yourself the very tangible process.

About the two yeses that haven’t paid yet… instead of making them unstable, what if you take responsibility in a useful way? not blame  but empowering ownership. In your evaluation of these two consults identify what was in your control and use that to empower you.

What the 2k process teaches is this is part of the process.

Part of learning how to say what they need to hear, in the right frequency, the right amount of time, clean and clear.

Sometimes yes moves fast. Sometimes it moves slower. Sometimes it becomes a no and none of that equals fluke. You get to decide what you make the no mean. What is useful.

And this is exactly one of the phases Stacey teaches in the 8 phases of business,  consults start coming, yeses happen, then some wobble. the next phase is more consistent yeses. but there isn’t a stage of business where yes never turns into no again. It just happens less because your skill increases and your meaning around it changes.

Go back and listen to the bonus training in the portal — The 5 vital steps. especially the section on this phase. it will help you normalize what’s happening instead of dramatizing it.

Failure is never a sign you’re a failure, it’s part of the learning process,  trial and error.

Each experience gives feedback.

How do I show up on these calls if I already achieved the result?

How do i think?

How do i feel?

How do i carry myself?

Because capacity isn’t about making sure all 7 sign.

It’s about being steady whether 3 sign or 6 sign.

You’re already attracting aligned women! That’s evidence, now the work is staying in that energy and not making a delayed payment or a no mean you lost the springboard.

What will make the difference isn’t whether all 7 convert, it’s your willingness to keep learning and not make any single consult mean something about your future.

This isn’t a fragile tipping point, it’s expansion. Keep going Great work.

---

## Looking for success stories to knock fear away

**Date:** 2026-01-27  
**Topics:** fear

**QUESTION:**

It’s been quite some time since I came to AAC…and if my memory serves me correctly, pretty much every time I come back it’s to freak out about things “not working”.

My business is not doing well right now, I currently have 1 client. This is my full-time job and the business has no money to pay me a wage – so things are getting very tight. In 2024, I had 5 consults and all 5 signed up. Those clients were all people that I already knew or had been part of one of my previous group containers.

The last month I’ve been pretty burnt out, so decided to take the month to get all my business energy leaks cleared up, but today I had a freak out, so it’s back to promotion to invite new clients forward.

I have posted a lot in FB – in groups, on my pages – telling them about a webinar I have next week as well as putting it out there that I have 1:1 coaching available. I’ve also been pitching myself to be on podcasts, and to support local charities, and provide freebies for IWD events in March. I am feeling so good with all of these touch points of letting people know what I have and how to take a step forward.

Fear is of course present and reminding me that what I am doing is not going to get me fast results, that warming up an audience takes time, that I don’t even have the webinar created for next week that I am promoting.

I know fear isn’t helping. It’s just trying to keep me safe. Do you know of any success stories that can inspire me? I’m looking for stories of people who had virtually no money in the bank and then blew themselves away with 5 figures in 30 days type thing.

**ANSWER:**

Hi Coach,

Welcome back. Take a breath you are in the right place. And first I want to name something important. You mentioned that every time you come back, it’s when things feel like they’re “not working.” That kind of thinking is incredibly triggering, especially when money is tight and this is your full-time work. Of course your nervous system is loud right now. You’re not doing anything wrong by feeling this way and you’re not alone here.

I also want to reflect something you already see: fear isn’t the enemy. It’s doing what it always does trying to keep you safe. The problem isn’t fear showing up; it’s what we make fear 
mean
. When fear gets interpreted as “this isn’t working,” everything starts to feel urgent and shaky. What if, instead, fear became information you could channel into grounded action rather than a signal to panic?

Let’s slow this down and look at where the real tension is. You shared , ” The last month I’ve been pretty burnt out, so decided to take the month to get all my business energy leaks cleared up, but today I had a freak out, so it’s back to promotion to invite new clients forward.”  This is your opportunity to offer yourself some compassion.

You’re noticing yourself making predictions about how long this 
should
 take and what 
should
 be working by now. That matters, because expectations quietly drive our emotional response to results. So here’s an important question not to judge, but to clarify:
What is your current expectation for how long it should take for this level of promotion to turn into clients?
And just as important: why do you believe that timeline is true?

This is where many coaches get stuck. When effort doesn’t match the imagined timeline, we unconsciously slow down, second-guess, or change strategies too quickly. Then we start believing the actions don’t work not because they don’t, but because we didn’t stay with them long enough. If that’s happening here, it’s worth asking:
What do you tend to do when things take longer than expected and what would you like to do instead?

What are the specific actions I’ve decided to take each day, and am I actually doing them without expecting immediate results?

This might look like deciding how many hours you’re working, what those hours are for (meeting more people, posting, inviting, following up, creating, evaluation, managing the fear?)

What would a more realistic amount of time look like for this to start gaining traction  and what will you need to believe while it’s unfolding?
Selling almost always takes longer than our fear wants it to. The belief you hold 
during
 that time determines whether you stay in motion or freeze.

How can you create safety for yourself so fear doesn’t taint your action plans?

You asked for success stories  the “five figures in 30 days” kind. Those stories exist, but they’re often outliers or the result of groundwork that isn’t visible. If your nervous system is looking for certainty,  question might be: What kind of success story do you want to be living ? How can you lean into the uncertainty?  Who do you need to become to hold the fear and keep going ?

To bring this back to something practical, here’s an exercise I’d encourage you to do:

Make three lists:

What you 
were doing
 when you first started taking action

What you’re 
doing now

What you’d need to 
do consistently
 moving forward to create the results you want

List both actions you’re taking and actions you’re avoiding. This isn’t about blame it’s about seeing clearly, without fear running the show.

Nothing here says your business isn’t working. What I see is someone in the middle of the uncomfortable part  where consistency is required before evidence shows up. That’s not a sign to panic; it’s a sign to stay anchored.

You don’t need a miracle. You got this. You are back at it and showing up. You need clarity, patience with the process, and a plan you trust enough to follow even when fear is talking.  This is how you make it happen.

And you’re already closer to that than you think. You are in the space where coem to support the part of you that feels fear.  Bring your answers back and we can coach further on this.

## #86 consult – responding to her fear

**Date:** 2026-01-19  
**Topics:** fear

**QUESTION:**

after sharing her pivotal moment, that she’s doing it from obligation.

I then asked what would be the impact of no longer doing things from obligation, what would that be like for you?

alot happier, more capacity for what I do want to spend my time doing b/c they aren’t taking up space.

I asked a few questions ,but mostly I was explaining.

I want to back away from that in my calls, but I feel that very weird to ask questions and then ask her if she’d like to move forward?

Maybe I am not understand the appropriate to explain/coach in these moments?

In this situation with her, I’d get clear on:

What are you thinking will be hard about this journey?

What changes are you thinking will be the most hard? (that wasn’t clear)

How do you think they will feel about you changing that?

After that I am lost… it gets muddy and I feel myself clamming up right now, thinking- “I don’t know what else to say.”

I feel myself in the pool…that IS hard

I can’t guarantee they won’t do xyz

How does she move forward with this when she’s thinking this way?

why would she move forward?

yes, that might happen and it will suck.

it’s like I am in agreement with that part of her brain that is scared!

**ANSWER:**

Hi Coach,

First, great job evaluating this consult. You clearly identified what worked and where things started to feel hard. That’s exactly what we want,  awareness so we can decide what to do differently next time. Let’s look at this one piece at a time and use what you noticed to make a plan for your next consult.

When she shared her pivotal moment ,  that she’s been doing things from obligation ,  you asked a strong question: 
What would be the impact of no longer doing things from obligation?
 Her answer gave you clarity. She could see she’d be happier and have more capacity for what she actually wants because obligation wouldn’t be taking up space.

You noticed that after asking a few questions, you mostly went into explaining. You also noticed it feels weird to ask questions and then ask if she wants to move forward. .

When you feel that weirdness, it’s usually worth asking yourself why? Often it comes from wanting to prove value, control how the client feels, or make sure the conversation goes a certain way. Understanding 
why
 you go there helps you decide what you want to feel next time (curiosity, connection, steadiness)  so you can set the tone before asking the next question.

The questions you wanted to get clear on are good ones:

What do you think will be hard about this journey?

What changes feel the hardest?

How do you think they’ll feel about you changing?

These questions matter because they surface the fear underneath the desire. Where things started to feel muddy for you wasn’t the questions ,  it was what happened after you asked them.

What do you think keeps you in the pool?

When You noticed yourself “in the pool” with her. Notice the automatic thoughts: they are not true, they are not useful. 

“This is hard.”

“I can’t guarantee they won’t do xyz.”

“Yes, that might happen and it will suck.”

When you’re in agreement with the scared part of her brain, it becomes very hard to help her see a path forward. Not because you can’t help her, but because you’re believing the same story. That’s when your brain offers thoughts like, 
I don’t know what else to say,
 
How does she move forward thinking this way?
 Of course you feel yourself clamming up there.

This isn’t a coaching skill problem. This is a belief problem in the moment.

Your brain goes on a rampage reinforcing lack of belief:

“I don’t know what else to say.”

“I can’t guarantee they won’t do xyz.”

“How does she move forward like this?”

That’s your cue to do your work first. This is why the pre-consult thought work matters.  Try using the pre-consult worksheets.  Use it every time, not to eliminate doubt, but to clean up your thinking so you can stay out of the pool. You go first. You go often. You go with purpose.

It is your job to believe in the client so you can show her the way, not by promising outcomes, but by holding belief steady while she feels uncertainty.

Doubt is part of the process. The work is noticing when you’re slipping into agreement and choosing not to. Allow the uncertainty. Allow the fear. And don’t let it run the session.

So the questions for 
you
 are:

What do you need to believe about the client to stay out of the pool?

What do you need to believe about yourself to keep asking questions instead of explaining?

If you truly saw her as whole and capable, what would change about how you show up in that moment?

## Overcoming past fear

**Date:** 2025-10-06  
**Topics:** fear

**QUESTION:**

I had a consultant today. She found me organically.

What went well

I built rapport and trust, creating a safe space to share struggles openly.

I positioned my coaching clearly as a blend of business, marketing, and mindset.

I connected the challenges directly to my strengths and past client results.

I painted a clear future vision that included growth beyond one on one.

I handled pricing with confidence and transparency, offering flexible options.

I used Stacey’s my job is to help you decide a clear yes or no and both are fine, when she said she wanted to wait.

I booked a follow up.

What did not go well

I left the conversation without guiding to a clear decision.

I focused too much on long-term vision instead of solving immediate pain points.

I could have gone deeper in addressing past disappointments with other programs.

I did not create enough urgency around acting now instead of waiting.

I did not use a strong closing structure to move towards a yes.

C She has paid for course before that didn’t work for her

T I’m different and prefect for her

F Annoyed – I have come so far – her I was in service energy – I didn’t help her decide

A Didn’t show her how I am different / real value

R She didn’t decide to buy

My question is how do I show her that I am different and allow her to trust herself?

She said she wanted 1 on 1 coaching, marketing and messaging, support on her confidence etc etc everything I am offering and when she said she wanted to wait I called her out on that. “But you sound like the others and they didn’t work”. I went to the belief triad and asked if she didn’t think she’d do the work. She said syes whent confident. I asked when she wasn’t she said that was when iot was an issue. I addressed this and tried to show her how I was different using her own words. She said she wouldn’t make a decision today so I suggested a follow up.

Thank you

**ANSWER:**

You wanted to show her how you were different.

Tell me – how would coaching with you be different than what she had in the past?

We have to first really understand what worked and didn’t work for her in the past in order to answer that. What didn’t work with the other coaches she has hired in the past? It could be the style wasn’t what she needed, maybe she wasn’t in a place to receive the coaching (too busy with work, other stress, etc.), maybe she didn’t feel like she could ask for what she needed, etc. If she didn’t do the work – how would this be different? How could you help her in doing the work this time or what would she need in order to do the work?

What didn’t work for her in the past? Get specific.

Then – how would this coaching with you be different? Both in terms of how you coach, the program and her and how she shows up.

## fear running the show

**Topics:** fear

**QUESTION:**

Thought: stop selling coaching for financial freedom.

When I first opened my biz I did so because I felt professionally under-realized. I knew I was valuable and meant for more; worked hard yet still under-earning. Added consulting to income seeking financial freedom.
