## Price increase drama

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I increased my 3 month package recently and have had more money objections on consults. I am now doubting my new price. I’m thinking I should have more pricing options available for clients to choose from. I start to doubt the value I offer and start believing the price is too high. I want to be coached on believing in the value I offer and not getting hung up on the price. I know my money beliefs get in the way of me being able to coach clients on their money objections.

I want to feel confident and fully believe in the value I offer. I want to convey my belief in my offer so strongly that the price becomes irrelevant.

**ANSWER:**

Why do you want to raise your rate?  Do you like your reasons?  How full is your coaching practice? How many spots do you have left?  If it’s almost full, it’s probably the right time to raise them.  If it’s not, give some more detail about your reasoning for the price increase.

How do you know if it’s the right time to raise your rates?  When you’re in a place where you’re willing to lose a potential client because of it.  This is NOT to say that it’s your new price that is driving them away.  You’re simply going to show up on the consult differently than you would if you were offering your previous price, because you feel differently about your new price than you do your old one.  You aren’t sure of it yet, so you show up that way.

Complete these two models.

C: Previous price for 3 months

T:

F:

A:

R: Get paid that price.

C: New price for 3 months.

T: The price is too high.

F:

A:

R: Don’t get paid that price.

With each model, get really specific in the A line as to how you show up on the consult.  See how you’re creating each result for yourself?

Why are you thinking you need to have different pricing options?  If you were to do that, who are you making that decision for?  Are you choosing to do that because you think it’s in the best interest of your clients?  Or are you doing it for reasons that have to do with you–perhaps because it makes you more comfortable with discussing your prices or is a way to avoid feeling whatever F you put down in that second model.

By creating different package prices, notice how you’d be making your client make another decision before they decide whether or not to go forward and hire you as a coach.  Now your client has to decide which package they would want before they decide whether they’re a yes.

Instead of doing you’re own work on your Ts and Fs about your new price, you’re making your client do more work.  Do you like your reasons?

## Converting with excitement

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

Excitement is a feeling I can easily generate. It’s a feeling I always believed would be what makes me money. But, staying in excitement doesn’t seem to be creating the result of making more money. T

F

A

R

## Building up belief

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I’m struggling around my belief that I can bring people into my live event. I’m going to make a high-ticket offer (that I’m working on right now), but now all the thoughts are kicking my butt–what if I can’t fill the room? I’ve only sold 20% of the tickets? What if no one signs up for my offer? Oh, Lawdy!!!!

My ideal outcome would be to bring 700 people to the event and sell 20% of the room into my high ticket offer.

**ANSWER:**

First of all, “Oh, Lawdy” is just AMAZING! I love it!

This is a perfect example of cognitive dissonance and how our primitive brains like to freak out whenever we try to achieve something new.  Our brains don’t like change.  They want to keep us safe.  To do this, they want to maintain the status quo.  Any change to the status quo is scary and dangerous and will likely be the death of us (so our brains believe).  And so our brains freak out when we start to think any new thoughts that conflict with our old thoughts.  Any time we try to grow, change, or accomplish something new, we start thinking conflicting thoughts simultaneously.  Right now, you’re conflicting Ts are: “I can create and high-ticket live event and people will buy tickets” and “People won’t buy tickets.”  Whenever you’re thinking two conflicting thoughts, you’re going to experience discomfort.  The truth of the matter is simply this . . . if you want to grow, you have to be willing to feel uncomfortable.  With that said, you can take action despite the negative emotion your feeling.  You can take action deliberately and on purpose.

What if you only sell 20% of the tickets to your first “high-ticket” event?  So what?  Answer that for yourself.  What are you making that mean?  More specifically, what are you making it mean about you?

C: 20% of tickets sold.

T:

F:

A:

R:

What if you chose to focus on what you have instead of what you don’t?  What results would that kind of thinking create?

## Creating a routine

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I am often reluctant to do too much self coaching before minis because often I get so emotional in unpredictable ways when I go deep. If I allocate a half an hour, the likelihood of me uncovering e.g. sadness and crying is pretty high. It seems safer for me to do e.g. a 5-minute focus on offering help and transformation right before the call.

I’d love to have a routine that allows me to show up full on. My aim in any coaching call is to help the client experience new insights and (ideally) transformation.

**ANSWER:**

You get to direct your own self coaching. “Deeper” coaching isn’t better coaching. The best coaching is the coaching that gets us the result that we want.

The intent behind self coaching before a call is to get you into a state of belief and feeling where you can best serve your client. It sounds like doing your own deep work right before the call isn’t working for you. You can do that deep work at another time, and come here to ask questions and get help with those thoughts, which will definitely help.

Before the call focus on thinking the thoughts you need to think in order to show up fully present for your client.

What’s the routine that I need to show up fully? Take a guess and then try it out. Your self coaching before the call might look different than someone else’s. Maybe it’s easier for you to get into the space you need by taking a walk before the call and focusing on all of the possibility you have for your client. Maybe it’s practicing your belief plan. Maybe it’s something else.

C mini session preparation

T

F

A

R I show up fully for my client.

## Hero’s journey

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I’ve been in the 2K program since March, and am struggling with square 1, the hero’s journey. It seems like I don’t have a story to share. My “breakthroughs” don’t seem big enough to share (like in my marriage).

I would love to have inspiring experiences to share on my personal Facebook page that attracts people to coach with me.

**ANSWER:**

First, there is no transformation that is too small.  What are some of the revelations you’ve had as a result of coaching/self-coaching?  What concepts were groundbreaking to you when you learned them that totally changed your own life?

Go re-watch the Organic Marketing module video on “Sharing Your Story.”  Don’t get caught up in this step.  If you’re trying to curate an Academy Award winning story, that’s not necessary.  In fact, if you force it, it can come off contrived.   There’s more than one way to share your story with your audience.  Part of that involves just sharing what’s going on for you day to day.  Over time, if you are yourself, speak from the heart and allow yourself to be vulnerable, your hero’s journey will come together all on its own.

Different ways to share your story include sharing the following:

The changes you’re making and why.

Your recent breakthroughs/transformations/achievements

Your failures and setbacks

Client success stories

Also, notice how your current T is keeping you stuck.  Complete the following model.  Figure out what F you feel when you think that T and the actions or inaction that result from that F.

C: Sharing your story.

T: I don’t have a story to share.

F:

A:

R: Share nothing. / Don’t share your story.

Then run an intentional model with the result you want to create in the R line.  Figure out what actions you have to take to create that result, how you would have to feel in order to take that action, and what you would have to think to feel that way.  What if instead of “I don’t have a story to share” you tried on this T: “If it’s a problem for one person, it’s a problem for a million people.”  Whatever the T, practice it, be willing to feel uncomfortable, and just put yourself out there.  You won’t know whether what you have to say resonates with someone if you never share it.

## Falling off the habit

**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I’ve been trying to create a consistent morning routine that includes getting up early, self-coaching, and working out for the last 2 years but I keep messing up. When I adhere to the consistent morning routine for even a few days in a row I see results, meaning more consults and clients. I then fall off the habit until I panic and prioritize it again because I need to deliver. I’ve modelled it many times and keep getting different thoughts to work on, but then I seem to come up with new loopholes. I know that this work is like peeling an onion with layers of awareness and self-discovery, but it’s like this is the largest onion in the world!

I would like to CONSISTENTLY have a productive morning routine that includes waking up early (6:30am), self-coaching, and working out. This brings out my best as an entrepreneur, and as a coach. Succeeding in this would be amazing for my hero’s journey as I would be blowing my own mind.

**ANSWER:**

It’s not your morning routine that creates you results.  When you stick to your routine, you think different thoughts and feel different feelings.  Bottom line, how you show up with consults and clients is still caused by your thoughts, not your morning ritual.  That being said, use the Model to gain some self-awareness as to (1) why you fall off the bandwagon in the first place and (2) how you treat yourself when you do.

C: Morning routine.

T:

F:

A: Don’t follow it.

R:

C: Didn’t complete morning routine.

T: I keep messing up.

F:

A:

R:

If you want to stick with the program you’ve created for yourself, you need to get a clear picture on why you aren’t sticking to it at certain times.  With the second model, work it through to see what As you take based on that T and whether or not you make matters worse by beating yourself up about diverging from the plan.

## Buffering

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I often find myself making excuses for what actions I should be taking, and when I model it out, I find I tend to put judgement on top of what I’m doing and the whole thing leads me to feel discouraged. For example, I will have a great idea to make a post on facebook, and then when the time comes, I decide not to post because I don’t know if it’s what I should be doing, if it is working toward the top three things (meet people, tell them I’m a life coach, make offers to help). How do I get out of this buffering cycle?

I would like to follow through on my actions with purpose. I recognize that I’m buffering and making excuses, but I want to be able to really have conviction in what I’m doing and commit to follow through on those actions.

**ANSWER:**

You make the excuses because of the judgment. That’s what our brains do when we are feeling judged – they try to defend back with excuses.

C post for social media

T I don’t know if this post is what I should be doing.

F  confused

A I think about if this follows the 2k process. I think instead of taking action.

R I don’t post.

Then, you are seeing that and judging it.

C didn’t post on social media

T I should have posted.

F judged/judging

A I don’t post.

R I stay in a buffering cycle.

What if instead of thinking that you should have posted, you asked yourself “What was going on in my mind that caused me not to post?” Then, “What would I have to think to post?” No judgement. No excuses. Just thoughts that cause feelings and feelings that cause actions.

C didn’t post on social media

T

F curious

A

R

## Free content not converting

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I want to always be working from a feeling of selflessness and the desire to help other people. This feeling is inconsistent for me. Sometimes I can get there. Other times, it’s hard for me to show up unless I can see the personal benefit for myself. For example, I’d rather spend my time consuming content (selfish) than creating it (selfless). I want to cultivate desire to serve when I feel unmotivated. I do believe my free content has helped a lot of people, but not enough that they actually book a consult or take action.

I want to create free content that helps people so much that they want to work with me more. I want them to join my email list, or book a consultation (I call them complimentary coaching sessions). I’m not sure why they don’t take action, since they tell me the free resources are very helpful. I get good feedback, but I’ve only had two consults and one paid client so far. It would be amazing if every time I created content people were signing up for my emails or a consult!

**ANSWER:**

You said “I’d rather spend my time consuming content than creating it.”  Why?

Do thought downloads on each of the following Cs: (C #1: Consume content. C #2: Create content today.) Write down EVERYTHING that comes up for each one.

C: Consume content.

T:

F:

A:

R:

Your brain is going to want to come up with thoughts that sounds nice about the first C.  For example: “I’m learning more so I can give more to my future clients.”  Stop it.  Dig deeper.  You need to see the real Ts and Fs that are driving you to indulge in inaction.  That’s where the second C comes in.  Complete a model for each T that you wrote down for your “Create content today” thought download.

C: Create content today.

T:

F:

A: Consume instead of create.

R: Don’t create content.

Did you know you don’t have to make money from selflessness?  Seriously, let that marinate.  You can make it from desire.  If you want to.  It CAN be about you and what YOU want.  You want to be an amazing coach with lots of clients and lots of money.  That’s a totally good enough WHY.  It doesn’t need to be about other people.  When you sit down to make content . . . what if you DID make it about you?  You’re resisting that, and it’s that resistance that is causing your pain.

Also, notice that creating content is not the same as making money.  (Go listen to Make Money as a Life Coach Podcast Episode #11 “Creating Content vs Creating Clients.)  What if you could make money without creating content at all?

For example, I made an offer and before the offer I thought “I am just practicing making offers and it doesn’t matter if I get consults or not” as opposed to “I am doing this to get consults.” But then I am bummed out if I don’t get a consult from the offer so I didn’t totally believe that thought which ends up in not valuable offers.

There’s a difference between being unattached to the outcome so you can stay in service of your client as a coach and being unattached to the outcome to the extent that you aren’t taking the process seriously, which isn’t in service of your client/potential client.

C: Making offers.

T: I am just practicing making offers.

F:

A:

R: Zero consults

C: Making offers.

T: It doesn’t matter if I get consults or not.

F:

A:

R: Zero consults.

## Compare and despair

I have booked one paid client and had four consults. While I defn see progress, I still struggle with compare and despair when I see other coaches who started after me killing it. While I get everyone is on their own journey with their own hang ups, I really get discouraged and worry that I am going to lean back on nursing and give up, like I have done in the past with a master’s degree. I would like help on keeping the momentum going even when things are hard and I am not on my desired timeline.

I want to be confident that clients are coming and be in confident that I am going to be a successful if I keep going and taking action.

C other coaches

T She’s killing it and I’m not.

F discouraged

A worry that I’ll give up and never get there

R I don’t get there because I’m thinking about how I’m not where I want to be instead of working on my business.

The only way that you can fail at something is to give up. Makes sense right? If we keep going and keep trying then we can’t fail because we haven’t stopped trying.

Feeling confident that clients are coming is a feeling that comes from a thought. You can help strengthen that thought over time by practicing it and by taking action (like meeting people, telling them you are a coach, and making offers to help) that strengthens that thought.

Confidence isn’t something that just happens. It comes from what you are thinking. You get to decide to believe that clients are indeed coming. What model do you want to practice believing?

T

F

A

R I sign clients

## Money Objections

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I’m not effectively coaching clients through their money objection (and their belief in their ability to get the results). I had 27 minis in July; two people registered (paid) for coaching. Three more people said they were definitely Yes, agreed to a time etc but then ghosted—these are the people that confuse me most because *I really thought there had been a breakthrough in my consults. Over the years I’ve coached more than 100 paid clients in the same niche, and they get amazing results, so I’m not in doubt about myself or my programme or my ability to help people. I am working on my belief about their ability to choose to pay and my belief in my own ability to master these consults.

I want to help people get that transformation on the call so that they can really believe in their own potential and ability. My ideal outcome is to double my conversion rate.

**ANSWER:**

Notice the disconnect between what you said and what you believe.  Compare “I’m not in doubt about myself or my programme or my ability to help people,” with “I am working on . . . my belief in my own ability to master these consults” and “I’m not effectively coaching clients through their money objection (and their belief in their ability to get the results).”  You’ve got some incongruent thoughts.

Selling is coaching. Coaching is selling.  You’re saying you aren’t in doubt about yourself, but you if that were true you wouldn’t be saying that you’re not effectively coaching clients through their money objections or that you need to work on your belief in your ability to master consults.

Also, notice that you are assigning responsibility for your feelings to their actions, not your thoughts about their actions.

C: Three people said yes during consult, didn’t show up for coaching at scheduled time.

T: I really thought there had been a breakthrough in my consults.

F: Confused

A:

R:

Look at the meaning your giving their decision to not show up.  They didn’t tell you they didn’t have a breakthrough during the consult. You’re mind created that thought for you all on its own. What if their decision to not show up for their first scheduled session had absolutely nothing to do with whether or not they had a breakthrough during their consult with you? What if they did think they had a breakthrough, but still chose to not show up?  That’s just as possible and available to you as the thought you’re currently thinking–that they didn’t show up because they didn’t have a breakthrough.

Going back to the model up above, fill in the A and R lines. Does that T create your desired result? Or does it just cause you to indulge in confusion and prevent you from doubling your conversion rate? What would you have to think, feel, and do to create the result you want?

I know my thought is creating this result…but I don’t know how to create a different thought that would serve and support me to get to $100k.

## 50% confidence

**Date:** 2019-10-28

I set a huge goal of 20 new clients by the end of the year. I plan to do 3 consultations a week to serve a new client each week. I’ve created a practice a belief plan. The shame and anxiety bleeds in from other areas of my life – my partnership and parenting. I continue to take action but I know it’s not with the same vibrancy I’m capable of having. Is this just a case of needing more practice taking massive action despite difficult emotions or am I lying to myself and missing something bigger?

I see myself serving 20 clients and feeling so pumped and energized. I’m helping change people’s lives – this is what I’m here to do. I have a great balance of work and family life – I’ve created it. I have financial freedom to reinvest in my business and myself as well as contribute to my family in ways that only I can. I have a full practice of 1:1 clients and it’s just the beginning.

**ANSWER:**

What Ts are causing your feelings?  Both your positive feelings and your negative ones.

C: Coaching business.

T:

F: Confident.

A:

R:

C: Coaching business.

T:

F: Ashamed.

A:

R:

C: Coaching business.

T:

F: Anxious.

A:

R:

Find the Ts that create the F of confidence.  Practice those every day.  It’s like building a muscle.  You have to practice and keep at it.  Also identify the Ts that make you feel ashamed and anxious.  When those Ts pop up, you’ll have an easier time spotting them and rerouting yourself back to your positive Ts that serve you.

Lastly, shame and anxiety don’t bleed in from other areas of your life.  Feelings are vibrations in our bodies.  They don’t pack bags, pick up and move.  You bring them with you by way of the thoughts you think.  That’s it.  If there’s “bleeding,” you’re creating that for yourself.  Why?

## Breaking the “addiction”

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I’m lapsing back into old patterns of working, spurts of intense/scattered action (random mix of tactics, throwing anything out there to see if it will stick, texting old clients, making offers to the wrong people) that demonstrate that I’ve fallen out of belief about what really creates money. As past over-worker I know that lots of A-line activity creates a dopamine roller coaster that gives me a false sense of achievement but is unsustainable and unstrategic.

I want to take repeated thoughtful, strategic, well-aligned actions from belief even when I don’t have immediate evidence that it’s working, stick with my actions long enough to see if they will create results in time. I want to break the “addiction” of taking tons of intense action for the dopamine, and instead adopt a more balanced approach that’s more sustainable, almost boring, relying on my belief to keep me on track.

**ANSWER:**

C: Coaching business.

T:

F:

A: Throwing anything out there to see if it will stick / texting old clients / making offers to the wrong people.

R:

What Ts and Fs are driving the actions you listed?  What results are those current actions getting you?

What result do you want?  What Ts and Fs would you need to think and feel in order to take the more deliberate actions you said you wanted to take? Also, what does that kind of action look like?  The more specific you are, the better you can assess whether or not the actions you’re taking are creating the results you want to attain.

One more thing . . . See what comes up for you in the model below.

C: You.

T: I’m a past over-worker.

F:

A:

R:

Notice that how you’re choosing to define yourself is a T, and it’s optional.  That story you’re creating about yourself only exists, because you’re choosing to continue telling it.  The past only exists / has meaning if you give it meaning in the present.  Is the story you’re continuing to tell about your past self serving you?

## Success = no freedom

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I have a belief that reaching my goals and making $ has to be hard. I believe it has to take up all my free time. And this prevents me from moving forward and taking action. Which prevents me from making $. Even though I know that, it’s a sneaky belief that still holds me back.

I want a new thought that allows me to make $ with my business not at the expense of my freedom.

**ANSWER:**

What if you were wrong?

What if not investing in your goals now is what’s costing you your freedom in the long run?

What if you saw that both paths have different flavors of discomfort?

You either go after your goals and have to put in the work

OR

You don’t, and you deal with the discomfort of not accomplishing the things you’d like to.

Would you rather suffer and not have what you want or suffer and have it?

One more thing:  Who would you have to be if you no longer believed the thought “Reaching my goals and making money is hard”?

## Corporate job

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

My C is – working in my corporate job. My thought is I am grateful to and for it. This makes me feel loyal which has me doing my best, performing at a high level & being a better leader and my result is I stay working for them. This isn’t working because I want out. “Where your focus goes is where your results grow”, I do feel held back by my identity as this employee.

I want to leave my corporate job and replace my income with coaching.

**ANSWER:**

Why?  Answer this question for yourself?  Why do you want to leave your corporate job?  Why do you want out?  Get clear on your reasoning.

You talked about your current thoughts about your corporate job, but didn’t discuss any of your current thoughts about your coaching business or making money as a coach.  What are your current thoughts about your coaching business?  If you’re looking at it as a side business and treating it as such, it’s going to keep producing side business results.  When you want this result badly enough–more than you want the financial security your corporate job provides–you will make achieving this result a priority and take the action to make it happen.

Refer back to Making Money as a Life Coach Podcast Episodes 27, 28, and 29 on the three stages of belief: impossibility, possibility, and probability.  Then, do a thought download on that C: “Making money as a coach.”  Be honest with yourself about where you are with your belief about your goals and the money you want to make.  Awareness is everything.  You’re either making money or not making money, because of what you’re thinking.

## Showing the possibility

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I would like to be coached on overcoming the objection that either “this doesn’t apply to me” or when sharing what I do, people immediately shut down. I’m a holistic nutritionist, and it seems like people often don’t want to engage or are very dismissive. I want to help show them the possibility without being preachy, and I’m having a hard time even getting the chance to because people will 1. actively walk away from me, 2. are deeply ingrained in their own story about food/weight and shut the convo down or 3. tell me their life story and then basically say “that’s nice” when I offer to help them.

My ideal outcome would be to really show them the possibility and get them excited that working with me will be completely different, despite all the potentially bad or disappointing experiences they’ve had in the past. I want to inspire people to actually take action, not just say “that’s so awesome, what you’re doing” and walk away.

**ANSWER:**

Let’s look at the model you have now that is creating your result.

C meeting new people

T

F

A

R people walking away, shutting down the convo

What are you thinking and how are you feeling and what are you saying or doing that’s causing people to walk away or shut down the conversation? Fill in the model.

When that happens, what do you make it mean? What’s your thought about it? and what result is that getting you?

C people walking away, people shutting down the conversation

T

F

A trying to figure out how to overcome their objection of thinking it doesn’t apply to them

R

What if you didn’t have to overcome the objection of someone not being interested? What if all you needed to do was share from the heart and the people who wanted help would come to you? How would you show up in your business when you met someone new?

## Struggling to make offers

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I realized going through the 2k process that one of my BIGGEST challenges in making offers, positioning myself as an expert, and telling people I’m a life coach is that I have a core belief about myself that “Nobody likes me”. That I’m uninteresting, I have nothing interesting to talk about, and I’m not a fun person to be around. I’ve been shy for so long and have struggled with confidence. Stacey’s program was the first to encourage me to get my ass out the door and meet people (which was terrifying for me). I’ve joined since end of February and have gone to SO many Networking events, and have greatly improved my comfort in social situations. However , when I make offers I feel like I’m literally dying and I’m so anxious. I don’t make consistent offers and I’m deathly afraid of rejection because I keep rejecting myself as an expert, entrepreneur, or influencer.

I want to create a strong belief in myself that I CAN help people, I am worthy, and that I know exactly how to help them. I struggle with maintaining this belief and constantly revert to thinking I’m a fraud, no one likes me, I’m boring, and I’m not good enough.

**ANSWER:**

“I’ve been shy for so long and have struggled with confidence.”  Notice that this is a thought.  It isn’t true.  It only exists in your mind, because you continue to 
choose
 to think it and believe it every day.  The same goes for “I’m deathly afraid of rejection.”  That isn’t true.  It’s just the story you’re choosing to tell yourself about yourself.  What if you never said that to yourself again?  Who would you be if you never thought that thought again?  How would you feel?  What actions would you take?

Other people don’t make you feel rejected.  You make yourself feel rejected based on your thoughts about what other people have said or done.  You’re attributing how you feel to whether they say yes or no to your offer.  Their yes doesn’t cause your positive feeling just as much as their no doesn’t cause your negative feeling.  Both are caused by the thoughts you think and what you make both answers mean.  Apply the Model to both scenarios and see how this is true.

C: Potential Client said “No.”

T:

F: Rejected

A:

R:

C: Potential Client said “Yes.”

T:

F:

A:

R:

Can you see that you are creating your experience yourself?  Consider this model too.  You wrote in your submission that your fear of rejection leads to you rejecting yourself.  That’s not your R.  This is.

C: Making offers.

T: I’m deathly afraid of rejection.

F: Terrified.

A: Avoid making offers./Don’t make offers consistently.

R: Reject potential clients.

In the “Bonuses” section of the 2k for 2k Portal, you can find all of the Diva Business School Podcast episodes.  Go listen to Episode 65: “My Story Uncut & A Big Change.”  Part of that episode talks about the power of overcoming fear.  What are you missing out on because of your fear?  What’s more terrifying: someone saying no to your offer or you missing out on what’s possible if you just got over yourself and your fear of rejection?  The worst case scenario isn’t someone saying no to your offer; it’s missing out on the best case scenario.

## Procrastinating

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

My next step in my business is to reach out to organizations in my area to offer free workshops for their parents. I have brainstormed schools, daycares, libraries, etc. I haven’t done that yet because of fear. I am waiting for school to resume. I am waiting to build my website. I am waiting to lose weight. Some of the free coaching I have been providing has not resulted in new business as I had assumed. Some clients raved and referenced me to friends but I have yet to hear from them. I have only coached parents that I already know. I want to market myself organically to build my confidence and sales abilities but my thoughts like to tell me that parents that are willing to pay for coaching are hard to find.. organically.

I want to go to networking events and have some measure of success. Meaning… quality connections, learning and maybe 1 client. I want to build my identity as a parent coach but I am procrastinating. I am ready to shift into a coach that takes payment.

**ANSWER:**

You’re waiting to take action until the right time or until you feel ready.  Stop it.  That line of thinking won’t serve you.  Neither will your thought “Parents that are willing to pay for coaching are hard to find.”  Using the Model, see what result that thought creates for you.

C: Coaching business.

T: Parents that are willing to pay for coaching are hard to find.

F: Discouraged.

A: Procrastinate. [Fill in all other action/inaction you take.]

R: Make it hard for parents willing to pay for coaching hard to find you.

You said that you haven’t reached out to organizations to make workshop offers because of fear.  Run a model on this.  What thoughts are causing that feeling?

C: Contacting organizations to make workshop offers.

T:

F: Afraid

A: Don’t contact the orgs.

R: Made zero offers.

How would you need to feel to take action and reach out to them?  What would you need to think/believe in order to feel that way?

One more thing: Pay attention to how you’re thinking is one step removed from thinking that generates feelings that will drive you to take action.

Your T: “I want to go to networking events.”

New T: I am going to networking events.

Your T: I want to build my identity as a parent coach but I am procrastinating.

New T: I am building my identity as a parent coach. OR I am a parent coach.

Your T: “I am ready to shift into a coach that takes payment.”

New T: I am a coach that takes payment.

Run models on each T and see how each thought creates a different feeling and, ultimately, a different result.  It’s a small shift, but it’s everything.  Thoughts that passively focus on want will just create results that leave you wanting.

I want to believe that people are willing and ready to hire me to solve their problem so that I can show up from a place of inspiration and compassion to use my talents and serve the world.

C: Making offers.

F:

A: Don’t make offers.

R: Continue being afraid. / Sell nothing.

C: Making offers.

F:

A: Don’t make offers.

## Waiting for inspiration

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I have watched the modules. I have taken notes. I know WHAT to do. I have made the time in my schedule to do it. But I have not developed the discipline to simply follow through. I self-coach daily, but I am always waiting for inspiration to hit for me to write an email or Facebook post. I know this doesn’t serve me but I can’t seem to break this pattern.

I want to put a task on my schedule and actually do it despite “not feeling like it.” I want to feel like what I am doing is working and I need to simply do more of it to get the desired result- more consults.

**ANSWER:**

What we do or don’t do comes from how we are thinking and feeling.

C schedule

T I haven’t developed the discipline to follow through.

F undisciplined

A I don’t follow what’s on my schedule.

R I don’t develop discipline.

What if you thought about discipline as a feeling you create by what you decide to think in the moment when you go to do something and you don’t feel like doing it? Try on thoughts that help you feel disciplined and test out what happens when you think them. Here is an example of one:

C schedule

T I never feel like doing this and I’m going to do it anyway.

F disciplined

A I do what’s on my calendar

R I develop discipline.

It’s the same with inspiration. It’s a feeling we get from what we think. You don’t have to wait for it. You can start working without feeling inspired or you can purposefully find a thought that creates the feeling of inspired.

## Shifting beliefs

**Date:** 2019-10-28  
**Topics:** archive-2019-2020

**QUESTION:**

I would like to be coached on shifting belief from a neutral to a more positive thought. Right now I’m stuck at “It’s possible to make 2k this month,” which is making me take action to create content, meet people, tell them I’m a life coach, but having a hard time shifting to “I can make 2k this month” which would feel more certain. Part of me thinks finishing my certification will make me more certain (finishing in September). I would like to feel certain that it’s possible and certain for ME to make 2k. I know others can do it. I would love to shift my belief to be certain which would drive my actions toward creating content and telling others with even more passion and conviction than I have now.

**ANSWER:**

Let’s look at your current model.

C Goal: $2k this month

T I need to feel certain that I can make $2k this month or else I’m not going to make $2k.

F uncertain

A

R I don’t make $2k this month.

Every time you are thinking “I need to feel certain that I’m going to make $2k this month or else I’m not going to make $2k this month” you end up feeling uncertain. That thought isn’t working for you.

What if “It’s possible to create $2k this month” was the only thought you needed right now? What if that thought actually did produce $2k? How would you show up in your business then?
